**Instance of:** `CG-AABPP-GOV-018`
**Instance version:** `0.2.0`
**Updated:** 2026-07-14T10:29:19+07:00
**Policy:** Track unresolved, accepted, deferred, or externally constrained issues. Exact runtime failures belong in `ERROR_LEDGER.md`.

## 1. Status and severity

Statuses: `OPEN`, `TRIAGED`, `PLANNED`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `MONITORING`, `ACCEPTED_RISK`, `RESOLVED`, `VERIFIED`, `SUPERSEDED`.

## 2. Standing accepted risks

| Decision | Accepted condition | Required permanent handling |
|---|---|---|
| RPD-022 | Supreme Admin can mutate/delete audit, ledger, payment, final records | No tamper-proof/immutability claim; disclose in security/finance/retention/support material |
| RPD-034/036 | Direct GA without external pilot | Full internal UAT/penetration/performance/DR/finance/all-module + zero-critical-defect gates |
| RPD-031/037 | Contract-silent RPO/RTO is best effort | Never imply a recovery guarantee; record actual rehearsal evidence |
| RPD-038 | Custom non-AI integrations, no generic abstraction | Shared codebase, explicit ownership, credentials, contracts, tests, monitoring, runbook |

A full audit of the remaining ~74 policies for the same latent-owner-scope-branch pattern is a repository-wide task beyond any single capability checkpoint's own file scope — deliberately not attempted ad hoc by `ATW-023`, matching this entry's own original "do not patch ad hoc" instruction. **Audit completed at `CG-S10-ATW-032`** (post-Prompt-248), replacing this entry's own "~74, not exhaustively audited" estimate with an exact, reproducible figure -- and correcting its stated premise. This entry said "most have no owner-scope branch at all and so already fail closed for a `customer_user` actor (no `org_unit_id`)". That reasoning holds only where a policy ALSO requires an org-unit or record-scope predicate. It does not: **97** `app` SELECT policies key on `app.has_active_tenant_membership` with NO further scope predicate of any kind -- tenant membership is the entire test -- and only **4** carry the explicit `not app.actor_holds_customer_user_layer(tenant_id)` narrowing that `20260730311000` established. Proven empirically in a disposable database rather than inferred: `app.invite_user` writes an `app.tenant_user_identities` row, `app.has_active_tenant_membership` reads exactly that table, and a freshly granted `customer_user` principal returns `has_active_tenant_membership = true` / `actor_holds_customer_user_layer = true`. So those 97 policies do NOT fail closed -- they admit a portal principal to `finance_journals`, `credit_profiles`, `customer_contracts`, `role_assignments`, `users`, telemetry and the rest. The repair was deliberately NOT attempted here and the reason is the same one this entry already gives: the pure `AND`-narrowing is mechanically obvious, but deciding which of the 97 the portal legitimately needs IS the Phase 8 contract, and inventing it inside an audit checkpoint is exactly what `AGENTS.md` reserves for a dedicated prompt. What Step 13 inherits is now an exact, machine-checkable worklist instead of an estimate. **Default-deny landed at `CG-S10-ATW-032`** (`supabase/migrations/20260730560000_harden_customer_user_layer_default_deny.sql`): all **98** SELECT policies whose entire test was `app.has_active_tenant_membership` now carry `not app.actor_holds_customer_user_layer(...)` beside that membership call. This deliberately does NOT decide what the portal may read -- that is Phase 8 -- it makes the DEFAULT deny so Phase 8 opens exactly what it needs instead of inheriting 98 open policies. The narrowing is written beside each membership call rather than appended to the qual, because six of these tables (`approval_request_steps`, `finance_invoice_lines`, `finance_vendor_bill_lines`, `finance_period_close_checklist_items`, `role_versions`, `tenants`) carry no `tenant_id` column and reach tenancy through a parent inside an `EXISTS`, where an appended predicate either fails to compile or references an out-of-scope alias. Verified live in a disposable database: staff still read every row; a `customer_user` now reads ZERO on `accounts`/`org_units`/`users`/`tenants`/`role_assignments`/`finance_journals`. SELECT-only is sufficient because `authenticated` holds SELECT on 233 tables and zero INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE. Full `db:test` green, so no legitimate access was removed. A standing gate in `scripts/db-tests/rbac-enforcement.sql` fails if any SELECT policy admits a customer_user-layer principal on tenant membership alone | Substantially reduced. A production portal principal can no longer read the 98 tenant-only tables through a raw client. What remains is Phase 8's own job: deciding and implementing which records the portal SHOULD see, on the four `customer_portal_scoped` tables and any owner-scoped surface it adds. |

| `ISS-2026-024` | `scripts/db-tests/advanced-tms-wms-picking.sql`'s own "idempotency-key reused across two different operations" regression proof (`CG-S10-ATW-017`, already `VERIFIED`, committed weeks before this discovery) is itself intermittently non-deterministic -- discovered incidentally while independently re-verifying the `ISS-2026-023` fix at `CG-S10-ATW-027` (Prompt 246). Across 11 total runs (7 of the `ISS-2026-023`-fixed file, 4 of the original, byte-for-byte-unmodified file straight from `HEAD`), 2 runs failed: both racing processes returned the byte-for-byte identical row (same id/timestamp) instead of one succeeding and the other cleanly rejected `idempotency_key_conflict` -- i.e. the second, genuinely-different request (a different `outbound_order_line_id`) was treated as a valid idempotent replay of the first rather than a content mismatch. Confirmed unrelated to the `ISS-2026-023` fix itself: the fix's only change (`set_config`/`current_setting` for file-path plumbing) executes strictly *after* both race processes have already fully completed and exited, and cannot influence their own timing or outcome; the original, unmodified file reproduced the identical failure shape when run enough times | Medium (was mis-severity: the underlying product defect was High) | `RESOLVED` (`CG-S10-ATW-030`, post-Prompt-248 audit) | Runtime agent (closed) | None yet -- not investigated further (out of `ATW-027`'s own bounded GPS/tracking security-hardening scope; `ATW-017` is an already-`VERIFIED`, unrelated capability). Plausible root cause, not yet confirmed: `app.reserve_inventory`'s idempotent-replay short-circuit may, under a sufficiently-overlapping real two-process race, resolve the second request against the first's own in-flight (not-yet-fully-visible) row before its own content-mismatch check can observe the difference -- a narrower, harder-to-hit timing variant of the cross-target idempotency-misattribution bug class fixed repeatedly elsewhere in this build (`ATW-020`/`021`/`022`), rather than a missing check outright. **That diagnosis was wrong, and is corrected here (`CG-S10-ATW-030`).** This was never a flaky test and never a race-only condition: `app.generate_wms_pick_task` carried **no content-mismatch check of any kind** -- its replay short-circuit matched `(tenant_id, idempotency_key)` alone and returned the found row unconditionally. Live-reproduced deterministically, with zero concurrency, on a freshly migrated database: calling it for outbound order line Y under a key already used by line X returned **line X's own pick task** -- a different item, a different order, potentially a different customer -- with no error. The test passed in 9 of 11 runs only because a sufficiently tight overlap collides on the unique index, which *does* raise; whenever the second process merely started after the first committed (the ordinary, non-racing case) the unguarded short-circuit silently returned the wrong row and the assertion correctly failed. The 2-in-11 failure rate measured the scheduler, not the product being mostly correct. **Fixed** at `CG-S10-ATW-030` (`supabase/migrations/20260730380000_harden_advanced_tms_wms_idempotency_target_mismatch.sql`), together with the same defect in 19 sibling WMS functions -- see `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-030.md` §3. The pre-existing two-process keyrace proof now passes deterministically as a direct consequence of the repair, with its own assertion never weakened or modified, and a new race-free deterministic regression block was added alongside it. `pnpm run db:test` run twice from scratch, `ALL PASSED` both runs | No -- closed. Retained as a standing lesson: an intermittent test failure that reproduces on a *sequential* path is a product defect, not a flake, and must be re-derived from the code rather than hypothesised from timing |
| `ISS-2026-025` | `app.vehicle_source_health.last_location`/`last_seen_event_at` are updated unconditionally on every ingested telemetry candidate, regardless of whether that candidate wins arbitration or is rejected (by original design, `20260729390000`'s own table comment: "even a rejected/disabled-source report is real evidence the source is alive"). `CG-S10-ATW-027` (Prompt 246)'s own adversarial device+driver-mobile probe live-observed that even a report rejected as `impossible_movement` still updates this baseline, opening a "salami-slicing" path: an attacker with a valid but compromised/impersonated source credential could submit a sequence of small, individually-plausible position deltas -- each losing arbitration outright, never becoming the vehicle's official position -- to gradually walk the per-source impossible-movement baseline arbitrarily far from the true position, without ever needing a future-dated timestamp. The sibling `CG-S10-ATW-027` fix agent independently analyzed and disclosed the adjacent, narrower "poisoned `last_seen_event_at`" concern (`supabase/migrations/20260730350000_harden_advanced_tms_third_party_hybrid_tracking.sql`'s own header) and correctly concluded it does not reopen Finding 2's "permanent lockout" defect (external freshness reads only real server-clock `last_seen_received_at`) -- but that analysis addresses only the future-timestamp angle (now bounded to 24h by that same fix) and does not cover this position-based salami-slicing vector, which requires no future timestamp at all. Neither of the two `ATW-027` fix agents closed this specific vector -- each reasonably assumed the other's scope covered it (device+mobile explicitly deferred it since fixing it meant touching `app.arbitrate_and_project_vehicle_position`, which was named off-limits to that agent; the sibling agent's own bounded 5-finding scope, defined before this cross-agent gap was visible, never included it) -- discovered as a coverage gap by this checkpoint's own orchestrating session while reconciling both fix agents' final reports, not fixed ad hoc under time pressure to a function already twice-widened this same checkpoint | Medium | `RESOLVED` (`CG-S10-ATW-031`, post-Prompt-248 audit) -- residual `ISS-2026-031` | Runtime agent (closed) | **Fixed** at `CG-S10-ATW-031` (`supabase/migrations/20260730430000_harden_source_health_plausibility_baseline.sql`). The mechanism the probe observed is closed: an INTEGRITY-rejected report (`impossible_movement`, `event_time_implausible_future`, `accuracy_below_threshold`) no longer advances `last_location`/`last_seen_event_at`, so the plausibility rule can no longer be defeated by the very reports it refuses. An ARBITRATION rejection (`switch_suppressed`, `stale_event_time`) deliberately still advances the baseline -- those reports are plausible and simply did not win, and freezing a legitimate secondary source's baseline while it keeps moving would make its first accepted report after becoming authoritative look like a teleport and be falsely rejected; the distinction between the two rejection classes IS the repair. `last_seen_received_at` stays unconditional (pure liveness evidence on the server clock, and what external freshness reads consume -- `ATW-027` Finding 2's reasoning preserved intact). Regression proof added to `advanced-tms-canonical-telemetry-arbitration.sql`. **Residual `ISS-2026-031`**: the check remains per-source, so a source can still drift far using only plausible steps and teleport the canonical position when it wins a switch -- a working cross-source guard was built and tested this checkpoint then deliberately withdrawn, see that issue for why | Partly -- the observed mechanism is closed; the per-source-vs-canonical gap is tracked as `ISS-2026-031` |

| `ISS-2026-029` | The unguarded idempotent-replay defect class `CG-S10-ATW-030` proved real and fixed across 20 Phase 5 WMS functions (`ISS-2026-024`, `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-030.md` §3) **is still open in roughly 35 further mutation functions outside that repaired set**. Same shape: `select ... where tenant_id = ... and idempotency_key = p_idempotency_key; if found then return ...; end if;` with no check that the found row belongs to the target the caller named. Confirmed by direct inspection this checkpoint in Finance (`create_finance_journal_draft`, `capture_finance_receipt`, `allocate_finance_receipt`, `prepare_finance_journal_reversal`, `prepare_finance_journal_adjustment`, `prepare_finance_settlement`, `apply_finance_ap_settlement`, `reverse_finance_ap_settlement`, `stage_finance_exchange_rate_import`), Operations (`create_shipment_order_from_job`, `transition_shipment_order`, `dispatch_shipment_order`, `ingest_milestone_event`, `start_epod_capture`, `add_actual_cost_component`), Platform Core (`provision_tenant`, `start_workflow_instance`, `request_approval`, `allocate_or_reserve_number`, `set_custom_field_values`, `initiate_file_upload`, `create_import_export_job`, `enqueue_job`), and Phase 5 TMS/inventory (`add_shipment_leg`, `reserve_vehicle_capacity`, `prepare_route_planning_scenario`, `post_inventory_movement`, `reserve_inventory`). The sharpest instance found: `apply_finance_ar_allocation` and `reverse_finance_ar_allocation` write the SAME table under the SAME `(tenant_id, open_item_id, idempotency_key)` unique key, so reusing a key to REVERSE an allocation it had previously applied silently did nothing and returned the open item unchanged -- the reversal never posted, on a Finance record. | `ISS-2026-031` | `app.arbitrate_and_project_vehicle_position`'s plausibility check is PER SOURCE -- it compares an incoming report against that same source's own last position, never against the canonical position. It was withdrawn because it changes WHICH SOURCE WINS arbitration, and the already-`VERIFIED` `advanced-tms-canonical-telemetry-arbitration.sql` suite demonstrates why that is not an audit checkpoint's call: its fixtures legitimately compress real journeys into minutes (~13 km in 60 s across several tests), so any physically-honest threshold either rejects those or is too loose to mean anything. Choosing that threshold and reworking the fixtures to be physically coherent is a real design decision about arbitration semantics, which `AGENTS.md` reserves for a dedicated prompt | Yes if unaddressed -- it is the remaining, and more consequential, half of the salami-slicing path; the exploit needs a compromised source credential and sustained interaction, but needs no single implausible step |
| `ISS-2026-032` | `CG-S10-ATW-031` closed `ISS-2026-017` by wiring `app.assert_actor_is_session_identity` into `app.evaluate_permission`, covering the 416 functions that call it. Roughly 315 further functions accept `p_actor_auth_user_id` without calling `app.evaluate_permission` -- helpers, read-only projections, and functions that delegate to another function which does check. They are NOT newly exposed by that migration (nothing changed for them); they are simply outside its coverage, and any of them that is both granted to `authenticated` AND performs a side effect without delegating to a checked function would still accept a forged actor | Medium | `RESOLVED` (`CG-S10-ATW-032`, post-Prompt-248 audit) | Runtime agent (closed) | **Fixed** at `CG-S10-ATW-032` (`supabase/migrations/20260730510000_harden_actor_identity_unchecked_authority_surface.sql`). The classification pass this entry asked for was run against the LIVE post-migration catalogue rather than by reading files, and the ~315 estimate turned out to be far too high: **761** functions in `app` take `p_actor_auth_user_id`; **566** reach `app.evaluate_permission` or `app.assert_actor_is_session_identity` through the transitive call graph (closure computed over `pg_get_functiondef` bodies), so `ATW-031` already covers them. Of the remaining 195, **119** hold no `authenticated` EXECUTE grant at all (bucket (b), internal helpers) and **43** are `STABLE`/`IMMUTABLE` pure reads where a forged actor changes nothing a caller could not already read. That leaves **33** that are `VOLATILE`, granted to `authenticated`, and reach no actor check -- exactly bucket (a). Each now carries `perform app.assert_actor_is_session_identity(p_actor_auth_user_id)` as its first statement. Every one of the 33 already validated authority; the gap was that each asked "is the CLAIMED actor allowed?" and none asked "is the caller that actor?" -- `app.approve_rate_version` gated on `app.is_support_grant_authority(p_actor_auth_user_id, v_rate.tenant_id)`, so any authenticated session that knew a tenant_admin UUID could approve a vendor rate version as them, and `app.mark_notification_read` compared `recipient_auth_user_id <> p_actor_auth_user_id` with the same hole. The five that are also called internally were checked individually rather than assumed: every nested call site threads `p_actor_auth_user_id` straight through and none substitutes a system or record-owner identity, and `auth.uid()` reads a request GUC unaffected by `SECURITY DEFINER` nesting, so the assertion cannot fire on a legitimate nested call. A standing gate in `scripts/db-tests/rbac-enforcement.sql` recomputes the same closure and fails if any side-effecting, client-callable function taking `p_actor_auth_user_id` reaches neither check | No -- closed. |
| `ISS-2026-033` | **`SECURITY DEFINER` functions granted to `authenticated` that perform no authority check of any kind.** A structural sweep of the live schema at `CG-S10-ATW-031` found **91** such functions -- `prosecdef = true`, an `EXECUTE` grant to `authenticated`, and no `evaluate_permission` / `can_access_record` / `is_supreme_admin` / `has_active_membership` call anywhere in the body -- of which **30 are `VOLATILE`** (they write). Because a `SECURITY DEFINER` function runs as its owner and bypasses RLS, any logged-in user of ANY tenant can call one with another tenant's identifiers. Four were spot-verified directly against the live schema and are structurally confirmed: `app.recalculate_quotation_totals` (rewrites any tenant's quotation money columns), `app.generate_route_planning_candidates` (destroys and rewrites another tenant's route plans), `app.evaluate_dispatch_readiness` (reads any tenant's shipment state), `app.next_quotation_number` (burns another tenant's number sequence). Full volatile list: `app.add_wms_inbound_order_lines`, `app.add_wms_outbound_order_lines`, `app.approve_rate_version`, `app.bulk_dispatch_shipment_orders`, `app.calculate_finance_job_profitability`, `app.create_rate_version`, `app.decide_credit_profile_approval_step`, `app.decide_quotation_approval_step`, `app.disqualify_lead`, `app.export_audit_logs`, `app.export_customer_inventory_snapshot`, `app.generate_route_planning_candidates`, `app.ingest_driver_mobile_report`, `app.ingest_third_party_provider_webhook_event`, `app.list_api_keys_for_tenant`, `app.list_webhook_endpoints_for_tenant`, `app.lookup_public_shipment_tracking`, `app.next_quotation_number`, `app.preview_import_job`, `app.qualify_lead`, `app.query_audit_logs`, `app.recalculate_quotation_totals`, `app.record_customer_inventory_access_denial`, `app.record_gps_device_installation`, `app.reject_rate_version`, `app.reverse_inventory_movement`, `app.revoke_quotation_acceptance_token`, `app.run_next_route_planning_job`, `app.send_quotation_for_acceptance`, `app.withdraw_rate_version` | **High** (pending per-function triage; several are expected to be legitimate) | `RESOLVED` (`CG-S10-ATW-032`, post-Prompt-248 audit) | Runtime agent (closed) | **Fixed** at `CG-S10-ATW-032` (`supabase/migrations/20260730460000_harden_security_definer_authority_surface.sql`), after triage reduced 92 candidates to 13 real problems. 79 were false positives of the sweep's own pattern: 47 delegate to a callee that DOES check (proven by transitive closure over the call graph, e.g. `decide_quotation_approval_step` -> `decide_approval_step`, which enforces `check_approval_request_authority`, `is_eligible_approval_approver` AND a self-approval denial); several authenticate by a mechanism the sweep did not know (`check_api_webhook_admin_authority`, `check_import_export_admin_authority`); four are deliberately anon-facing per the ratified design (HMAC-signed webhook ingestion, the public sanitized tracking projection, pre-login branding/domain/locale); and several ARE the authority primitives and cannot check themselves. Of the 13: five internal helpers that take no actor parameter had their `authenticated` grant revoked (the three writes were the serious ones -- `recalculate_quotation_totals` let any logged-in user rewrite any tenant's quotation money columns, `generate_route_planning_candidates` deleted and rewrote another tenant's route plans, `next_quotation_number` burned another tenant's sequence); seven client-callable ones gained `app.assert_session_identity_in_tenant`, which reads `auth.uid()` directly so no signature or caller changes. Three revokes were withdrawn during the work and each is recorded: `customer_warehouse_eligibility_active`/`resolve_customer_owner_account_scope` are asserted customer-portal-reachable by `ATW-023`'s own db-test (they are customer-SCOPE primitives, not helpers that forgot a check), and `evaluate_dispatch_readiness` is embedded in two views the dispatch board reads -- a view executes with the querying role's privileges, so revoking it broke every authenticated board query. `scripts/db-tests/rbac-enforcement.sql` gained a standing gate that re-runs the whole sweep and fails if any NEW function joins the unauthorized set, so the class stays closed as Phase 6 adds surface | No -- closed, with a standing regression gate. |
| `ISS-2026-034` | A 12-way adversarial audit fan-out at `CG-S10-ATW-031` produced **60 candidate findings (10 critical, 27 high, 21 medium, 2 low)** across Finance, Operations, Platform Core, Commercial, Advanced TMS and WMS. The workflow's own adversarial VERIFICATION phase never ran -- it stalled after the finder phase -- so every one of them is an unverified claim, not a confirmed defect. They are preserved verbatim in `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-031-DEEP-AUDIT-FINDINGS.md` rather than discarded, because the finder phase demonstrably surfaces real defects (`ISS-2026-033`'s four spot-verified authorization holes came from it) | Medium (as a register; individual findings range critical to low) | `RESOLVED` (verification complete, `CG-S10-ATW-032`) | Runtime agent (closed) | **The verification phase this entry called for was run at `CG-S10-ATW-032`** and its outcome is appended to `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-031-DEEP-AUDIT-FINDINGS.md` plus recorded in `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-032.md`. Each claim was re-derived from the LIVE post-migration catalogue (`pg_get_functiondef`, `pg_policy`, `pg_constraint`) and judged against the ratified design (`docs/blueprint/`, `00-control/02_CONFIRMED_DECISION_REGISTER.md`, `docs/architecture/06_RLS_RBAC_WORKSTREAM.md`) before being treated as a defect. Measured disposition: **20 CONFIRMED and repaired**, **7 ALREADY-FIXED** by a later migration, **5 BY-DESIGN** with the ratified text quoted. The register's own warning was correct -- the false-positive rate is high, and the two dominant causes are (a) the claim describes code a later migration already changed and (b) the claim assumes a design constraint the design does not impose (notably that a tenant-only RLS policy is a defect, when `06_RLS_RBAC_WORKSTREAM.md` §4 makes it correct everywhere outside the four `customer_portal_scoped` tables). Repairs landed as `20260730500000` (rounding order, leg-cargo and exchange-rate locks), `20260730510000` (`ISS-2026-032`), `20260730520000` (74 silent-no-op `stale_version` paths and one swallowed idempotency guard), `20260730530000` (operations/inventory/tracking record scope), `20260730540000` (finance lifecycle exits and reconciliation basis) and `20260730550000` (numbering continuity, custom-field replay, rate limits), plus contract fixes to the Finance journal, import/export job-type and audit-log-limit schemas. Two findings were against this audit's OWN predecessors and are the most consequential: an idempotency guard raising `unique_violation` inside a block whose handler traps `unique_violation` is inert (all 55 `ATW-030`/`ATW-031` guards swept structurally; one genuinely dead), and a version-predicated UPDATE that matches nothing must raise rather than fall through with a NULL composite and a fabricated `success` audit row (74 functions) | No -- closed. Individual survivors were repaired directly rather than promoted to separate IDs, since each is fixed and covered by a standing gate or a db-test; the by-design and already-fixed dispositions are recorded with their evidence so the same claims are not re-raised. |
| `ISS-2026-035` | `app.publish_milestone_template_version` resolved its supersede target by id ALONE and then archived it, while its authority check is evaluated against the tenant of the version being PUBLISHED. A tenant admin acting legitimately inside their own tenant could pass another tenant's published milestone template version id and archive it -- a cross-tenant WRITE by an ordinary tenant-scoped actor, exactly what `CPD-004`/`INV-002` exist to prevent, and NOT covered by the `RPD-022` Supreme Admin exception since it is reachable by a normal tenant admin. Surfaced by the `ATW-031` audit fan-out and confirmed by direct inspection of the live function definition | High | `RESOLVED` (`CG-S10-ATW-032`) | Runtime agent (closed) | **Fixed** in `supabase/migrations/20260730470000_harden_milestone_template_supersede_tenant_scope.sql`: the supersede target must belong to the same tenant as the version being published. It deliberately raises the SAME `milestone_template_not_found` the id-miss path already raises, so a cross-tenant id is indistinguishable from a non-existent one and the error cannot be used to probe another tenant's records | No -- closed |

| `ISS-2026-037` | `CG-S11-PRC-005` (Prompt 254, Vendor Banking and Tax Security): every *denied* attempt at a privileged action on vendor bank/tax data (a reveal blocked by missing `PRC:View personal data`, a decide/hold/reactivate/deactivate blocked by `insufficient_authority`/`self_approval_not_allowed`/`self_reactivation_not_allowed`/stale `reauth_confirmed_at`) raises a Postgres exception, which rolls back the entire transaction including any audit-log insert attempted before it -- so `app.audit_logs` records every *successful* privileged access to this capability's own most sensitive data but zero denied/blocked attempts. Prompt 254 §18 explicitly requires "every reveal/export denial" to be audited; this repository has no `dblink`/`pg_background` anywhere (confirmed by repository-wide grep) to make a durable audit write survive a rolled-back caller transaction, and the identical raise-before-audit shape is the pervasive, pre-existing convention this capability's own maker-checker pattern was deliberately copied from (`app.approve_warehouse_billing_event`, `ATW-020`, and by extension every other self-approval/authority guard in this repository) -- so this is not a defect unique to PRC-254, but PRC-254 is the first capability whose own source prompt explicitly names denial-auditing as a binding requirement, making the gap load-bearing rather than incidental here. One read-path instance was fixed in place during this checkpoint's own adversarial-review fix pass (the two evidence-access RPCs, which already used a non-raising return shape for their other branch, now record `result='denied'` before returning rather than raising) -- the remaining ~10 raise-based RPCs in this capability, and the equivalent shape repository-wide, were correctly judged out of this checkpoint's own bounded scope: converting every raise-based authority/version/self-approval guard across the repository to a non-raising, always-audited return shape is a cross-cutting architectural change, not a capability-sized fix | Medium | `OPEN` | Runtime agent / a dedicated cross-cutting audit-on-denial hardening prompt | The one instance that was safely fixable in place (both evidence-access RPCs) is fixed. No workaround exists for the remaining raise-based paths without either (a) a durable secondary audit sink outside the caller's own transaction (a real infra addition, e.g. a Postgres `NOTIFY`-driven external logger or a genuinely separate autonomous-transaction mechanism, neither of which exists in this repository today), or (b) restructuring every affected RPC to a non-raising, caller-must-check-status return shape -- both are capability-spanning changes, not a bounded fix | Not currently a blocker for any Phase 6 capability (251-255 all pass their own gates; the underlying authority/encryption/masking controls this gap sits beside all independently hold, confirmed by live adversarial-review reproduction) -- but is a real, disclosed security-monitoring blind spot on this repository's single most sensitive data class (decrypted bank account numbers and tax identities), and should be weighed before any external security assessment or production readiness claim involving Phase 6 |

Found while fixing a live-reproduced `CG-S11-PRC-010` (Prompt 259) instance of the resulting crash (`app.decide_vendor_profile_review` called this function with no lock on its own parent row, so two concurrent double-decides derived the identical governance idempotency key and the loser hit a raw, unhandled `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "approval_requests_tenant_idempotency_unique"` all the way up through the caller). That specific reachable path was closed in `supabase/migrations/20260730670000_harden_procurement_batch_257_259_review_fixes.sql` by locking `app.decide_vendor_profile_review`'s own parent row first (so a concurrent second caller now blocks and re-reads instead of ever reaching `app.request_approval` with a colliding key) -- and, separately, this batch's own `app.create_procurement_exception_request` was fixed with its own advisory lock. But `app.request_approval` itself is shared by every domain that composes the Platform Approval Engine (Commercial quotation/credit approvals included, not just Procurement), and any OTHER call site anywhere in the repository that does not already hold a row lock serializing its own idempotency key before calling it would hit the identical raw, unhandled crash | Medium | `OPEN` | Runtime agent / the Platform Approval Engine's own owning team (PLT-123) | None implemented at the shared function itself -- wrapping `app.request_approval`'s own INSERT in a `begin ... exception when unique_violation then select existing row ... end` (the same defense-in-depth every other C-01-hardened RPC in this repository already carries) is a one-function fix that would close this class at the single shared choke point for every domain at once, but the function lives in a pre-existing, already-VERIFIED PLT-123 migration outside `CG-S11-PRC-008..010`'s own allowed-files scope (Procurement RFQ/vendor-comparison/approval only) | Not a blocker for batch 257-259's own three capabilities (every reachable call site in this batch's own code now holds a serializing lock before calling `app.request_approval`) -- but is a real, structural single-point-of-failure shared by every domain built on the Platform Approval Engine, and should be closed at the source before another capability composes this function without its own caller-side lock |
| `ISS-2026-045` | `CG-S11-PRC-010` (Prompt 259, Procurement Approval) §16/§25's own spec text requires "value/currency/branch dimensions" to be validated, but the shared threshold contract this migration built -- `app.procurement_approval_policies.min_value_amount` / `app.evaluate_procurement_approval_requirement(p_entity_type, p_tenant_id, p_value_amount, p_actor_auth_user_id)` -- has no currency dimension at all: `p_value_amount >= v_policy.min_value_amount` compares raw numeric amounts regardless of the entity's actual currency. Live-confirmed: a published `rate_version` policy with `min_value_amount=5000` returns the identical `required=true` for both a 6000 USD rate (genuinely large) and a 6000 IDR rate (economically trivial, ~USD 0.40) -- USD and IDR differ by roughly 15,000x in this repository's own supported currency pair. This governs two genuinely multi-currency entity types this same batch produces (`rate_version` via `app.vendor_rate_versions.base_amount`, `vendor_selection` via `app.vendor_comparison_offers.normalized_amount`). **Extended 2026-08-07 (batch `CG-S11-PRC-011`, Prompt 260):** `app.submit_purchase_order_for_approval` composes the identical `app._request_procurement_entity_approval` -> `app.evaluate_procurement_approval_requirement` call chain for `entity_type='purchase_order'`, threading `v_po.total_amount` (no currency) into the same currency-blind comparison -- re-confirmed live by the batch 260 Tier C review (a published `purchase_order` policy with `min_value_amount=1,000,000` returns identical `required=true` for both a 1,000,000 IDR PO, ~USD 62, and a 1,000,000 USD PO). `purchase_order` is now a third affected entity type, not a new gap -- PRC-260's own migration header discloses this extension accurately (`app.purchase_orders.currency` is threaded through to the audit context snapshot only, never into the comparison itself) rather than concealing it, and no new fix is owed by that batch for an architectural gap it did not introduce | High | `RESOLVED (CG-S11-PRC-020, Prompt 269 Hardening)` | Runtime agent / a dedicated Procurement Approval currency-threshold design task | None implemented. Two structurally different, both-legitimate fix shapes exist -- (a) add a `currency` column to `app.procurement_approval_policies` and widen the published-policy uniqueness key to `(tenant_id, entity_type, currency)`, letting a tenant publish one threshold per currency, or (b) keep a single tenant-wide reference currency per policy and convert the incoming amount via the same `app.convert_finance_amount`/FIN-194 machinery vendor comparison already composes, accepting an `insufficient_authority`/`finance_exchange_rate_missing` failure mode for a caller without `FIN:View` or a configured rate -- and Prompt 259's own spec text does not disambiguate which is intended. Choosing between them is a genuine architectural decision (schema shape, uniqueness key, and a new failure-mode contract for every future caller of `app.evaluate_procurement_approval_requirement`) this batch has no mandate to make under an adversarial-review fix pass, and picking wrong would need to be redone rather than incrementally corrected. **Resolved 2026-08-09 (`CG-S11-PRC-020`, Prompt 269 Hardening, `supabase/migrations/20260730810000_harden_procurement_approval_currency_normalization.sql`):** shape (b) was selected -- a new `app.procurement_approval_policies.policy_currency` column (default `USD`, validated via `app.validate_currency_code`), resolved at policy-create time from an explicit caller value, else the tenant's own published locale default currency, else `USD`. `app.evaluate_procurement_approval_requirement` gained a trailing `p_value_currency` parameter (DROP+CREATE, not a bare CREATE OR REPLACE -- appending a parameter changes a function's identity even with a default, verified live rather than assumed); a same-currency (or currency-omitted) comparison is byte-identical to before. A genuine mismatch normalizes via a new private helper `app._evaluate_procurement_currency_threshold` (a deliberate, disclosed variant of PRC-258's own `app._normalize_vendor_comparison_currency`) composing `app.convert_finance_amount`. Per this entry's own explicit reasoning ("under-routing... the more dangerous direction"), any conversion-unavailable outcome -- missing FX rate, unsupported currency, OR the actor lacking `FIN:View` -- FAILS CLOSED (`required=true`, reason `value_currency_unverifiable_defaulted_to_required`) rather than silently skipping governance; the whole-transaction abort risk that fail-closed choice could otherwise cause for a caller like `app.create_rate_version` (whose own authority model is `is_support_grant_authority`, unrelated to `FIN:View`) is handled by catching `insufficient_privilege` inside the new helper rather than letting it propagate. `app._request_procurement_entity_approval` threads its own already-received `p_currency` into the evaluator's new parameter -- no entity-type submit RPC (`rate_version`/`purchase_order`/`vendor_selection`) needed to change. Regression evidence: `scripts/db-tests/procurement-approval.sql`, six new test blocks proving (a) same-currency unaffected even for an actor with zero FIN permissions, (b) real cross-currency normalization both directions via the full `app.create_rate_version` chain, (c) `purchase_order`/`vendor_selection` normalize identically (including one direct-through-`_request_procurement_entity_approval` proof), (d) fail-closed on missing FIN:View with the full transaction committing, (e) fail-closed on a missing FX rate, (f) `vendor_contract`/`vendor_activation`/`exception_override` structurally cannot reach the new code path at all (`min_value_amount` can never be set for them, unchanged `procurement_approval_policies_value_dimension_check`). | Yes -- a tenant that publishes a rate_version/vendor_selection policy today gets a currency-blind threshold; a rate or selection denominated in a low-value-per-unit currency can cross a threshold meant for a high-value-per-unit currency (over-routing) or vice versa (under-routing, the more dangerous direction: a large IDR-denominated value could silently skip governance review if a policy was tuned assuming USD-scale numbers). Should be resolved before any tenant is told procurement governance thresholds are currency-aware |

Picking any of these under an adversarial-review fix pass, rather than a dedicated design task, risks getting the shape wrong in a way that would need to be redone | Not a blocker for this batch's own gates (the two implemented alternative flows -- partial fulfillment and post-issue amendment/cancellation -- both work end-to-end and are live-tested) -- but a real, disclosed spec-compliance gap against §22's explicit alternative-flow list, and should be resolved (or the spec's own scope narrowed with an explicit decision record) before this capability is presented as fully implementing its own source spec |

The identical shape is confirmed, unchanged, in the write RPCs of the already-VERIFIED PRC-257/258/259 siblings (by direct inspection: e.g. `app.issue_rfq`/`app.cancel_rfq`/`app.close_rfq_for_comparison` in `20260730640000_create_procurement_rfq.sql`, `app.revise_vendor_comparison`/`app.cancel_vendor_comparison` in `20260730650000_create_procurement_vendor_comparison.sql`, and most `create_*`/`decide_*`/`cancel_*` RPCs in `20260730660000_create_procurement_approval.sql` -- roughly 20-25 functions across those three migrations) and, per the same reasoning `ISS-2026-043` already applied to reads, is very likely present across most write RPCs written before either C-05 fix pattern existed -- an inherited, repository-wide convention, not invented by this batch. **Update (batch 3, `CG-S11-PRC-012/013/014`, Tier C security/RLS lens, live-reproduced independently): this exact gap reappeared in ~26 new write RPCs across PRC-261/262/263's own three migrations (`20260730700000`/`20260730710000`/`20260730720000`) -- every prompt's own per-prompt Tier B self-check claimed C-05 was clean without checking write RPCs specifically, the same blind spot this issue already exists to track. Fixed directly in the same fix pass** (folding `app.has_active_tenant_membership` into the not-found branch of all ~26 functions, the identical established pattern) -- so PRC-261/262/263 are no longer part of this issue's open scope. | Medium | `OPEN` | Runtime agent / a dedicated repository-wide by-id-write-RPC hardening prompt | Fixed for `CG-S11-PRC-011`'s own 6 functions (batch 260) and, as of batch 3, for all ~26 write RPCs across PRC-261/262/263 (plus the narrower creation-time "found but wrong tenant" variant on `app.draft_purchase_order_from_selection`/`app.draft_rfq_from_sourcing`, also fixed in batch 260) -- reordering the remaining ~20-25 write RPCs across PRC-257/258/259's own three migrations is a mechanical but large sweep genuinely outside any single-prompt batch's own allowed-files scope; the same disposition `ISS-2026-043` already established for the analogous read-RPC sweep | Not a blocker for PRC-257/258/259/261/262/263's own capabilities (all fixed directly where found) -- but PRC-257/258/259 still disclose their own real tenant_id to a zero-membership cross-tenant caller via this exact error-ordering shape on a DENIED write attempt, and should be swept together with `ISS-2026-043`'s own still-open repository-wide read-RPC sweep before any external security assessment or production-readiness claim |

Building this properly requires a genuine design decision this batch's Tier C fix pass has no mandate to make on its own: a new nullable `corrects_bill_id`/`correction_type` column on `app.vendor_bill_match_cases` (an additive migration, in scope for a dedicated task but not a Tier C fix-pass sized to close live-reproduced correctness/security defects), validation that the corrected bill shares the same vendor/currency, and a decision on whether a correction is always routed through the existing exception-approval path (since it is inherently a variance-bearing event) -- correctly disclosed per `BUILD_EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md` §5.6 rather than built under Tier C time pressure | Not a blocker for PRC-265's own capability as built (non-PO match, partial invoice, and tolerance-exception approval all function correctly and are live-tested) -- but a real, disclosed spec-compliance gap against §22's explicit alternative-flow list, and should be resolved (or the spec's own scope narrowed with an explicit decision record) before this capability is presented as fully implementing its own source spec |
| `ISS-2026-116` | Discovered at `CG-S13-CPL-002` (Prompt 300, Customer User Scope)'s own Tier C adversarial review: `app.accept_customer_portal_invite`/`app.set_customer_portal_account_membership_status` (`supabase/migrations/20260801010000_create_customer_portal_account_scope.sql`) raise a distinguishable `customer_portal_membership_not_found` (errcode `no_data_found`) vs. `insufficient_authority` for a nonexistent vs. a real-but-unauthorized `p_membership_id`, deviating from the ATW-023 (`app.get_customer_inventory_balance` et al.) precedent of one identical anti-enumeration error regardless of true cause. Live-confirmed distinguishable by the reviewing lens | Low | `OPEN` | Runtime agent / next checkpoint touching `app.accept_customer_portal_invite`/`app.set_customer_portal_account_membership_status` | Not fixed — accepted as bounded, non-blocking risk. `p_membership_id` is a random, non-guessable uuid the caller must already possess (from their own invite notification) or already have legitimate visibility into via `app.list_customer_portal_account_memberships` (itself identity-checked). No practical enumeration path exists: an attacker who does not already hold a real membership id gains nothing distinguishable beyond "this specific random uuid does not exist," which reveals no other identity's data. Straightforward fix for whichever future checkpoint picks it up: raise the identical `customer_portal_membership_not_found` message/errcode for both the not-found and the wrong-actor branches, mirroring `app.get_customer_inventory_balance`'s own single anti-enumeration error shape | Not a blocker for `CG-S13-CPL-002` or Phase 8 — bounded, disclosed, no practical exploitation path identified. Should be swept together with any future repository-wide anti-enumeration consistency pass |
| `ISS-2026-115` | Discovered at the Phase 8 kickoff checkpoint (`CG-S13-CPL-001`, Prompt 299) while establishing a fresh baseline `pnpm run db:test` pass: `scripts/db-tests/hris-overtime-timesheet.sql` (Phase 7, `HRT-281`, Overtime and Timesheet) pins its overtime request's `work_date` to `current_date` (line 169) and then hard-asserts the server-computed `eligible_classification = 'weekday'` (line ~284). 2026-08-16, the real date this checkpoint's baseline ran, is a Sunday; `app.decide_overtime_request`'s classification logic correctly returned `'weekend'` for that date, causing the test's own hardcoded assertion to fail (`ERROR: assertion failed: expected approved/120/weekday/120, got approved/120/weekend/120`). The production classification logic is not in question -- verified correct for the actual day of week -- only the test fixture's own date assumption, unpinned to a guaranteed weekday, is wrong. Confirmed non-Phase-8: `hris-overtime-timesheet.sql` is 100% Phase 7 (HRIS) scope; this checkpoint touches no HRIS file. Confirmed genuinely date-dependent, not a one-off: `bash scripts/db-tests/run.sh` halts (`set -euo pipefail`) at this exact file every time it is run on a Saturday or Sunday; a scratch re-run excluding only this one file confirmed all other 168 `scripts/db-tests/*.sql` files pass clean on the same checkpoint | Low | `OPEN` | Runtime agent / next checkpoint authorized to touch `scripts/db-tests/hris-overtime-timesheet.sql` (Phase 7/HRIS scope, not Phase 8) | None implemented -- fixing a pre-existing Phase 7 test fixture is outside this kickoff's own Phase 8 planning-only scope, per `AGENTS.md` "Fix only task-caused failures. Log unrelated/pre-existing failures and create a separate recovery task." Straightforward fix for whichever future checkpoint picks it up: pin `v_work_date`/the request's date to a literal known-weekday date (or derive the nearest Monday-Friday from `current_date`) instead of using `current_date` directly, mirroring how every other date-bearing fixture elsewhere in `scripts/db-tests/` uses a literal or offset-from-a-literal date rather than the wall-clock date | Not a blocker for Phase 8 or for Phase 7's own already-`VERIFIED` status (Phase 7 closed before this defect was discovered, and the underlying classification logic this test exercises is correct) -- but `pnpm run db:test` will continue to halt at this exact file on every Saturday/Sunday run until fixed, which will recur and inconvenience future checkpoints (Phase 8 batches included) unless a future checkpoint in Phase 7's own scope pins the date |

## 4. Issue records

### ISS-2026-002 — Parallel-session collision (no single-writer discipline)

Two sessions ran Prompt 21 concurrently on separate branches (`…-oanf5a`, `…-b492y3`); both merged to `main` and the merge corrupted the discovery baseline and duplicated the context set (see `ERROR_LEDGER.md` ERR-2026-001). Root cause: no lock/convention preventing concurrent execution of the same runtime step. **Handling:** designate one authoritative build branch per runtime step; do not launch parallel agent sessions on the same step; a future prompt must verify checkpoint lineage before writing. Status `OPEN` until a written single-writer convention is adopted (candidate: note in `AGENTS.md`).

**Recurrence (2026-07-14, third session):** a third branch, `claude/eloquent-mayer-s40hn4`, was cut from `main` at `d587445` *before* this reconciliation (`CG-S2-DISC-001-R1`) had merged. It independently discovered the same underlying corruption and — not knowing about `-R1` — resolved it the opposite way (root-level context kept canonical, `docs/runtime/*` marked superseded) while also completing Step 2 discovery Prompts 22–34. On merge with `main`, that branch's canonical-location choice was reverted in favor of this ratified decision (`docs/runtime/` canonical); its Step 2 discovery deliverables (Prompts 22–34, `docs/discovery/02_*.md`–`14_*.md`) were kept and layered on top of the reconciled baseline (see `TASK_LEDGER.md` `CG-S2-DISC-002..014` and `CHANGE_MANIFEST.md` `CHG-2026-003`). This is the second occurrence of this exact issue and reinforces that it remains `OPEN` until a single-writer convention is actually enforced, not just documented.

**Recurrence (2026-07-15, fourth occurrence — reconciliation never actually performed):** a fourth branch, `claude/sleepy-ride-4vxsk6`, diverged from `origin/main`@`27389a4` (PR #8, Prompt 45) and independently executed Prompts 46–51, Phase 0 kickoff (80), and Prompts 81–82 — nine commits, three full architecture documents, a full Phase 0 kickoff, and two Phase 0 capability prompts — in parallel with this branch (`agent/cargogrid-autonomous-build`) doing the equivalent Prompts 49–51/80/81. That branch opened GitHub PR #10 15 seconds after this branch's PR #9 merged into `main`, confirming near-simultaneous parallel execution. The two lineages' outputs for the same task IDs materially differ (e.g. 607 vs. 401 traced requirement items for the "same" Prompt 49/CG-S3-ARCH-014 output). A prior session halted further Phase 0 execution on discovering it and recorded `BLOCKED_WORKTREE` in `HANDOFF.md`, pending an explicit operator decision among three reconciliation options. Full detail: `ERROR_LEDGER.md` `ERR-2026-002`.

**Recurrence (2026-07-15, fifth occurrence — corruption now committed to `main`, escalated to Sev-1):** without that operator decision ever being recorded, PR #10 and then PR #11 (this run's designated branch) were both merged into `main` (`a8a197f`, `b7653cb`). Because neither merge produced git conflict markers (both lineages' edits were pure insertions relative to their shared base), git silently concatenated both divergent lineages' full content into the same files. `docs/architecture/14_REQUIREMENT_PHASE_TRACEABILITY.md`, `15_RISK_RANKED_CRITICAL_PATH.md`, and `16_STEP3_CLOSURE_REPORT.md` each now contain two complete, contradictory copies of the same document (confirmed: two `## 1.` sections in each, 607-item vs. 401-item traceability totals). `docs/runtime/HANDOFF.md`, `CARGOGRID_BUILD_STATUS.md`, and `TASK_LEDGER.md` show the same stacking pattern for narrative/status content. Full detail and evidence: `ERROR_LEDGER.md` `ERR-2026-003`. This is the clearest evidence yet that `ISS-2026-002` needs an *enforced* single-writer lock (e.g. a pre-flight check in each routine invocation that fails fast if any other open PR/branch already claims the current task-ID range, and a rule that merging a PR while a sibling PR covering the same task-ID range is still open requires explicit reconciliation, not a fast-forward/no-conflict merge) — a documented convention has now been silently violated five times.

**Resolution of the 5th-occurrence corruption (2026-07-15):** the operator authorized adopting **Lineage A** as authoritative. `docs/architecture/14..16_*.md` were rewritten as single coherent Lineage A documents (607-item baseline), Prompt 82 was re-verified against it, and the duplicate Phase 0 build logs were consolidated onto the singular prompt-package path. `ERR-2026-003` is `RECOVERED`. This cleared the *content* corruption and unblocked Phase 0.

**Enforcement adopted (2026-07-15, `CG-S5-PH0-008`, Prompt 87 — closes the *process* gap):** `docs/git/GIT_STRATEGY.md` §7 documents, and `AGENTS.md`'s "Required pre-flight" section now mandates, a two-part pre-flight collision check before starting any Phase 0+ prompt: (1) list open PRs/branches via the GitHub API/MCP and stop if another session claims the same task-ID range — **executed for real this checkpoint**: zero open PRs, and the only branch with commits ahead of `origin/main` is this session's own `agent/cargogrid-autonomous-build` (`claude/sleepy-ride-8pg1em`, this session's harness-assigned branch, is fully contained in `origin/main` — confirmed via `git merge-base`, not assumed); (2) `pnpm run git:check` (`scripts/git/check-worktree-collision.ts`, 4 passing tests) automates the locally-checkable half — a genuine fork between two `agent/*`/`claude/*` branches (each having commits the other lacks), the exact `ERR-2026-002`/`ERR-2026-003` precondition. **Status `RESOLVED`**: this is now a documented, followed, and partially-automated procedure rather than an unenforced convention silently skipped five times — no further action required unless a 6th occurrence demonstrates the procedure itself is insufficient.

### ISS-2026-003 — No root `.gitignore` (RESOLVED)

Reported during Step 2 discovery: no `.gitignore` tracked, harmless while documentation-only but required before application code/secrets/build artifacts land. **Resolved 2026-07-15** at `CG-S5-PH0-006` (Prompt 85, `docs/build-log/phase-00/PH0-85.md`): root `.gitignore` was the first file added this checkpoint, before `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, or any other file — covers `node_modules/`, `.next/`, `.env*.local`, Supabase local runtime state, build/test caches, editor/OS files. Verified: `git check-ignore -v .env.local` confirmed the rule fires; a real secret scan across every file added this checkpoint found none. **Status `RESOLVED`** — no further action.

### ISS-2026-001 — Primary source documents not tracked (RESOLVED)

Reported by session A at checkpoint `53e3d4a`, where the six primary sources were absent. They were subsequently uploaded (commit `db1742c`) and are now tracked under `docs/blueprint/` (Product Concept Brief + Charter/BPR/UX/Technical/Delivery). Verified at reconciled checkpoint `d587445`: `git ls-files docs/blueprint/` returns all six sources (plus `tes.md` placeholder). **Status `RESOLVED`** — no further action; source-fidelity verification can now trace to in-repo sources. The `tes.md` placeholder has since been formally classified `CONFIRMED_PLACEHOLDER` (empty, 1-byte file) in `docs/discovery/10_PLACEHOLDER_DEAD_CODE_INVENTORY.md` finding `PH-001`; it awaits owner-approved deletion (out of scope for read-only discovery since it sits inside the authoritative `docs/blueprint/` folder).

### ISS-2026-004 — `check-worktree-collision.test.ts` hardcoded a specific branch name (RESOLVED, Low)

Discovered `2026-07-15` during `CG-S5-PH0-012` (Prompt 91, Testing Foundation), running `pnpm run test` on this checkpoint's own designated branch `claude/lanjut-btusq6` (every prior Phase 0 checkpoint ran on `agent/cargogrid-autonomous-build`). `scripts/git/check-worktree-collision.test.ts`'s test `"agent/cargogrid-autonomous-build (the current branch) is reported as diverged from origin/main"` (added at `PH0-87`, Prompt 87) asserted that `checkWorktreeCollision()`'s result includes a diverged-branch entry named exactly the literal string `agent/cargogrid-autonomous-build` — a session-identity assumption baked into the test, not a property of the collision-detection algorithm under test. `checkWorktreeCollision()` itself was never defective (confirmed: it correctly reported `claude/lanjut-btusq6` as the diverged branch; the suite's other 3 assertions passed unchanged throughout). **Fix (this checkpoint, out of `PH0-091`'s literal file scope but a one-line, behavior-preserving, non-weakening correction of a test that would otherwise leave the shared `quality` CI gate red for every future checkpoint regardless of branch name):** the test now reads `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` instead of a hardcoded string — asserts the same property (current branch is reported as diverged), against whichever branch is actually checked out. Verified: `pnpm run test` — 58/58 (was 57/58 with the hardcoded assertion failing before this fix, confirmed reproducible via `git stash`/re-run before committing the fix). **Status `RESOLVED`** — no `checkWorktreeCollision()` behavior changed, only the test's fixture assumption.

### ISS-2026-005 — `CHANGE_MANIFEST.md` gap for Prompts 83–90 (OPEN, Low)

Discovered `2026-07-15` during `CG-S5-PH0-012` (Prompt 91), while adding this checkpoint's own change-manifest entry. `docs/runtime/CHANGE_MANIFEST.md`'s actual last entry before this checkpoint was `CHG-2026-023` (`ERR-2026-003` discovery, `2026-07-15`) — but `docs/build-log/phase-00/PH0-83.md` through `PH0-90.md` (Prompts 83–90, `CG-S5-PH0-004..011`) each state in their own §11/closing section that a `CHG-2026-0NN` entry (e.g. `PH0-90.md` cites `CHG-2026-031`) was added to this manifest in the same checkpoint; no such entries exist in the file (confirmed via `grep -n '^### CHG-2026-' docs/runtime/CHANGE_MANIFEST.md`, this checkpoint). **Impact assessed as low:** every other record this manifest cross-references (`TASK_LEDGER.md` rows, the build logs themselves, `docs/adr/**`) is independently present and correct — this is a gap in one manifest's own completeness, not evidence that the underlying Prompts 83–90 work is untrustworthy (their build logs, ADRs, and code/doc artifacts are all directly verifiable on their own). **Handling:** out of `PH0-091`'s scope to backfill eight historical entries for checkpoints this session did not author (risk of fabricating detail not directly observed); this checkpoint adds its own entry (`CHG-2026-024`, the correct next sequential number given the file's real content, not `032`) and records the gap here for a future documentation-focused task to backfill from each cited build log. **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity, non-blocking.

**Update (`2026-07-16`, `CG-S5-PH0-020`/Prompt 99's integrated-verification traceability audit):** the `PH0-091` choice above ("next sequential given the file's real content") means `CHG-2026-024`–`CHG-2026-031` were assigned twice — once as the phantom index-table rows this issue describes (referencing `CG-S5-PH0-004..011`, Prompts 83–90), and again as the real `###` entries `PH0-091`–`PH0-098` (Prompts 91–98) actually added. This checkpoint's audit re-confirmed the gap but did not attempt the backfill (same out-of-scope reasoning, restated in `docs/build-log/phase-00/PH0-99.md` §4/§5); it did adopt a different convention for its own entry going forward — `CHG-2026-032`, the lowest ID not used anywhere in the file today — specifically so no further collision is added while this issue stays open. A future backfill task must renumber the phantom `024`–`031` index rows (or the real `PH0-091`–`098` entries, whichever it displaces) as part of closing this issue, not merely add the eight missing historical entries.

**Update (`2026-08-03`, `ATW-226A`):** the identical failure mode recurred, this time in Phase 5. `docs/runtime/CHANGE_MANIFEST.md`'s last entry before this checkpoint was `CHG-2026-155` (Prompt 218, Finance Closure Verification, `2026-07-29`) — but Prompt 219 (Phase 5 README), `CG-S10-ATW-001..006` (Prompts 220–225), and the `ATW-226` nine-child decomposition reconciliation checkpoint each did real, evidenced work (build logs and execution-index rows all present and correct) without adding their own manifest entry. Unlike the Prompts-83–90 case, no ID-collision risk exists here (no phantom entry ever claimed `156`–onward), so no special numbering convention was needed — `ATW-226A`'s own entry simply uses the correct next sequential number, `CHG-2026-156`. Backfilling the six-plus missing historical entries remains out of scope for the checkpoint that discovers the gap, the same reasoning `PH0-091` already established; this issue's own title is retained as-is (Prompts 83–90) since renumbering/retitling it to cover both ranges would itself be exactly the kind of historical-record edit `ISS-2026-006`'s own handling section already declined to do.

### ISS-2026-006 — Broken historical citations of deleted plural `docs/build-logs/` files (ACCEPTED_RISK, Low)

Discovered `2026-07-15` during `CG-S5-PH0-013` (Prompt 92, Documentation Foundation), running the new `scripts/docs/check-doc-links.ts` validator against the full repository for the first time. Four files — `docs/build-logs/CG-S5-PH0-001_phase0_execution_index.md`, `..._phase0_wbs.md`, `..._002_source_alignment_context_bootstrap.md`, `..._003_requirement_traceability_baseline.md` — are cited by `docs/build-log/phase-00/PH0-81.md`, `PH0-82.md`, `docs/runtime/CHANGE_MANIFEST.md` (multiple historical checkpoint entries), and `docs/runtime/ERROR_LEDGER.md`. Every citation was correct at authoring time: these files were the plural-directory-convention Lineage B build logs, real and present when `PH0-81.md`/`PH0-82.md`/those `CHANGE_MANIFEST.md`/`ERROR_LEDGER.md` entries were written. A *later* checkpoint's `ERR-2026-003` recovery (`docs/runtime/HANDOFF.md` §1: "duplicate Phase 0 build logs consolidated") deleted them as part of standardizing on the singular `docs/build-log/phase-00/` path — correctly, per that checkpoint's own reconciliation decision — but did not also update the (by-then-historical, append-only) documents that cited the old paths. **Handling:** rewriting `PH0-81.md`/`PH0-82.md`'s own text, or the historical checkpoint sections of `CHANGE_MANIFEST.md`/`ERROR_LEDGER.md`, to erase what those documents actually cited at the time would itself compromise the append-only evidence discipline these records exist to provide (`AGENTS.md` "Branch, commit, and checkpoint rules": "a task is not a valid checkpoint until documentation and evidence are committed" — evidence, once committed, is not revised to look retroactively correct). Instead: `scripts/docs/check-doc-links.ts`'s `KNOWN_HISTORICAL_BROKEN_LINKS` allowlist excuses exactly these four enumerated, dated paths (with this issue ID cited in the code comment) so the validator stays useful for catching *new* breaks without either hiding this one or forcing a historical rewrite. **Status `ACCEPTED_RISK`** — no further action required; if a future documentation-focused task chooses to backfill `ISS-2026-005`'s `CHANGE_MANIFEST.md` gap, it may opportunistically add a forward-pointer note to these four historical entries, but that is not required to close this issue.

### ISS-2026-007 — `pnpm audit` calls a retired npm endpoint (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-07-15` during `CG-S5-PH0-015` (Prompt 94, Security Baseline Controls). Running `pnpm audit` against this repository's real, current lockfile (`pnpm-lock.yaml`) failed with `ERR_PNPM_AUDIT_BAD_RESPONSE`: both `registry.npmjs.org/-/npm/v1/security/audits/quick` and `registry.npmjs.org/-/npm/v1/security/audits` responded `410 Gone` — "This endpoint is being retired. Use the bulk advisory endpoint instead" (exact error captured this checkpoint, `docs/standards/SECURITY_STANDARDS.md` §5). `npm audit` was attempted as a fallback and requires a `package-lock.json`, which this repository deliberately does not carry (`ADR-0002`) — generating a second, non-authoritative lockfile solely to audit was rejected as a worse outcome. **Handling:** no CI gate was wired for this — either a hard-fail (blocks every future PR for an upstream endpoint problem, not a real dependency vulnerability) or a soft-pass via `--ignore-registry-errors` (a fabricated "audited, clean" signal with no real subject) would violate this repository's own established "never fabricate a passing gate" discipline (`docs/build-log/phase-00/PH0-88.md` §3). **What remains a real control:** `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` (already wired in CI since `PH0-88`) still guarantees deterministic, lockfile-pinned installs — this issue is specifically about the *absence of automated vulnerability-database checking*, not about install determinism. **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (a real, disclosed gap in supply-chain evidence, though no specific vulnerability is known or suspected) — re-attempt owner: DevEx, once pnpm ships bulk-advisory-endpoint support (pnpm `11.13.1` is available upstream as of this checkpoint, but upgrading the pinned major version is out of `PH0-94`'s scope, Prompt 94 §12's "no broad dependency upgrade"; GitHub's native Dependabot alerts are a viable interim signal but enabling them is a repository-settings change, not a code change, and was not performed unilaterally this checkpoint).

### ISS-2026-008 — `check-secrets.ts`'s generic-assignment pattern is narrower than sibling foundations' PII coverage (RESOLVED, Low)

Discovered `2026-07-16` during `CG-S5-PH0-020` (Prompt 99, Phase 0 Integrated Verification), via `scripts/verification/phase0-integration.test.ts`'s cross-foundation sensitive-key-pattern consistency suite — a test that runs the *same* representative key names (`password`, `npwp`, `bankAccountNumber`, `salary`) through all three independently-authored pattern lists together, something no single module's own isolated test suite (each written at a different prior checkpoint — `PH0-93`, `PH0-94`, `PH0-97`) could have caught on its own. Result: `scripts/observability/logger.ts`'s `redact()` and `scripts/product-analytics/analytics.ts`'s `findProhibitedProperties()` both correctly flag all four representative keys; `scripts/security/check-secrets.ts`'s `GENERIC_HARDCODED_SECRET_ASSIGNMENT` pattern (`secret|password|token|api[_-]?key|private[_-]?key`) flags only `password`, not `npwp`/`bankAccountNumber`/`salary`. Prompt 99 explicitly deferred the scope judgment call to a dedicated task rather than deciding it unilaterally during verification (§11: "default no repair").

**Resolved `2026-07-16` during `CG-S5-PH0-021` (Prompt 100, Phase 0 Hardening)** — the dedicated task Prompt 99 named. Decision: **keep `check-secrets.ts` scoped to credential shapes; do not widen it to general PII detection.** Rationale: (1) industry-standard secret scanners (gitleaks/trufflehog/detect-secrets) target key/token/password shapes, not arbitrary PII — widening the scope would make `check-secrets.ts` a second, overlapping PII scanner, which Prompt 100 §24's "no new capability" rule forbids; (2) this repository already has three real, tested, purpose-built PII-handling mechanisms for their own respective data-flow surfaces — `scripts/data-classification/registry.ts`/`check-registry.ts` (schema-field classification), `scripts/product-analytics/analytics.ts` (event-property rejection), `scripts/observability/logger.ts` (log-field redaction) — so a hardcoded PII literal in source is correctly out of `check-secrets.ts`'s lane, not an uncovered risk; (3) a real PII value should never be a literal in tracked source regardless of its key name, which is a code-review/data-classification concern, not a key-name-pattern-matching one. **Evidence:** `docs/standards/SECURITY_STANDARDS.md` §3 now states this boundary explicitly; `scripts/security/check-secrets.ts` carries a one-line scope comment; `scripts/verification/phase0-integration.test.ts`'s dedicated test was rewritten from "documents a disclosed gap" to "proves the intentional boundary holds in both directions" (asserts `check-secrets.ts` still does not match the PII-shaped keys, and that `logger.ts` still redacts them regardless — the PII-handling responsibility is proven to land somewhere, not merely absent from one module). Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-00/PH0-100.md`. **Status `RESOLVED`** — a documented design boundary, not a defect; no code behavior changed, `pnpm run security:check` still reports zero findings.

### ISS-2026-053 — `app.enqueue_job` (PLT-132)'s idempotency replay matches the key but never verifies the target tuple (OPEN, Low)

Discovered `2026-08-08` during `CG-S11-PRC-017` (Prompt 266, Procurement Dashboard and Reports), while implementing `app.enqueue_procurement_report_export`. `app.enqueue_job` (`supabase/migrations/20260719180000_create_background_job_framework.sql`, PLT-132) accepts an `idempotency_key` and, on a replay with the same key, returns the existing `app.jobs` row unconditionally — it never compares the replay's own `job_type`/`payload` against the original row's, so a caller that reuses a key for a genuinely *different* request silently receives the first request's job back instead of a rejection. This is the C-01 class ("idempotency-tuple gaps... claiming full tuple comparison but comparing only a leading subset," recurring at `PRC-257`/`258`/`259`, `PRC-261`/`262`/`263`) applied to a shared Platform primitive rather than a single domain RPC.

**Handling:** `PLT-132`'s own migration is already-applied and outside `CG-S11-PRC-017`'s allowed-files scope (a Phase-1 Platform capability, not a Phase-6 Procurement one) — fixing it upstream is a separate task with its own mandate and its own regression surface (every module that already calls `app.enqueue_job`: `COM-159`, `FIN-213`, `OPS-183`, and now `PRC-266`). `app.enqueue_procurement_report_export` performs its own real, full-tuple pre-check against `app.jobs` (`job_type`/`report_type_code`/`parameters`) before ever calling `app.enqueue_job`, raising `idempotency_key_conflict` on a genuine mismatch — this closes the gap for every caller reachable through this one new entry point, but does not fix `app.enqueue_job` itself, so any *other* future caller of `app.enqueue_job` that skips an equivalent pre-check remains exposed. **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (mitigated at every currently-known call site's own layer; no live-reproduced cross-tenant or financial-integrity impact — a key collision only ever returns a caller's own already-enqueued job, never another tenant's) — re-attempt owner: whichever future task next touches `PLT-132`'s own migration file.

### ISS-2026-054 — C-05 tenant-id-disclosure oracle, widened to ≥10 more by-id RPCs across PRC-251/252/253/254/256 (RESOLVED, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S11-PRC-019` (Prompt 268, Procurement/Vendor Integrated Verification), by a live security-isolation sweep introspecting the full `get_/list_` by-id RPC surface (107 functions) via `pg_get_functiondef` against a live, fully-migrated database, independently re-derived by the Prompt 268 synthesis itself. `ISS-2026-043` (read RPCs) and `ISS-2026-048` (write RPCs) already register the pattern "a by-id RPC resolves the target row's real `tenant_id`, then echoes it verbatim into the `insufficient_authority` error text to a caller who has not yet been shown to belong to that tenant" — both entries enumerate specific fixed/still-open function lists. This sweep found the identical vulnerable shape, unfixed, in at least 10 further functions spanning PRC-251 (the phase's very first, unbatched, pre-`ADR-0021` capability), PRC-252, PRC-253, PRC-254, and PRC-256 — none of which appear in either issue's own enumerated disposition text: `app.suspend_vendor_profile` (and, by the identical code template, its lifecycle-transition siblings `reactivate_`/`archive_`/`blacklist_vendor_profile`), `app.get_vendor_assessment_score_breakdown`, `app.list_vendor_assessment_findings`, `app.list_vendor_assessment_template_criteria`, `app.list_vendor_assessment_corrective_actions`, `app.get_vendor_compliance_requirement`, `app.get_vendor_compliance_waiver`, `app.get_vendor_compliance_document`, `app.decide_vendor_compliance_waiver`, `app.get_vendor_payment_term_proposal`, `app.get_sourcing_request_history`, `app.list_sourcing_candidates`. Live-reproduced directly by the Prompt 268 synthesis for one instance (`app.get_vendor_compliance_requirement`): a fresh tenant + a real `vendor_compliance_requirements` row + an `auth.users` identity with ZERO `principal_memberships` rows anywhere, called with that identity's own session simulated — raised `insufficient_authority: identity 00000000-0000-0000-0000-0000000c0502 lacks PRC:View (no_active_assignment) for tenant 11111111-1111-1111-1111-111111111111`, disclosing the real tenant_id to a caller who is not a member of any tenant. The two write-path instances tested (`suspend_vendor_profile`, `decide_vendor_compliance_waiver`) correctly BLOCKED the actual state-changing action — only the tenant_id disclosure is the defect, not a privilege-escalation-to-write. `app.get_sourcing_request` itself remains the one already-disclosed, still-open `ISS-2026-043` instance, reconfirmed unchanged, not a new finding.

**Handling:** Not fixed by Prompt 268 (a documentation-only Integrated Verification checkpoint, `docs/build-log/phase-06/PRC-268.md` §6.2/§12: "this prompt does not fix defects, it finds and registers them"). The repair is the same already-established pattern `ISS-2026-043`/`048`'s own fixed instances use (fold the tenant-membership check into the not-found branch before disclosing any resolved `tenant_id`). **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (matches `ISS-2026-043`/`048`'s own severity — cross-tenant read/write access itself is correctly blocked in every case tested; only a low-value identifier is disclosed to a non-member) — owner: PRC-251/252/253/254/256's own owning capabilities, same repair pass that eventually closes `ISS-2026-043`/`048`'s remaining instances (Prompt 269, Hardening, is the next-eligible task with a mandate to fix Phase 6 findings).

**Resolved 2026-08-09 (`CG-S11-PRC-020`, Prompt 269 Hardening, `supabase/migrations/20260730820000_harden_procurement_c05_tenant_disclosure_sweep_batch5.sql`):** all 15 named functions fixed using the exact already-established pattern (`app.get_rfq`'s own template) — `app.has_active_tenant_membership(v_tenant_id, p_actor_auth_user_id)` folded into the SAME not-found branch the row-miss case already raised, so a non-member now gets the identical not-found error a genuinely missing id would produce, and only a real member ever reaches the real `evaluate_permission`/authority check below it. No permission check was weakened — the authority check itself is unchanged, only its ordering relative to the tenant-membership pre-check. `app.suspend_vendor_profile` also received `ISS-2026-055`'s own fix in the same `CREATE OR REPLACE` (see that entry). Regression evidence: one live-reproduced test per function (or per owning db-test file, matching each file's own established convention) added to `procurement-vendor-registration.sql`, `procurement-vendor-assessment.sql`, `procurement-vendor-compliance.sql`, `procurement-vendor-financial-security.sql`, and `procurement-sourcing.sql` — each proves a zero-membership identity calling the function against a real target row gets a not-found-shaped error, never the real `tenant_id`. `procurement-sourcing.sql`'s own two PRE-EXISTING cross-tenant assertions for `list_sourcing_candidates`/`get_sourcing_request_history` (which had asserted `insufficient_privilege`, the exact disclosing shape this fix closes) were updated to assert the new not-found shape instead — a deliberate, disclosed test-behavior change, not a weakened test. `app.get_sourcing_request` itself remains the one already-registered, still-open `ISS-2026-043` instance (out of this fix's own 15-function scope, tracked separately).

### ISS-2026-055 — `vendor_profiles` lifecycle-transition write path discloses `record_version` before the authority check (RESOLVED, Low)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S11-PRC-019` (Prompt 268), by direct code inspection of `app.suspend_vendor_profile` (`supabase/migrations/20260730580000_create_procurement_vendor_registration.sql`) while investigating `ISS-2026-054` above, independently re-confirmed by the Prompt 268 synthesis reading the live function definition a second time. The function's `p_expected_version` check runs and can raise a `stale_version` error containing the row's real current `record_version` strictly BEFORE `app.evaluate_permission` is ever called — the same code template is shared by its lifecycle-transition siblings (`reactivate_`/`archive_`/`blacklist_vendor_profile`). A much lower-value oracle than `ISS-2026-054` (a numeric version counter, not a tenant/business-data value), and the same authorization-check-ordering class already disclosed at LOW severity for the Platform Approval Engine (`ISS-2026-049`, "entity_type discloses before authority check").

**Handling:** Not fixed by Prompt 268 (docs-only checkpoint). Fold the version check, like the tenant check (`ISS-2026-054`), behind or into the not-found/authority gate. **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (a version counter disclosure, no tenant/business-data value, no privilege escalation) — owner: PRC-251, same hardening pass as `ISS-2026-054`.

**Resolved 2026-08-09 (`CG-S11-PRC-020`, Prompt 269 Hardening, `supabase/migrations/20260730820000_harden_procurement_c05_tenant_disclosure_sweep_batch5.sql`):** each of `app.suspend_vendor_profile`/`reactivate_vendor_profile`/`archive_vendor_profile`/`blacklist_vendor_profile` was individually read (a structural sweep, not a blind grep-and-copy) and confirmed to share the identical ordering bug — all four reordered to fetch the row (folded into `ISS-2026-054`'s own not-found branch) → evaluate the authority check → THEN the `stale_version` check, so neither the real `tenant_id` nor the real `record_version` is ever disclosed to a caller not yet shown to hold both tenant membership and the required permission. Regression evidence: `scripts/db-tests/procurement-vendor-registration.sql` gained one new test block proving, for all four functions, both (1) the cross-tenant not-found shape (`ISS-2026-054`) and (2) a real tenant member who both lacks the required authority and supplies a deliberately stale `expected_version` gets `insufficient_authority`, never `stale_version` — confirming the new ordering, plus a sanity check that the fixture vendor's `lifecycle_status`/`record_version` are genuinely untouched by every rejected call.

**Correction, same checkpoint (2026-08-09, `CG-S11-PRC-020` Prompt 269 Hardening's own adversarial security-regression review round, before this checkpoint's own two new migrations were ever committed):** the resolution paragraph directly above, and this same checkpoint's own build log (`docs/build-log/phase-06/PRC-269.md` §2 Fix 5), both originally stated that `app.decide_vendor_compliance_waiver` was individually checked and confirmed to NOT share this ordering defect ("its own `self_approval_not_allowed` identity check runs before `stale_version` on identity grounds"). A live re-check against the actual applied function body (`pg_get_functiondef`), prompted by this same round's own instruction to spot-check any "checked and found NOT affected" claim, found that statement FALSE: `stale_version` ran BEFORE both `self_approval_not_allowed` and the `PRC:Approve`/`PRC:Reject` authority check in the function's real body, so a real tenant member holding neither authority (and not the waiver's own requester) could still extract the waiver's genuine current `record_version` via a deliberately stale `p_expected_version`, before ever being told they lacked authority to decide it. Live-reproduced: `viewer@pcmp1.test` (real `PRC:View`-only tenant member, `PRC:Approve=false`, `PRC:Reject=false`, confirmed via `evaluate_permission`) calling `app.decide_vendor_compliance_waiver` with a real waiver id and a wrong `p_expected_version` received `stale_version: ... expected version 999 but found 3` — the row's real version — instead of `insufficient_authority`.
