Not a full authorization bypass (re-checked separately with the correct version: the same actor is still correctly denied `insufficient_authority`) — a disclosure-ordering defect only, matching this issue's own severity and shape. **Fixed in place, same migration, before commit** (`supabase/migrations/20260730820000_harden_procurement_c05_tenant_disclosure_sweep_batch5.sql`, this checkpoint's own not-yet-committed file — edited directly rather than stacked with a further migration, per this checkpoint's own applied-migration discipline): `app.decide_vendor_compliance_waiver` now runs `self_approval_not_allowed` and the authority check (their own prior relative order to each other unchanged) BEFORE `stale_version`/`invalid_transition`, matching the same "authority before state-disclosure" ordering as the four `vendor_profiles` functions above. Regression evidence: `scripts/db-tests/procurement-vendor-compliance.sql` gained two new assertions (a non-requester lacking authority, and the requester itself, each supplying a deliberately stale `expected_version`) proving `insufficient_authority`/`self_approval_not_allowed` fire, never `stale_version`, plus a sanity check that the fixture waiver's real `record_version`/`status` are untouched by any of the rejected calls.

### ISS-2026-056 — `app.list_vendor_contracts` missing `(tenant_id, created_at desc)` covering index (RESOLVED, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S11-PRC-019` (Prompt 268), by a live performance/migration sweep, independently re-confirmed by the Prompt 268 synthesis via a direct `pg_indexes` query against a live, fully-migrated database. `app.vendor_contracts` (`supabase/migrations/20260730700000_create_procurement_vendor_contract.sql`, PRC-261) carries indexes on `(tenant_id, contract_number, version_no)` ×2, `(tenant_id, effective_end)` partial, and `(tenant_id, vendor_master_id, status)` — but none on `(tenant_id, created_at desc)`. Its three sibling list-instrument RPCs in the same PRC-25x/26x family all carry the matching index, independently confirmed present: `rfqs_tenant_created_idx` (`app.rfqs`), `purchase_orders_tenant_created_idx` (`app.purchase_orders`), `vendor_comparisons_tenant_created_idx` (`app.vendor_comparisons`). `app.list_vendor_contracts`'s own internal query (`select * from app.vendor_contracts where tenant_id = p_tenant_id ... order by created_at desc limit ...`) is the identical shape to its siblings' own internal queries, but lacking the matching index the planner cannot do an ordered Index Scan with early-LIMIT termination the way `app.list_purchase_orders` does. At a realistic proportional 25-tenant/~500-contract-per-tenant scale (12,500 rows total, ~4% per-tenant selectivity) it still correctly uses a tenant-scoped Bitmap Index Scan (via the `(tenant_id, vendor_master_id, status)` index's leading column) and does not degrade to a full Seq Scan; under a live-reproduced single-tenant-concentration test (one tenant holding 68% of the table), the identical query degrades to a full Seq Scan on `app.vendor_contracts`. A CargoGrid tenant with a genuinely large individual contract count would see this RPC's latency scale linearly with its own row count rather than the O(log n + limit) behavior its three siblings already have.

**Handling:** Not fixed by Prompt 268 (§13: "prefer no production schema change" — a docs-only Integrated Verification checkpoint is not the place for a new index migration). Add a `(tenant_id, created_at desc)` index on `app.vendor_contracts`, mirroring the three sibling tables exactly. **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (no seq scan yet at realistic proportional scale; a real, reproducible degradation under single-tenant concentration, which a large enterprise customer could plausibly reach) — owner: PRC-261, a future schema-touching hardening task (Prompt 269).

**Resolved 2026-08-09 (`CG-S11-PRC-020`, Prompt 269 Hardening, `supabase/migrations/20260730820000_harden_procurement_c05_tenant_disclosure_sweep_batch5.sql`):** `create index vendor_contracts_tenant_created_idx on app.vendor_contracts (tenant_id, created_at desc);` — plain `CREATE INDEX`, mirroring the three sibling tables' naming exactly (no `CONCURRENTLY` anywhere in this repository's own migrations, confirmed by grep). Verified live against a seeded, skewed dataset mirroring this entry's own reproduction (7,497 total rows, one tenant holding 5,100 = 68.0%): `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` against `app.list_vendor_contracts`'s own exact query shape went from `Seq Scan on vendor_contracts` (203 buffer hits, 3.03ms) with the index absent to `Index Scan using vendor_contracts_tenant_created_idx` (3 buffer hits, 0.09ms) with it present — confirmed via a throwaway database, never wired into the permanent `db:test` suite (a multi-thousand-row seed does not belong in the standing regression gate).

### ISS-2026-057 — PRC-251 "Bulk-import staged vendors" (§22) — undisclosed C-23 gap (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S11-PRC-019` (Prompt 268), by a spec-anchor-completeness sweep diffing each capability's own source-prompt §22 against its build log's own itemized disclosures, independently re-confirmed by the Prompt 268 synthesis three ways: (1) `251_VENDOR_REGISTRATION_ONBOARDING_PROMPT.md:100` names "Bulk-import staged vendors" as a required alternative flow; (2) the only trace in the codebase is the `bulk_import` value in the `intake_source` CHECK constraint and TypeScript enum (`supabase/migrations/20260730580000_create_procurement_vendor_registration.sql:279,583`; `server/contracts/vendor-profile/vendor-profile.ts:22,444`) — i.e. a caller can tag ONE vendor draft as `bulk_import`-sourced via the ordinary single-vendor RPC, with no batch/CSV ingestion RPC, no staged-multiple-rows table, no commit/review-staged-rows workflow, and no PLT-131-style import adapter anywhere (`grep -rln "bulk_import|bulkImport" app/ server/ src/ lib/` returns only the one contract-file enum definition); (3) `docs/build-log/phase-06/PRC-251.md` §8 ("Residual disclosures / not built") itemizes nine other real gaps in meticulous, precedent-citing detail and never names bulk-import. Contrast with PRC-255 (Vendor Rate & Pricelist), which built a real import adapter and precisely disclosed its own import-scope boundaries — PRC-251 does neither. Same undisclosed-named-requirement (C-23) shape the taxonomy already tracks for `ISS-2026-046`/`047` (PRC-260) and `ISS-2026-050` (PRC-261) — a further instance, in a different capability.

**Handling:** Not fixed by Prompt 268 (docs-only checkpoint; also, per `BUILD_EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md` §5.6, building a real batch-import pipeline is itself an architectural decision, not a hardening-sized fix). **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (no live production exposure — a caller cannot currently reach a broken bulk-import path since none exists; the gap is a missing named capability, not an insecure one) — owner: PRC-251, a future Procurement task with an explicit schema/import-adapter mandate (mirroring PLT-131/PRC-255's own precedent).

### ISS-2026-058 — PRC-262 "manual confirmation with evidence" (§22) — undisclosed C-23 gap (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S11-PRC-019` (Prompt 268), by the same spec-anchor-completeness sweep as `ISS-2026-057`, independently re-confirmed by the Prompt 268 synthesis three ways: (1) `262_VENDOR_CAPACITY_AVAILABILITY_PROMPT.md:100` names "...or manual confirmation with evidence" as a required alternative flow; (2) `grep -n "evidence|file_id|attachment|initiate_file_upload" supabase/migrations/20260730710000_create_procurement_vendor_capacity.sql` returns zero matches, run directly this checkpoint — no evidence/file-linking mechanism exists on `app.vendor_capacity_offers`/`app.vendor_capacity_reservations` at all; (3) `docs/build-log/phase-06/PRC-262.md` §5 ("Residual, disclosed limitations") itemizes exactly four items (no recurring-availability declaration, no vendor-portal surface, no UI caller for the by-id reservation read, no UI picker for resource/contract linkage) — none names the missing evidence-attachment capability, matching the capability's own Tier B self-check which marked taxonomy class C-10 (file/evidence linking) "N/A (no file-linking in this capability)" without recognizing the named spec requirement behind that N/A. Same undisclosed-named-requirement (C-23) shape as `ISS-2026-046`/`047`/`050`/`052`/`057` — a further instance.

**Handling:** Not fixed by Prompt 268 (docs-only checkpoint; building real evidence/file-linking on capacity offers/reservations is itself an architectural decision touching the Document/File Engine, not a hardening-sized fix). **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (no live production exposure — no evidence-linking path exists to be insecure; the gap is a missing named capability) — owner: PRC-262, a future Procurement task with an explicit schema/file-linking mandate.

### ISS-2026-059 — `app._calc_vendor_kpi_rate_validity` (PRC-264) fails `procurement-vendor-performance.sql` deterministically when run between ~00:00–03:59 UTC (OPEN, Low)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S11-PRC-020` (Prompt 269 Hardening)'s own adversarial review round, closing out three independent review lenses (financial-currency-correctness, security-regression, full-regression-and-integration) — all three independently hit the identical `pnpm run db:test` failure (`scripts/db-tests/procurement-vendor-performance.sql:978`, `assertion failed: expected New Vendor rate_validity to be computable (a real 0%, not a missing-data case)`) and all three correctly confirmed, via `git stash`/baseline re-runs, that it reproduces identically against the pre-PRC-269 baseline — i.e. genuinely pre-existing, not caused by any of this checkpoint's own changes. None of the three lenses root-caused it beyond "pre-existing, unrelated" (out of each lens's own assigned scope); this checkpoint's own fix round did, while reproducing the finding, and records the real mechanism here rather than leaving only a prose disclosure with no ledger entry (the specific gap the full-regression lens itself flagged as CONFIRMED — no `ISS-2026-0NN` tracking existed for this failure despite every other deferred gap in this checkpoint having one).

**Root cause, live-verified, not assumed:** `app._calc_vendor_kpi_rate_validity` (`supabase/migrations/20260730740000_create_procurement_vendor_performance.sql:1186`) computes its own calendar-day denominator via `generate_series(p_window_start::date, (p_window_end - interval '1 day')::date, interval '1 day')`. For the test's own fixed 21-hour window (`v_window_start := date_trunc('hour', now()) - interval '1 hour'`, `v_window_end := v_window_start + interval '21 hours'`), the upper bound simplifies to `(p_window_start - interval '3 hours')::date` — which falls on the PREVIOUS calendar date relative to `p_window_start::date` whenever `p_window_start`'s own hour-of-day is less than 3 (UTC, this sandbox's server timezone). When that happens, `generate_series` is called with a start date AFTER its end date, which PostgreSQL correctly returns as zero rows (ascending step, descending bound) — collapsing `v_den` (and therefore `sample_size`) to `0`, which flips `is_computable` to `false` even though the window itself is real, non-empty, and (per this function's own comment) meant to be unconditionally computable. Live-reproduced directly, isolated from the rest of the test file: at `now() = 2026-08-09 02:38:31 UTC`, `(date_trunc('hour', now()) - interval '1 hour')::date = 2026-08-09` but `((date_trunc('hour', now()) + interval '20 hours') - interval '1 day')::date = 2026-08-08` — start-date > end-date, `generate_series(...)` returns 0 rows, confirmed via direct `select count(*) from generate_series(...)`. This is a genuine, deterministic, time-of-day-dependent off-by-one in the function's own day-window boundary arithmetic, not a flaky/nondeterministic test — it fails on every run whose wall-clock time falls in roughly the `00:00`–`03:59 UTC` band (as this checkpoint's own sandbox time did throughout this review round, explaining why all three lenses, and this checkpoint's own re-runs, hit it consistently) and passes cleanly outside that band (confirmed: every prior checkpoint's own build log, including `PRC-269`'s own original §7, reported this file passing).

**Handling:** Not fixed by this checkpoint. `app._calc_vendor_kpi_rate_validity` lives in `PRC-264`'s own migration (`20260730740000_create_procurement_vendor_performance.sql`), a different capability/checkpoint than the six findings `CG-S11-PRC-020` (Prompt 269) was chartered to fix, and this checkpoint's own adversarial-review mandate is to close the three lenses' CONFIRMED findings against Prompt 269's own fix pass, not to open and fix an unrelated capability's pre-existing defect discovered incidentally — the same scope-discipline reasoning `docs/build-log/phase-06/PRC-269.md` §6 already applied when it first disclosed this failure in prose without registering it. The eventual fix is bounded and low-risk once undertaken: replace the `(p_window_end - interval '1 day')::date` upper bound with an inclusive-end computation that does not depend on time-of-day sign (e.g. `(p_window_end - interval '1 second')::date`, or deriving the day count directly via `date(p_window_end) - date(p_window_start)` with an explicit floor/ceiling decision on partial trailing days) — a `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` against this same function, plus a regression test that pins the window to specific UTC hours (e.g. `01:00`–`22:00` AND `10:00`–`11:00`) rather than a `now()`-relative window whose pass/fail depends on when the suite happens to run. **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (self-heals outside the ~4-hour daily UTC band; no data-integrity impact — the metric value that would be recomputed with the wrong `is_computable` is never published as a scorecard line in a way that silently fabricates a value, since `is_computable=false` correctly still routes the vendor into `insufficient_kpi_coverage`/reduced-coverage handling rather than showing a wrong number) — but a real, currently-red `db:test` gate for roughly one-sixth of any given day; owner: PRC-264, a future task with an explicit mandate to touch `20260730740000`'s own capability.

### ISS-2026-060 — Vendor rate engine (PRC-255) has no `zone`/`distance` pricing dimension (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S11-PRC-022` (Prompt 271, Closure Verification), by the commercial-engine verification lens plus an independent synthesis-pass re-check. `255_VENDOR_RATE_PRICELIST_PROMPT.md` (lines 60, 76, 100) names `zone`/`distance` explicitly, twice, as required rate-engine tiering dimensions alongside route/service/mode/fleet/container/weight/volume — all of which are genuinely implemented. `ADR-0015` (the founding Phase 2 rate-engine decision) also names both dimensions. Independently `grep`-confirmed this checkpoint: `zone|distance` returns zero real matches (only unrelated prose) across both `supabase/migrations/20260724150000_create_commercial_rate_cost_lookup.sql` and `supabase/migrations/20260730620000_extend_commercial_vendor_rate_for_procurement.sql` — the two migrations that jointly define the rate engine's tiering surface. `docs/build-log/phase-06/PRC-255.md` §9/§10 discloses the rate engine's tax-dimension absence in detail (registered as `ISS-2026-039`) but names zone/distance nowhere; the gap is absent from `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` and the Phase 6 WBS prior to this checkpoint.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this checkpoint (Prompt 271 is read-only verification, per its own charter). **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (every other named rate dimension works correctly; this is a bounded, disclosed scope gap against two explicitly named dimensions, not a correctness defect in what is implemented) — owner: PRC-255, a future Procurement rate-engine task with an explicit mandate to add zone/distance tiering.

### ISS-2026-061 — Vendor invoice match-exception approval bypasses the canonical Platform approval engine (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S11-PRC-022` (Prompt 271, Closure Verification), by the Finance-boundary verification lens plus an independent synthesis-pass re-check. `259_PROCUREMENT_APPROVAL_PROMPT.md` (line 91) names "activation/rate/selection/PO/contract/match/exception" as the 7 entity types the canonical Platform approval engine (`app.approval_requests`/`app.request_approval`/`app.decide_approval_step`, `PLT-123`) must govern. Independently confirmed this checkpoint: `supabase/migrations/20260730660000_create_procurement_approval.sql:190`'s `entity_type` enum carries exactly 6 values (`vendor_activation`, `rate_version`, `vendor_selection`, `purchase_order`, `vendor_contract`, `exception_override`) — no 7th value for vendor invoice match. `app.request_vendor_bill_match_exception_approval`/`app.decide_vendor_bill_match_exception_approval` (`supabase/migrations/20260730750000_create_procurement_vendor_invoice_matching.sql:1739,1786`) is a wholly separate, self-built maker-checker mechanism with zero reference to `app.approval_requests`/`request_approval`/`decide_approval_step` anywhere in that migration (`grep`-confirmed, zero hits) and no `p_reauth_confirmed_at` parameter — meaning it also carries none of the canonical engine's delegation/escalation/stale-source-invalidation apparatus, not only its MFA gate. The MFA-absence sub-point is already correctly disclosed at `docs/build-log/phase-06/PRC-265.md` §5 item 11 as "a deliberate reading of this prompt's own spec text, which never names MFA anywhere"; the broader canonical-engine bypass itself is not disclosed anywhere (`PRC-259.md`, `PRC-265.md`, `KNOWN_ISSUES.md`, `PROCUREMENT_VENDOR_RUNBOOKS.md` §8 all checked, none names it).

**Handling:** Not fixed by this checkpoint (read-only verification). The mechanism is functionally real and live-proven (`FINTEST-016`, item 19) — this is a governance-consistency gap (a structurally weaker parallel approval path for one of 7 named entity types), not a broken or insecure one. **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity — owner: PRC-259 or PRC-265, a future task with an explicit mandate to either migrate match-exception approval onto the canonical engine or to formally, explicitly disclose the divergence as an accepted design variant.

### ISS-2026-062 — Vendor assignment (PRC-263) has no shipment-leg/task granularity (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S11-PRC-022` (Prompt 271, Closure Verification), by the contract-execution verification lens plus an independent synthesis-pass re-check. `263_VENDOR_ASSIGNMENT_PROMPT.md` (lines 24, 60, 76, 96, 125) repeatedly names "shipment/leg/task" as the assignment capability's own extension target and explicitly requires a "multi-leg" test scenario. Independently `grep`-confirmed this checkpoint: `leg_id|shipment_leg|task_id` returns zero real matches (one prose comment only) against `supabase/migrations/20260730720000_create_procurement_vendor_assignment.sql`, and `leg` returns zero matches against `scripts/db-tests/procurement-vendor-assignment.sql` — no multi-leg test exists. `app.confirm_vendor_assignment` composes with the unmodified `app.assign_resource` (`OPS-172`) at shipment-order granularity only, never with `app.shipment_legs.carrier_master_id` (`ATW-221`, Phase 5). `docs/build-log/phase-06/PRC-263.md` §5's own 5-item residual-limitations list does not name this gap; it is also absent from `KNOWN_ISSUES.md`.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this checkpoint (read-only verification). The capability is fully correct and tested at the one granularity its own canonical Operations dependency (`OPS-172`) currently supports — this is a scope gap against an explicitly named extension, not a defect in what is built. **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity — owner: PRC-263, a future Procurement task with an explicit mandate to extend vendor assignment to shipment-leg/task granularity.

### ISS-2026-063 — Procurement dashboard query-budget mechanism has no dedicated test; large-scale load proof covers 4 of ~9 named surfaces (OPEN, Low-Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S11-PRC-022` (Prompt 271, Closure Verification), by the Finance-boundary/scale verification lens plus an independent synthesis-pass re-check. `server/queries/procurement-dashboard.ts` implements a real `withQueryBudget`/`ProcurementDashboardQueryTimeoutError` mechanism (structurally identical to its Finance/Operations siblings), but independently confirmed this checkpoint: `ls server/queries/*.test.ts` shows `finance-dashboard.test.ts`, `ops-dashboard.test.ts`, and `dashboard.test.ts` each carry a live query-budget-timeout unit test, while no `procurement-dashboard.test.ts` exists at all — the identical mechanism in Procurement's own dashboard is asserted only by code inspection, never proven live by any test. Separately, the large-scale `EXPLAIN`-based load proof from `docs/build-log/phase-06/PRC-268.md`/`PRC-269.md` (a real, live-verified ~35× improvement on a 5,000-tenant×vendor synthetic seed) covers only 4 of the roughly 9 named target-volume surfaces in Prompt 271 item 21 (vendor/rate search, RFQ list, PO list, vendor-contract list) — scorecards, match-case list, capacity, assignment, and export throughput rest on indexing discipline by design, not a measured large-scale proof. Neither gap is itemized in `docs/build-log/phase-06/PRC-266.md` §5's 10-item residual list or in `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` prior to this checkpoint. **Sibling note, tracked under this same ID rather than a separate one:** `FINTEST-016`'s `non_po` match mode and its `p_is_partial_invoice`/`p_is_consolidated_invoice` parameters (item 19) are real, dispatchable code paths in `app.create_vendor_bill_match_case` that are never exercised by any live test anywhere in the repository — every successful case-creation call in `scripts/db-tests/procurement-vendor-invoice-matching.sql` passes `false,false` for both flags.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this checkpoint (read-only verification). **Status `OPEN`**, Low-Medium severity (the mechanisms themselves are real and structurally sound; the gap is test/proof coverage, not a known-broken code path) — owner: PRC-266 (dashboard test) and PRC-265 (non_po/partial/consolidated match-mode test coverage), a future task with an explicit mandate to add the missing test files/scenarios.

### ISS-2026-064 — Employee Master (HRT-274): manager-team UI route, client-side document upload, and browser/accessibility E2E are disclosed, not built (OPEN, Low)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S12-HRT-002` (Prompt 274, Employee Master), by this checkpoint's own scope-completeness self-check before closing. The full domain/service layer for all three items is real, tested, and live: `app.list_my_team_employees` (manager-scoped, self-resolved, zero-PII-column query, `supabase/migrations/20260730830000_create_hris_employee_master.sql`) is proven live in `scripts/db-tests/hris-employee-master.sql`'s own "manager-scoped team view" block and wrapped by `server/queries/employee.ts`'s `listMyTeamEmployees`; `app.initiate_file_upload`/`app.files` (PLT-128) is correctly reused for employee documents (`record_type='employee'`, a real `employee_document` document type registered and a real per-tenant config-publish path proven live in the same db-test file's staged-import block), and the employee detail page's Documents tab lists real `app.files` rows. What is NOT built: (1) no dedicated route exists at a `/hris/my/team` path surfacing `listMyTeamEmployees` to a manager in the browser (the query/mutation layer is ready, no page consumes it yet); (2) the employee detail page's Documents tab has no client-side file picker/drag-drop upload form wired to `initiateFileUpload` — a caller would need to script the RPC call directly today; (3) no Playwright/axe-core spec exists for the employee directory page (`e2e/vendor-registration.spec.ts`'s own one-spec-per-first-real-page precedent is not yet followed for HRIS). None of the three blocks HR staff or an already-linked employee from using the shipped Employee Master surface (directory, detail, own-profile read/request-change, lifecycle actions, transfer, duplicate review) — all of which are real, RLS/RBAC-enforced, and exercised by `scripts/db-tests/hris-employee-master.sql`.

**Correction (`2026-08-09`, same-checkpoint review round, `CG-S12-HRT-002` fix pass):** this entry's original text also listed "staged import" among the surfaces HR staff can use today — inaccurate. A spec-completeness review lens found, and this fix pass independently confirmed, that staged import (`app.validate_employee_import_row`/`app.commit_employee_import_job`) had zero TypeScript service-layer wrapper at the time this entry was first written — unreachable from the shipped application, exercised only by raw SQL in `scripts/db-tests/hris-employee-master.sql`. The fix pass added `validateEmployeeImportRow`/`commitEmployeeImportJob` to `server/mutations/employee.ts` (mirroring the PRC-255 precedent, `server/mutations/procurement-rate.ts`), closing the service-layer gap — but no Server Action/UI trigger surface exists yet (disclosed above as items (1)/(2)/(3)'s own sibling gap, not separately filed, since independently verified NO PLT-131/132 adopter in this repository has a staged-import UI yet, including the PRC-255 precedent itself — not a regression against an established pattern).

**Handling:** Not fixed by this checkpoint (disclosed scope trim under time/size constraints for a single capability checkpoint, per Prompt 274 §28's own "if you have time, add [E2E]; if not, disclose it as a residual gap" instruction) — the staged-import TS wrapper gap named in the correction above WAS fixed in the same review-round fix pass. **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (UI-depth gaps over an already-real, already-tested service layer, no security/data-integrity exposure) — owner: HRT-274 follow-up or a later Phase 7 UI-hardening checkpoint (HRT-293/295 are the phase's own dedicated privacy/integrity-hardening slots and are the natural place to close this before Phase 7 closure).

### ISS-2026-065 — Employee Master (HRT-274): no effective-dated employee identity/lifecycle mechanism, despite a binding, repeated Prompt 274 requirement (OPEN, High)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during the same-checkpoint (`CG-S12-HRT-002`, Prompt 274, Employee Master) adversarial review round (correctness/concurrency lens), independently re-derived by the fix pass before being accepted. `274_EMPLOYEE_MASTER_PROMPT.md` names "effective dates"/"effective-dated"/"effective version" as a required, non-optional element in multiple sections: §5 ("one effective-dated workforce truth"), §13 (fields this checkpoint must extend the domain with), §17 (indexing "... effective period"), §18 (audit records must include "effective version"), §20 ("Define effective-dated employee identity, lifecycle, duplicate and correction invariants"). ADR-0023 Part B's own invariant language ("one employee workforce profile may link to zero or one active Platform user identity PER EFFECTIVE CONTEXT") is time-boxed phrasing a permanent, unconditional partial-unique-index (`employees_user_id_unique`) structurally cannot represent. The original migration's own decision note (`supabase/migrations/20260730830000_create_hris_employee_master.sql`) claimed this requirement was satisfied because `app.master_records` already carries `effective_from`/`effective_to` — live `grep` across the full migration found those two columns referenced nowhere outside that one comment: no function in the migration reads or writes them for any employee purpose, `app.employees` itself has no effective-dated column of its own (only simple point-in-time `hire_date`/`probation_end_date`/`employment_end_date`), and no write RPC (`create_employee_draft`, `update_employee_draft`, `transfer_employee`, or any lifecycle transition) accepts an effective-date parameter or supports scheduling, backdating, or an "as of" query. Every change takes effect immediately at `now()`.

**Handling:** The false claim itself is corrected in place (the migration's own header comment, `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-274.md` §3.1/§11, `docs/build-log/phase-07/00_EXECUTION_INDEX.md`, `docs/runtime/CARGOGRID_BUILD_STATUS.md`, and `docs/runtime/TASK_LEDGER.md` all now accurately describe the lifecycle as point-in-time, not effective-dated), but a genuine effective-dating mechanism (real temporal/bi-temporal columns, "as of" query semantics across every lifecycle RPC and read projection) is a materially new capability — judged architecturally out of scope for a bounded review-round fix, and force-building it without the same depth of proof this checkpoint gave everything else would risk shipping a new, unverified defect class rather than closing one. **Status `OPEN`**, High severity (a named, repeated, binding requirement is genuinely unimplemented, though no security/tenant-isolation exposure exists — every RPC that IS implemented correctly enforces authority/concurrency/masking regardless of this gap) — owner: a future HRT task, before `HRT-293` (Sensitive personal and payroll data controls) or `HRT-294` (Integrated verification), since both depend on this capability's data model being materially final.

**Update (`2026-08-09`, `CG-S12-HRT-003`, Prompt 275, Organization and Position Linkage):** HRT-275 delivers a genuinely effective-dated mechanism for one specific, real slice of this gap — `app.employee_position_assignments` (`supabase/migrations/20260730840000_create_hris_organization_position_linkage.sql`) is a real, new, effective-dated (`effective_start_date`/`effective_end_date`, a `validity_range` generated column, an `app.get_employee_current_assignment(p_as_of date)` point-in-time read) history table for an employee's position/grade/manager assignment specifically, satisfying Prompt 275's own §13 "effective range"/§20 "historical queries" requirement in full for that domain. **This does NOT close `ISS-2026-065`** — the two are different claims. `ISS-2026-065` is about the EMPLOYEE's own record (`lifecycle_status`, `hire_date`, `probation_end_date`, `employment_end_date`, and every HRT-274 lifecycle-transition RPC), none of which HRT-275 touches or effective-dates; `app.employees.lifecycle_status` still transitions immediately at `now()` with no scheduling/backdating/"as of" capability, exactly as this entry originally found. Disclosed here explicitly so a future reader does not conflate "HRT-275 shipped effective-dated assignments" with "the employee lifecycle gap is closed" — it is not. `ISS-2026-065` remains `OPEN`, High, with the same owner/timing as originally recorded.

**Update (`2026-08-15`, dedicated HRT follow-up task, HRT-ISS-065-CLOSURE):** the Prompt 297 closure checkpoint (`CG-S12-HRT-025`) independently re-confirmed this entry CONFIRMED STILL OPEN and named it the sole remaining blocker to `PHASE_7_VERIFIED` (`docs/build-log/phase-07/HRIS_TICKETING_CLOSURE_REPORT.md` §19/§20/§22), and its own §22 named the exact resume condition: "a dedicated HRT follow-up prompt... Design and implement a genuine effective-dated (or bi-temporal) identity/lifecycle mechanism for `app.employees`." The operator explicitly authorized this dedicated follow-up task to build it as bounded new capability work. **Status `RESOLVED`.** `supabase/migrations/20260731310000_add_hris_employee_lifecycle_effective_dating_iss2026065.sql` adds `app.employee_lifecycle_versions` — a real, versioned, effective-dated history table for the employee's own record (`lifecycle_status`, company/branch/department/position/manager, `employment_type`, `hire_date`/`probation_end_date`/`employment_end_date`), mirroring `app.employee_position_assignments`' own shape (`effective_start_date`/`effective_end_date`, a generated `validity_range`, `status`, `change_reason`, `previous_version_id` chain, `decided_by`/`decided_at`/`decided_reason`, `record_version`). Every one of the 7 lifecycle-transition RPCs named by the original finding (`create_employee_draft`, `update_employee_draft`, `transfer_employee`, `suspend_employee`, `terminate_employee`, `reactivate_employee`, `archive_employee_profile`) now accepts an optional `p_effective_date` (defaulting to `current_date`, fully backward-compatible — verified against every existing positional and named-parameter call site in this repository without editing any of them), with a genuine future-scheduled path (deferred to a new maintenance sweep, `app.activate_due_employee_lifecycle_transitions`, HRS:Override-gated/idempotent/`NOT_RUN` — no live scheduler exists anywhere in this repository, the same standing, disclosed `ISS-2026-015` gap) and a genuine backdated-correction path (gated at least as strictly as `HRS:Override`, a mandatory reason, and a supersede-before-insert reconciliation of any intervening history). `app.get_employee_lifecycle_as_of(p_master_record_id, p_actor_auth_user_id, p_as_of)` is the real "as of" read this entry's own §17/§18/§20 citations required, mirroring `app.get_employee_current_assignment`'s own shape — reconstructs genuine historical/current/future state directly from `app.employee_lifecycle_versions`, never from `app.employees`' own current-state columns. Composition with HRT-295's already-VERIFIED identity coupling (`app.transition_user_status`) is timing-correct in both directions, live-tested: an immediate/backdated transition couples in the same transaction exactly as HRT-295 established (byte-identical statements, unmodified); a future-scheduled transition defers the Platform-identity call until the sweep genuinely activates it, never earlier (no premature revocation) and never omitted (no regression of the HRT-295 security fix). Every existing downstream reader of `app.employees.lifecycle_status` (attendance/leave/overtime/payroll eligibility checks) was grepped and confirmed unmodified and correct as-is — additive, not breaking. Full regression + new-capability proof in `scripts/db-tests/hris-employee-master-lifecycle-effective-dating.sql` (a dedicated new file) plus a required signature-reference fix to `scripts/db-tests/hris-employee-master.sql`'s own pre-existing schema-privilege defense-in-depth test (the 7 RPCs' argument-type signatures genuinely changed — `DROP FUNCTION`+`CREATE FUNCTION`, since `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` cannot add a parameter). Full detail, design decisions, Tier B taxonomy self-check, and gate results: `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-ISS-065-CLOSURE.md`.

### ISS-2026-066 — Organization and Position Linkage (HRT-275): no bulk/multi-employee reorganization wizard, no live scheduler for future-dated assignment activation, no staged-import crosswalk for position/grade, and no browser/accessibility E2E (OPEN, Low)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S12-HRT-003` (Prompt 275, Organization and Position Linkage), by this checkpoint's own scope-completeness self-check before closing, mirroring HRT-274's own `ISS-2026-064` disclosure discipline. The full domain/service layer for every item below is real, tested, and live — what is disclosed here is UI/automation depth beyond it, not a broken or fabricated code path:

1. **No bulk/multi-employee reorganization wizard.** Every RPC (`app.propose_employee_position_assignment`, `app.decide_employee_position_assignment`, `app.cancel_employee_position_assignment`) and the UI wizard at `/hris/employees/[masterRecordId]/positions` operate one employee at a time. A "move an entire department's employees to a new org unit/position in one transaction" tool is a genuinely new capability, not a bounded extension of this checkpoint's own singular-employee-centric table (section 13's own "employee-assignment/version table" wording).
2. **No live cron/scheduler invokes `app.activate_due_employee_position_assignments` automatically.** The RPC is real, HRS:Override-gated, idempotent, and proven live in `scripts/db-tests/hris-organization-position-linkage.sql`'s own "maintenance sweep" block — but nothing in this repository calls it on a schedule yet (no `pg_cron` or equivalent scheduler exists anywhere in this repository, the same standing gap `ISS-2026-015` already discloses repository-wide). Until it is invoked (by hand, by a future job scheduler, or by a Server Action a human triggers), a future-dated, approved assignment's `app.employees.position_id`/`manager_employee_id` convenience-cache columns remain stale past their own `effective_start_date` until the sweep runs — `app.get_employee_current_assignment`'s own `p_as_of` read is unaffected (it reads `app.employee_position_assignments` directly, never the cache) and is always correct regardless.
3. **No staged-import (PLT-131/132) crosswalk for position/grade/assignment rows.** Section 19's own "map existing department/position strings to canonical IDs through staged, reviewed crosswalks" describes a bulk-migration capability this checkpoint deliberately did not build (disclosed in the migration's own header, decision 10) — building a first-of-its-kind crosswalk-import UI was judged disproportionate, unrequested scope for this checkpoint.
4. **No Playwright/axe-core E2E spec** exists yet for the position catalogue, organization tree, or assignment-timeline/wizard pages — matching HRT-274's own `ISS-2026-064` item (3) precedent, not yet followed here either.

None of the four blocks HR staff from using the shipped capability (position/grade CRUD, organization-linked position tree, propose/approve/reject/cancel assignment workflow with impact preview, effective-dated point-in-time reads, hierarchy reads) — all real, RLS/RBAC-enforced, and exercised by `scripts/db-tests/hris-organization-position-linkage.sql`.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this checkpoint (disclosed scope trim under time/size constraints for a single capability checkpoint, mirroring `ISS-2026-064`'s own identical disposition). **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (UI/automation-depth gaps over an already-real, already-tested service layer; no security/data-integrity exposure — item 2's staleness window is a disclosed, bounded read-freshness gap, not a correctness defect, since `app.get_employee_current_assignment` is always accurate) — owner: a later Phase 7 UI-hardening checkpoint (HRT-293/294/295, the phase's own dedicated hardening/verification slots) or a dedicated future scheduler-infrastructure task for item 2 specifically.

### ISS-2026-067 — Recruitment, Job Portal and ATS (HRT-276): no UI caller yet for duplicate-review, exports, assessment/interview cancel-shaped actions, and no standalone candidate directory (OPEN, Low)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S12-HRT-004` (Prompt 276, Recruitment, Job Portal and ATS), by this checkpoint's own Tier B taxonomy self-check (class C-20) before closing, mirroring `ISS-2026-064`/`ISS-2026-066`'s own disclosure discipline. The full domain/service layer for every item below is real, tested, and live — what is disclosed here is UI depth beyond it, not a broken or fabricated code path:

1. **No UI caller for duplicate-candidate search/flag/decide.** `app.search_candidate_duplicates`/`app.flag_candidate_duplicate`/`app.decide_candidate_duplicate` are real, RLS/RBAC-enforced, and proven live in `scripts/db-tests/hris-recruitment-ats.sql`'s own "candidate" block (exact email/phone match plus `pg_trgm` fuzzy name similarity, human-reviewed-only, never auto-merges) and wrapped by `server/queries/recruitment.ts`/`server/mutations/recruitment.ts` — no page or form triggers any of the three yet.
2. **No UI caller for `export_job_vacancies`/`export_candidates`/`export_applications`.** All three read RPCs are real, `HRS:Export`-gated, and reachable via the query layer — no export button/download flow exists in any of the recruitment pages shipped this checkpoint.
3. **No UI caller for `cancel_candidate_assessment`, `reschedule_interview`, or `cancel_interview`.** All three write RPCs are real and tested; the application-detail page surfaces assessment creation/recording and interview scheduling/completion/feedback, but not these three cancel/reschedule-shaped actions.
4. **No UI caller for candidate `set_candidate_status` (block/archive).** The RPC exists and is tested; no page exposes it.
5. **No standalone candidate directory or profile-edit page.** A candidate's profile is reachable today only via an application's own detail page (`/hris/recruitment/applications/[applicationId]`), never independently by candidate ID — there is no `/hris/recruitment/candidates` directory route.
6. **No Playwright/axe-core E2E spec** exists yet for any of the 6 new page families (`/hris/recruitment`, `/hris/recruitment/[vacancyId]`, `/hris/recruitment/applications/[applicationId]`, `/hris/recruitment/my-interviews`, `/careers/[tenantSlug]`, `/careers/[tenantSlug]/[postingToken]`) — matching HRT-274/275's own identical, still-unresolved E2E-coverage gap.

None of the six blocks HR staff or a public applicant from using the shipped capability (vacancy lifecycle, candidate/application pipeline, assessment, interview + feedback, offer lifecycle with approval routing, and the public job-application intake surface) — all real, RLS/RBAC-enforced, and exercised by `scripts/db-tests/hris-recruitment-ats.sql`.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this checkpoint (disclosed scope trim under time/size constraints for a single capability checkpoint, mirroring `ISS-2026-064`/`ISS-2026-066`'s own identical disposition). **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (UI-depth gaps over an already-real, already-tested service layer; no security/data-integrity exposure) — owner: a later Phase 7 UI-hardening checkpoint (`HRT-293`/`294`/`295`, the phase's own dedicated hardening/verification slots) or a near-term follow-up once real tenant usage clarifies which gaps (most likely duplicate-review and export) matter first.

### ISS-2026-068 — Recruitment, Job Portal and ATS (HRT-276): hiring managers have no self-scoped "assigned slice" read surface, unlike interviewers (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during the Tier C batch review-round fix pass for `CG-S12-HRT-004` (Prompt 276), CONFIRMED by direct code read: `grep -n hiring_manager supabase/migrations/20260730860000_create_hris_recruitment_ats.sql` shows `app.job_vacancies.hiring_manager_employee_id` (stored and write-time-validated at `app.create_job_vacancy_draft`/`app.update_job_vacancy_draft`) is never read back by any read RPC — `app.list_job_vacancies`, `app.get_job_vacancy`, `app.get_application_detail`, and every other read path gate solely on tenant-wide `HRS:View`. Contrast with the interviewer half of the identical requirement (this prompt's own §26, "hiring managers/interviewers see assigned slices"), which IS real: `app.get_my_assigned_interviews` is a genuine, tested, self-scoped, identity-matched read requiring zero `HRS` permission. A hiring manager who is granted plain `HRS:View` (the only way to read anything about their own vacancy today, since no narrower grant exists) therefore also sees every OTHER vacancy/candidate/application/offer in the tenant, not just their assigned one — broader access than the named requirement describes, not merely an absent nice-to-have.

**Handling:** Not fixed by the HRT-276 review-round fix pass — a real self-scoped hiring-manager read surface (at minimum one new RPC mirroring `app.get_my_assigned_interviews`'s own shape, plus a query/mutation-layer wrapper and a UI consumer) is a new capability addition, not a bounded defect repair, and is out of a fix pass's mandate per `docs/standards/BUILD_EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md` §5.6 ("genuinely outside the batch's allowed-files scope"). **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (a real, named access-shape gap — a hiring manager's own `HRS:View` grant is wider than the spec's own "assigned slice" framing intends, though this is a pre-existing grant a tenant chooses to make, not a cross-tenant or unauthenticated exposure) — owner: a near-term follow-up prompt in Phase 7 (most naturally paired with `ISS-2026-067`'s own disclosed UI-depth gaps, since both are "the domain/service layer undershoots the hiring-manager/interviewer half of §26").

### ISS-2026-069 — Job-offer approval (HRT-276) is the fourth domain forced to share PLT-121's single tenant-wide `config_type_code='approval'` singleton, undisclosed as a risk at the point it widened (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during the Tier C batch review-round fix pass for `CG-S12-HRT-004` (Prompt 276), CONFIRMED by direct code read: `app.config_objects` carries a unique index on `(config_type_code, coalesce(tenant_id,...), scope_level, coalesce(scope_id,...))` (`supabase/migrations/20260717130000_...` lines 220-223), so at most ONE published `'approval'` config_object can exist per tenant at `scope_level='tenant'`. Every current consumer — Sales quotation approval (COM-153), Commercial credit control, Procurement PO approval (PRC-259/260), and now HRT-276 job-offer approval — selects its routing definition with the byte-for-byte identical filter `co.config_type_code = 'approval' and co.tenant_id = <tenant> and co.scope_level = 'tenant' and cv.status = 'published'`, confirmed by direct grep across all four migrations. `app.request_approval`'s own `entity_type` parameter (here `'job_offer'`) is stored purely for record-keeping on the resulting request row and is never used to select or filter which routing definition applies. Consequence: a tenant cannot run independent approval chains per business domain — a routing definition a tenant intends for HR job offers (e.g. HR manager then HR director) instantly governs every pending/future Sales quotation, credit override, and Procurement PO approval in that tenant, and vice versa. This is a real, pre-existing PLT-121 (Phase 6) architectural constraint, not something HRT-276 introduced — but HRT-276 is the checkpoint that measurably widens its blast radius by bringing a fourth, HR-compensation/employment-specific domain into the same shared singleton, and this consequence was not surfaced as a risk in `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-276.md`'s own residual-gaps section (§8) or filed as any known issue at the time.

**Handling:** Not fixed by the HRT-276 review-round fix pass — domain-scoping `app.config_objects` (e.g. widening the uniqueness key to include a domain/entity discriminator, or adding a new `scope_level`) is a shared-schema redesign affecting every existing PLT-121 consumer across Phases 2/6/7, requiring its own ADR and change control per `AGENTS.md` "Scope and refactoring" ("Broad refactors, framework upgrades, shared-schema redesign... require dedicated prompts and ADR/change control"), not a bounded fix within one capability's own review pass. **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (a real cross-domain governance-isolation gap a tenant might reasonably expect NOT to exist, but each individual domain's own approval routing still functions correctly and is not itself insecure — no tenant/cross-tenant data exposure, no missing authorization check) — owner: a future Platform/Approval-Engine hardening prompt with a mandate spanning every PLT-121 consumer, not a single domain checkpoint.

### ISS-2026-070 — Onboarding and Offboarding (HRT-277): notification engine not wired, no live job worker/overdue scheduler, and no UI caller yet for preview/export/transfer scenario coverage (OPEN, Low)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S12-HRT-005` (Prompt 277, Onboarding and Offboarding), by this checkpoint's own Tier B taxonomy self-check (class C-23) before closing, mirroring `ISS-2026-064`/`066`/`067`'s own disclosure discipline. The full domain/service layer for every item below is real, tested, and live — what is disclosed here is integration/automation/UI depth beyond it, not a broken or fabricated code path:

1. **PLT-127 Notification Engine is not wired.** Task assignment, due/overdue transition, provisioning/revocation completion, and finalize-approval routing all capture a real `app.onboarding_case_events` row and a canonical `app.audit_logs` entry, but none of them calls into a notification/email/in-app-alert mechanism — an owner is never proactively told a task was assigned to them. This mirrors the same standing, repository-wide gap already disclosed for every other domain that reaches a state-transition point (no PLT-127 adopter exists yet anywhere in this repository, confirmed by `grep`).
2. **No live worker/scheduler exists for enqueued jobs or overdue detection.** `app.enqueue_job` is called for `checklist_version_publish` fan-out (a real, tested, `job_type`-enum-registered job per PLT-131/132 discipline) but nothing in this repository polls `app.background_jobs` and executes it — the same standing gap `ISS-2026-015` already discloses repository-wide. Separately, "overdue" is deliberately computed at read time (`due_at < now() and status not in ('completed','waived')`, no live scheduler, disclosed up front in the migration's own decision 5 / table comment) rather than via a cron-driven transition or notification — a case list read always reflects the true state, but nothing proactively flags an overdue task to anyone without a human opening the page.
3. **`app.preview_onboarding_case_start` and `app.export_onboarding_cases` have no UI caller.** Both are real, RBAC-gated read RPCs, reachable via the query layer and exercised by `scripts/db-tests/hris-onboarding-offboarding.sql`, but no page wires a "preview before starting a case" step or an export/download control.
4. **No dedicated `case_type='transfer'` db-test scenario.** The `case_type` CHECK constraint and every RPC treat `'transfer'` identically to `'onboarding'`/`'offboarding'` (no code path special-cases it), but the db-test suite exercises only `'onboarding'` and `'offboarding'` end-to-end; a transfer-specific scenario (e.g. a case with no new hire, no employment termination) is unexercised.
5. **No `beforeunload` unsaved-change guard** on the multi-field case-start or template-authoring forms, and **no Playwright/axe-core E2E spec** exists yet for any of the onboarding/offboarding page families — matching HRT-274/275/276's own identical, still-unresolved E2E-coverage gap.

None of the five blocks HR, IT, Finance, Operations, or an employee/manager task owner from using the shipped capability (checklist template authoring and versioning, case start with idempotent employee/user conversion, task assignment/completion/waive/reopen, access provisioning/revocation requests, finalize approval routing, cancellation, rehire) — all real, RLS/RBAC-enforced, and exercised by `scripts/db-tests/hris-onboarding-offboarding.sql`.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this checkpoint (disclosed scope trim under time/size constraints for a single capability checkpoint, mirroring `ISS-2026-064`/`066`/`067`'s own identical disposition). **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (integration/UI-depth gaps over an already-real, already-tested service layer; no security/data-integrity exposure) — owner: a later Phase 7 UI-hardening checkpoint (`HRT-293`/`294`/`295`, the phase's own dedicated hardening/verification slots) or a dedicated future notification/scheduler-infrastructure task for items 1-2 specifically.

### ISS-2026-071 — Onboarding and Offboarding (HRT-277): Finance/Operations/IT task-owner completion authority is uniformly `HRS:Edit`-gated, not task-owner-identity-gated (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-09` during `CG-S12-HRT-005` (Prompt 277, Onboarding and Offboarding), by this checkpoint's own Tier B taxonomy self-check (class C-23), CONFIRMED by direct code read: `app.onboarding_case_tasks.owner_type` (and its template-task counterpart) carries a CHECK constraint admitting `'hr'`, `'manager'`, `'employee'`, `'it'`, `'finance'`, `'operations'` (`supabase/migrations/20260730880000_create_hris_onboarding_offboarding.sql` lines 227, 350) — a real, named per-task owner category the checklist model is built around (section 15's own "asset/access/training/payroll/Operations handoff" owners). However, every write RPC that mutates a task (`assign_onboarding_task`, `complete_onboarding_task`, `reopen_onboarding_task`, `request_onboarding_access_provisioning`, `request_onboarding_access_revocation`) routes through the single shared preamble `app.resolve_onboarding_case_task_for_write`, which gates on tenant-wide `HRS:Edit` (or `HRS:Override` for waive/cancel) via `app.evaluate_permission` — `owner_type` and `owner_auth_user_id` are read and displayed but never checked as an alternative or additional authority path. Concretely: an IT staff member with `HRS:Edit` can complete a Finance-owned payroll-handoff task (and vice versa), and `owner_auth_user_id` (the specific individual a task is assigned to) is informational only — completing a task assigned to a named colleague requires no special relationship to that assignment, only the same tenant-wide HR permission every other task type requires. This is a materially different design from `app.get_my_assigned_interviews` (HRT-276) or `app.list_my_team_employees` (HRT-274), both of which grant a real, narrower, identity-matched self-scoped path requiring no domain permission at all.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this checkpoint. A genuine task-owner-identity-scoped authority path (e.g. "the actor may complete a task if they hold `HRS:Edit` for the tenant OR `owner_auth_user_id = actor`, mirroring `ISS-2026-068`'s own hiring-manager framing") is a new authority-shape addition, not a bounded defect repair within the Implement stage's own mandate, and risks introducing an unreviewed authority-boundary change under time pressure rather than through the deliberate `resolve_onboarding_case_task_for_write` single-choke-point this migration was built around. **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (every current call site requires a genuine tenant-scoped HR permission — `HRS:Edit`/`HRS:Override` — so this is not a missing-authorization-check or cross-tenant exposure; it is a design gap between the domain's own per-task owner-category model and the coarser tenant-wide permission actually enforced, meaning any tenant staff member holding `HRS:Edit` can act on any task regardless of its declared owner category) — owner: a near-term follow-up prompt in Phase 7, most naturally paired with `ISS-2026-068`'s own disclosed hiring-manager/interviewer access-shape gap, since both are "the domain/service layer's authority grain is coarser than the data model's own named owner/assignee concept."

### ISS-2026-072 — `app.evaluate_permission` (PLT-112) never checks `app.users.status`/tenant-identity status, and `app.transition_user_status`'s (PLT-110) revoke branch never revokes `app.role_assignments` (PLT-111) — a revoked identity retains full permission-gated authority until every role_assignment is separately, manually revoked (OPEN, High)

Discovered `2026-08-10` during the Tier C batch review-round fix pass for `CG-S12-HRT-005` (Prompt 277, Onboarding and Offboarding), CONFIRMED by direct code read and live reproduction against a disposable Postgres 16 database (no race needed, fully deterministic): `app.transition_user_status`'s `'revoked'` branch (`supabase/migrations/20260716102620_create_users.sql:271-285`) only calls `app.revoke_auth_identity` (PLT-107) and loops `app.revoke_principal_membership` (PLT-108) — it never touches `app.role_assignments` (PLT-111) at all. `app.evaluate_permission` (`supabase/migrations/20260716104519_create_rbac_evaluator.sql:39-99`), the sole RBAC authorization decision point used by every domain in the repository, joins only `app.role_assignments`/`app.role_versions`/`app.role_version_permissions` and never re-checks `app.users.status` or `app.has_active_tenant_membership`. Live-reproduced end to end: a fresh identity was granted a role carrying `HRS:Create`; `app.transition_user_status(..., 'revoked', ...)` was called (the exact call HRT-277's own `app.request_onboarding_access_revocation` makes); `app.evaluate_permission` for that identity still returned `allowed=true` (`reason=role_grant`) immediately afterward, `app.role_assignments.status` remained `'active'`, and the identity could still successfully call a real, permission-gated write RPC (`app.start_onboarding_case`) end to end — while `app.has_active_tenant_membership` correctly flipped to `false` for the same identity (so RLS-gated table reads ARE correctly blocked; only the `evaluate_permission`-gated RPC write path is not).

**Handling:** HRT-277's own review-round fix pass (migration `20260730890000`) closes this LOCALLY for `app.request_onboarding_access_revocation` — the only present-day caller of `app.transition_user_status(..., 'revoked', ...)` anywhere in the repository (confirmed by grep across all 189 migrations) — by explicitly looping and revoking every active `app.role_assignments` row for the target identity as part of that same governed call. The SHARED primitives (`app.evaluate_permission`/`app.transition_user_status`) themselves are NOT fixed — a proper centralized fix (either `app.transition_user_status`'s own revoke branch cascading `app.role_assignments` for every future caller, or `app.evaluate_permission` itself re-checking `app.users.status`/tenant membership as defense in depth) is a shared-schema/RBAC-evaluator change affecting every domain in the Platform, requiring its own dedicated ADR and Platform/RBAC hardening prompt per `AGENTS.md` "Scope and refactoring" — not a bounded fix within one capability's own review pass. **Status `OPEN`**, High severity (a real, live-verified authority-retention gap in the shared RBAC evaluator/user-lifecycle primitives; currently contained because no OTHER domain in the repository yet calls `app.transition_user_status` to revoke a user, but any FUTURE domain that does so without ALSO independently looping `role_assignments` — exactly as HRT-277's own local fix now does — will reproduce the identical gap) — owner: a dedicated Platform/RBAC hardening prompt with a mandate spanning `app.evaluate_permission`/`app.transition_user_status` and every existing/future consumer.

**Partial resolution (HRT-295, `2026-08-15`): the `app.role_assignments` half, closed centrally.** `supabase/migrations/20260731230000_couple_hris_employee_lifecycle_to_platform_identity_hrt295.sql` moves the role_assignments-revoke loop OUT of the one local call site and INTO `app.transition_user_status` itself: every ACTIVE `app.role_assignments` row for the target identity in the tenant is now revoked whenever the identity moves to EITHER `'suspended'` or `'revoked'` — not merely `'revoked'` — for EVERY current and future caller, not just `app.request_onboarding_access_revocation`'s own local loop (which becomes a harmless, idempotent duplicate — `app.revoke_role_assignment` no-ops cleanly on an already-revoked assignment). This was the direct, required prerequisite for `app.terminate_employee`/`app.suspend_employee` (HRT-274) to newly call `app.transition_user_status` themselves without inheriting the same incompleteness — see `ISS-2026-104` below. Live-reconfirmed: `scripts/db-tests/rbac-enforcement.sql`'s new regression block grants a real role, suspends the identity via `app.transition_user_status`, confirms zero active role_assignments and `app.evaluate_permission` denial, confirms reactivation alone does NOT restore the grant, then confirms an explicit `app.assign_role` re-grant does. **The `app.evaluate_permission` half of this finding remains explicitly OPEN** — re-checking `app.users.status`/tenant membership as defense in depth is a distinct, systemic, shared-RBAC-evaluator change touching every domain in the Platform, not an HRIS-scoped repair, and was correctly out of this checkpoint's bounded mandate (option (a), the concrete coupling below, closes the live-proven authority-retention path without it). Owner unchanged for the remaining half: a dedicated Platform/RBAC hardening prompt.

### ISS-2026-073 — Onboarding and Offboarding (HRT-277): a direct-hire onboarding case has no "approved direct hire" precondition anywhere in the implementation, despite Prompt 277 §21/§25 naming it (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-10` during the Tier C batch review-round fix pass for `CG-S12-HRT-005` (Prompt 277, Onboarding and Offboarding), CONFIRMED by direct code read and live reproduction: `docs/ai-agent-build-prompt-package/12-phase-07-hris-ticketing/277_ONBOARDING_OFFBOARDING_PROMPT.md` §21 (main flow) says "HR starts from an accepted offer or **approved** direct hire," and §25 (validation rules) separately, explicitly lists "Validate source candidate/**direct-hire approval**... before completion/finalization" as its own named requirement. `app.start_onboarding_case`'s `source_type='direct_hire'` branch (`supabase/migrations/20260730880000_create_hris_onboarding_offboarding.sql`, ~line 1002) requires only `p_full_name`/`p_employment_type`/a caller-supplied `p_idempotency_key`, gated solely by the case-level `HRS:Create` check already performed earlier in the function — no field, table, or PLT-123 (`app.request_approval`) routing call anywhere in this migration represents a "direct hire approval," and `app.submit_onboarding_case_for_finalize_approval` never checks for one either. Live-verified: an actor holding nothing but `HRS:Create` (no `HRS:Approve`, no separate direct-hire-approval permission of any kind) successfully created a real, linked `app.employees` row via `direct_hire` with zero approval precondition of any kind, and the resulting case could be driven all the way to finalize with no direct-hire-approval evidence ever recorded. By contrast, the `job_offer` source path effectively inherits an upstream approval already (HRT-276's own job-offer approval routing gates the offer reaching `status='accepted'` before `start_onboarding_case` will accept it) — only the `direct_hire` path is genuinely unguarded.

**Handling:** Not fixed by the HRT-277 review-round fix pass. A genuine "direct hire approval" precondition (e.g. a new PLT-123-routed approval step gating `direct_hire` case start or finalize, or a minimum-authority bar change requiring `HRS:Approve` rather than `HRS:Create` to start a `direct_hire` case) is a new governed-workflow capability addition, not a bounded defect repair — building it under review-pass time pressure risks an unreviewed, under-specified approval-routing shape (which approver role, sequential vs. threshold, whether it blocks case START vs. finalize) rather than the deliberate design this checkpoint's other PLT-123 integrations (job-offer approval, case finalize approval) received. **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (every `direct_hire` case-start call still requires a genuine tenant-scoped `HRS:Create` permission — this is not a missing-authorization-check or cross-tenant exposure; it is a named spec requirement, §25's own "direct-hire approval" validation, that has no implementation anywhere) — owner: a near-term follow-up prompt in Phase 7, scoped specifically to adding a direct-hire approval gate (most naturally mirroring `app.decide_job_offer_approval`'s own PLT-123 wrapper shape).

### ISS-2026-074 — Overtime and Timesheet (HRT-281): two competing, both self-declared "canonical forward contract" sources for regular payroll time — `app.attendance_sessions` (HRT-278) vs. `app.timesheet_entries` (HRT-281) — never reconciled, and the disclosed bridging mechanism between them is dead code (RESOLVED at HRT-282, was OPEN High)

Discovered `2026-08-13` during the Tier C batch adversarial review for `CG-S12-HRT-009` (Prompt 281, Overtime and Timesheet), CONFIRMED by direct code read against the full, already-applied migration set (198 migrations) — the cross-prompt integration lens's own finding, independently re-verified rather than accepted from its citation alone. `app.attendance_sessions` (HRT-278, `supabase/migrations/20260730900000_create_hris_attendance.sql:337-339`) carries its own `payroll_input_status`/`payroll_approved_by`/`payroll_approved_at`, populated by `app.approve_attendance_for_payroll_input` (`:1436-1495`), whose own comment states explicitly: "the forward contract Prompt 282 (Payroll) is expected to consume." HRT-281's own migration header makes the IDENTICAL, unqualified claim about a completely different table: `app.payroll_time_inputs` is "the one versioned payroll-input handoff this checkpoint emits" (`supabase/migrations/20260730980000_create_hris_overtime_timesheet.sql:6-7`). `payroll_time_inputs.regular_minutes` is computed exclusively via `app._compute_timesheet_period_summary` as `sum(timesheet_entries.approved_minutes) where status='approved'` — purely from `app.timesheet_entries` rows, which are only ever created with `source in ('manual','import')` (grep-confirmed against every call site; `source='attendance_derived'` is accepted by the table's own CHECK constraint and validation branch but is never actually produced by any function, batch job, or UI path anywhere in the migration, the UI route trees, or `server/mutations`/`server/queries`). `app._reconcile_timesheet_day`, the one function that touches both tables, only annotates EXISTING `timesheet_entries` rows with `reconciliation_status` — it never creates one from an `attendance_sessions` row. Consequence: an employee whose only time-tracking activity is Attendance clock-in/out (with sessions correctly `payroll_input_status='approved'` via HRT-278's own governed flow) contributes ZERO `regular_minutes` to `payroll_time_inputs` unless someone separately, manually re-enters the same hours as a `timesheet_entries` row for every workday. Neither HRT-278's nor HRT-281's own build log or migration header acknowledges the other capability's competing, equally-absolute "the forward contract" framing.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this batch's Tier C review-round fix pass — resolving this requires an architectural decision this single-prompt batch has no mandate to make: either (a) implement the disclosed-but-dead `attendance_derived` path so `app.payroll_time_inputs` genuinely derives regular time from `app.attendance_sessions.payroll_input_status='approved'` when no timesheet entry exists for that workday (a real new capability addition, not a bounded defect repair — a "which source wins, and how is double-counting prevented when both exist for the same workday" question, needing its own design and test coverage), or (b) explicitly document in both `HRT-278.md` and `HRT-281.md` which of the two surfaces `HRT-282` (Payroll) must treat as canonical for which slice of time, with a mechanism preventing double-counting. **Status `OPEN`**, High severity (HRT-282, the very next prompt in sequence, is left to guess whether it should read one source, the other, or both — with a real over/under-payment risk if it naively reads both without a de-duplication rule) — owner: HRT-282's (Prompt 282, Payroll) own implementation MUST resolve this before it can honestly claim to consume "the" time-input contract; if HRT-282 is authorized before this is resolved, its own Tier B/C review must treat "which of app.attendance_sessions vs. app.payroll_time_inputs is canonical, and how is double-counting prevented" as a mandatory, non-deferrable design question, not an inherited ambiguity to silently pick one side of.

**Resolution (HRT-282, `2026-08-13`): option (a), implemented and live-proven, not merely documented.** `app._resolve_payroll_time_inputs_for_period` (`supabase/migrations/20260731000000_create_hris_payroll_foundation.sql`) reads BOTH sources for one (tenant, employee, payroll period date range) with an explicit, per-work-date precedence: (1) every `app.payroll_time_inputs` row with `status='active'` whose own `app.timesheet_periods` window falls inside the payroll period contributes its own `regular_minutes`/`overtime_*_minutes` as-is, and its `source_entry_ids` (joined back to `app.timesheet_entries`) mark exactly which work_dates are already covered; (2) for every OTHER work_date in the payroll period's range NOT covered by step 1, an `app.attendance_sessions` row for that SAME work_date with `payroll_input_status='approved'` contributes its own session length to REGULAR minutes ONLY, never overtime (overtime classification structurally requires HRT-281's own governed overtime-request approval). A work_date therefore contributes through exactly one source, never both — structurally, not merely by convention, since step 2's own query excludes every work_date step 1 already covered. **Live-proven** in `scripts/db-tests/hris-payroll.sql`: an employee with ZERO `app.timesheet_entries` rows, only a real, HR-approved `app.attendance_sessions` clock-in/out (8 hours), correctly produced `regular_minutes=480` in the frozen input snapshot, which then correctly fed an `hourly_rate` payroll component's gross-pay calculation end to end. Status `RESOLVED` — see `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-282.md` for the full decision record (this migration's own header carries the identical proof, decision 2).

### ISS-2026-075 — Overtime and Timesheet (HRT-281): `app.export_timesheet_entries` has no TS/UI wrapper — but this matches an identical, pre-existing, repo-wide pattern already present in every sibling HRIS capability, not a unique HRT-281 regression (OPEN, Low)

Discovered `2026-08-13` during the Tier C batch adversarial review for `CG-S12-HRT-009` (Prompt 281). The spec-compliance lens's own citation characterized this as a HIGH, undisclosed, HRT-281-specific gap ("built, granted to authenticated, live-tested at the SQL layer, zero TypeScript wrapper, zero caller anywhere"); independent re-verification confirms the underlying fact (grep-confirmed: `app.export_timesheet_entries`, granted to `authenticated, service_role`, has no wrapper in `server/queries/overtime-timesheet.ts`, the only one of 14 read RPCs left unwrapped) but corrects the severity/scope: `app.export_attendance_sessions` (HRT-278), `app.export_schedule_assignments` (HRT-279), and `app.export_leave_requests` (HRT-280) — all three already `COMPLETED`/`VERIFIED` sibling capabilities' own equally `authenticated`-granted export RPCs — have IDENTICALLY zero TS wrapper anywhere in `server/` (grep-confirmed against the whole `server/` tree). This is a consistent, repository-wide pattern across every HRIS capability built so far, not something HRT-281 introduced or shipped worse than precedent. The lens's companion finding that `app.validate_timesheet_import_row`/`app.commit_timesheet_import_job` also have no TS wrapper is likewise not anomalous: `app.validate_timesheet_import_row` is granted `service_role` ONLY (never `authenticated`), matching HRT-278's own identical `app.validate_attendance_device_import_row` grant exactly — by design, not oversight, staged-import row validation runs as a service-role/job-runner step in this repository's established architecture. `app.commit_attendance_device_import_job` (HRT-278, `authenticated`-granted) is ALSO unwrapped in `server/`, confirming the "real, tested, SQL-layer-only staged-import commit, no TS/UI wiring yet" shape spans the whole HRIS domain, not just this checkpoint.

**Handling:** Not fixed in this batch's review-round fix pass — building a TS wrapper for `export_timesheet_entries` alone (while its three sibling capabilities' identical gaps remain unfixed) would create a new, arbitrary inconsistency across this phase rather than resolve one; fixing all four export RPCs' TS-wrapper gap, plus the whole domain's "no staged-import commit UI anywhere in HRIS" gap, is a cross-capability sweep outside this single-prompt batch's allowed-files scope. **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (no security exposure — every one of these RPCs enforces its own `HRS:Export`/`HRS:Import`-equivalent authority server-side regardless of caller; this is a missing UI/service-layer convenience feature, not a control gap) — owner: a dedicated near-term Phase 7 follow-up prompt scoped to "wire bulk export and staged-import commit into the TS/UI layer across Attendance, Shift-Roster, Leave, and Overtime-Timesheet uniformly," so the fix lands once, consistently, rather than piecemeal per capability.

### ISS-2026-076 — Overtime and Timesheet (HRT-281): five mutation wrappers (HR-on-behalf create ×2, draft edit, explicit reconcile, per-employee payroll generate) exist in the TS service layer with zero caller from any Server Action, page, or panel (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-13` during the Tier C batch adversarial review for `CG-S12-HRT-009` (Prompt 281), CONFIRMED by direct code read: `createOvertimeRequestForEmployee`, `createTimesheetEntryForEmployee`, `updateTimesheetEntryDraft`, `reconcileOvertimeRequestActual`, and the singular, per-employee `generatePayrollTimeInput` (distinct from the plural `generatePayrollTimeInputsForPeriod`, which IS wired into the admin panel) all exist as real, typed, tested wrapper functions in `server/mutations/overtime-timesheet.ts`, but grep-confirmed zero occurrences of any of the five names across `app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/hris/overtime-timesheet*` or `.../hris/my/overtime-timesheet*` (every `actions.ts` and `*panel.tsx` file). Direct read of `overtime-timesheet-admin-panel.tsx` confirms the admin workspace offers decide/lock/reopen/approve/reject/reopen-summary/generate-bulk-payroll-inputs only — no "create on behalf of employee" form or "edit a draft entry" form exists anywhere; HR's only lever over an employee's own draft data today is to approve/reject a row the employee themselves already created, or cancel-and-recreate rather than in-place-correct a fixable data-entry mistake.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this review-round fix pass — this is the identical shape as `HRT-280`'s own disclosed, accepted residual gap (six leave-domain mutation wrappers with zero UI caller: `createLeaveRequestForEmployee`, `updateLeaveRequestDraft`, `loadOpeningLeaveBalance`, `runLeaveAccrualBatch`, `runLeaveCarryForwardBatch`, `approveLeaveForPayrollInput`), which this repository's own established convention treats as a legitimate disclosed gap rather than a defect requiring an in-pass UI build-out (a real UI form for HR-on-behalf entry and draft-correction is a genuine, non-trivial UI feature, not a bounded review-fix). **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (no security exposure — every wrapper correctly enforces server-side authority via its underlying RPC regardless of whether a UI caller exists yet; the gap is that HR has no in-app lever for on-behalf entry or draft correction beyond what already exists) — owner: whichever near-term Phase 7 follow-up prompt builds out the admin workspace's remaining HR-on-behalf/draft-correction forms, most naturally paired with `ISS-2026-075`'s own bulk-export/import UI work since both land in the same `overtime-timesheet-admin-panel.tsx` surface.

### ISS-2026-077 — `scripts/db-tests/hris-leave-permit-business-trip.sql` (HRT-280) fails on the repository's own current wall-clock date, aborting the entire `pnpm run db:test` suite before it ever reaches any test file sorted after it alphabetically (OPEN, Medium — blocks the shared db:test gate for every future capability, not just HRT-282)

Discovered `2026-08-13` during HRT-282's Tier C batch-close gate re-run (`docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-282.md`). `pnpm run db:test` fails inside `hris-leave-permit-business-trip.sql`'s own `>> HRT-278 integration ... an employee with NO leave coverage still gets a real, undisturbed late exception` assertion block (line ~622): `assertion failed: expected the NOT-on-leave employee to have a real late exception (negative control), found 0`. **CONFIRMED pre-existing and unrelated to the HRT-282 diff** — reproduced identically with `supabase/migrations/20260731020000_harden_hris_payroll_batch_282_tier_c_review_fixes.sql` (this checkpoint's own corrective migration) removed from the tree entirely, i.e. against the exact `COMPLETED` state of the branch this batch inherited. Because `scripts/db-tests/run.sh` aborts the whole suite on the first `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1` failure and globs test files in alphabetical order, `hris-leave-permit-business-trip.sql` (sorts before `hris-payroll.sql`) blocks the harness from ever reaching `hris-payroll.sql` or any test file alphabetically after it — this is a standing, repository-wide blocker on the shared `db:test` gate for every future prompt, not scoped to HRT-280 or HRT-282 alone.

Not root-caused in depth (`hris-leave-permit-business-trip.sql` and its own migration are HRT-280's territory, already `VERIFIED`, and out of this batch's authorized allowed-files scope — see `AGENTS.md` "work on one authorized task at a time"), but the shape is visible from the fixture itself: the assertion's own "negative control" employee (`emp3`) is never given a `app.schedule_assignments` row anywhere in the fixture (only `mgr1`/`emp2`/`emp1` are, via `app.assign_employee_schedule(..., current_date, ...)` at lines 179-181, all keyed off `current_date` at fixture-setup time), while the coverage-threshold scenario earlier in the same file explicitly derives its own requirement from `extract(dow from current_date)`. Whether the "late exception" detection path for a clock-in genuinely depends on the employee holding a same-day `schedule_assignments` row (in which case `emp3`'s negative-control clock-in was never going to raise a late exception, on any date, and the assertion is simply wrong) or on some other `current_date`-relative condition that has drifted since this fixture was authored is exactly the "an unverified finding is not a defect — re-derive first" question `RECURRING_DEFECT_TAXONOMY.md` §5 warns against answering from citation alone; a dedicated investigation against HRT-280's own migration is the correct next step, not a guess folded into this unrelated batch's fix pass.

**Verification performed for THIS batch's own gate-closure purposes:** a scratch, non-committed variant of `scripts/db-tests/run.sh` that continues past a per-file failure (kept only in scratchpad, never committed) confirmed every OTHER test file — including `hris-payroll.sql` and every file sorted after `hris-leave-permit-business-trip.sql` — passes clean against the full, fresh migration set including this checkpoint's own `20260731020000`. Full detail and exact pass/fail counts: `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-282.md` §Tier C gate results.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this batch (out of its authorized scope — HRT-280's own already-`VERIFIED` migration/test file). **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (test-suite-only; no security/data-integrity exposure — this is a fixture assertion in a test file, not a production code path; the safe workaround used above, running test files individually/continuing past one failure, is available to any session that needs a real `db:test` signal in the meantime) — owner: a dedicated near-term maintenance prompt scoped to "diagnose and fix `hris-leave-permit-business-trip.sql`'s date-relative late-exception negative control," since this currently blocks the shared, mandatory `db:test` gate for every prompt from this point forward, not just HRIS ones.

**Re-confirmed `2026-08-14` during the `CG-S12-HRT-011/012/013` (Prompts 283-285) Tier C batch-close A/B check, root cause now precisely identified rather than merely guessed at.** On today's wall-clock date, `hris-leave-permit-business-trip.sql` run standalone **PASSES cleanly (exit 0)** — the exact opposite of the `2026-08-13` observation this entry originally recorded, run against two separately built, fresh disposable databases: one with `supabase/migrations/{20260731030000,20260731040000,20260731050000}` (this batch's own migrations) removed from the tree entirely, one with them present. **Both legs behave identically (both pass today)** — the required A/B property (this bug's presence/absence has nothing to do with the 283-285 diff) holds exactly as it did for HRT-282's own A/B check, just with the opposite pass/fail direction than that earlier snapshot.
