This is not a contradiction: it CONFIRMS the entry's own prior hypothesis that the negative control is genuinely wall-clock-date-relative (not merely "current wall-clock date" vaguely, but literally day-of-week-relative) — `app.set_schedule_coverage_requirement`'s own `extract(dow from current_date)` call three lines above the schedule-assignment calls that seed `mgr1`/`emp2`/`emp1` (never `emp3`, the negative control) with a same-day `schedule_assignments` row ties the whole scenario's shape to which day of the week the suite happens to run on. The bug is real and still unfixed — a fixture whose own pass/fail flips between two different real dates one day apart is not a reliable Tier A gate regardless of which direction it happens to point today — but the exact reproduction command in this entry's own `2026-08-13` text should not be expected to reproduce on every date; a future maintainer diagnosing this should force `current_date` (e.g. via `SET TimeZone`/session-level date override, or, more robustly, parameterizing the fixture's own schedule-assignment day-of-week rather than deriving it from the literal run date) rather than assume a clean `db:test` run means this is resolved. **Status remains `OPEN`**, Medium — unrelated to the 283-285 diff, confirmed a second time by independent A/B reproduction, not merely re-asserted from citation.

### ISS-2026-078 — Payroll Foundation (HRT-282): `app._resolve_payroll_time_inputs_for_period`'s original full-timesheet-period-containment gate silently dropped a boundary-straddling week's worth of real, HR-approved time from payroll (RESOLVED at HRT-282 Tier C batch review)

Discovered `2026-08-13` during the Tier C batch adversarial review for `CG-S12-HRT-010` (Prompt 282), integration lens Finding 1, CONFIRMED live: a weekly `app.timesheet_periods` row straddling a monthly payroll period's boundary (e.g. Jul 27 – Aug 2 against an Aug 1–31 payroll period — a routine, ordinary configuration, not a contrived edge case) caused its ENTIRE `app.payroll_time_inputs` row's minutes to be excluded from the frozen input snapshot, even for work_dates genuinely inside the payroll period and genuinely HR-approved. Live-reproduced: a real, approved 480-minute timesheet entry for a work_date inside the payroll period produced `regular_minutes=0` in the frozen snapshot, with zero error/exception/audit trace. **Resolution:** `app._resolve_payroll_time_inputs_for_period` was rewritten (migration `20260731020000`) to compute contribution per work_date from the underlying `app.timesheet_entries`/`app.overtime_requests` rows a `payroll_time_inputs` row's own `source_entry_ids`/`source_overtime_request_ids` reference, scoped to `[period_start, period_end]`, rather than gating on full timesheet-period containment. Live-reproduced as fixed: the identical scenario now correctly produces `regular_minutes=480`. Status `RESOLVED`, closed in the same checkpoint it was found in.

### ISS-2026-079 — Payroll Foundation (HRT-282): `app.calculate_payroll_run`'s claimed multi-transaction crash-resumability (`app.claim_next_job`/`app.record_job_failure`) is not real — the whole job lifecycle runs inside one function invocation/transaction (OPEN, Medium — architectural, requires a design decision outside this batch's mandate)

Discovered `2026-08-13` during the Tier C batch adversarial review for `CG-S12-HRT-010` (Prompt 282), correctness lens Finding 3, CONFIRMED by direct code read and by live concurrent-session observation: `app.calculate_payroll_run` calls `app.enqueue_job`/`app.heartbeat_job`/`app.complete_job` (and, on cancellation, `app.acknowledge_job_cancellation`) but never `app.claim_next_job` or `app.record_job_failure` — PLT-131/132's own actual multi-worker-safe claim and backoff/dead-letter mechanisms, grep-confirmed zero occurrences anywhere in this checkpoint. The entire lifecycle runs inside ONE PL/pgSQL `FUNCTION` invocation (not a `PROCEDURE`), i.e. one transaction from the caller's perspective, so it structurally cannot commit incrementally. This checkpoint's ORIGINAL migration comment and build log overclaimed genuine durability ("a real chunk-boundary heartbeat... + a `record_job_failure`-compatible dead-letter path, all reusing PLT-131/132 unchanged") — corrected in migration `20260731020000`'s updated function comment. A crash mid-calculation is NOT a data-integrity risk (full atomic rollback — zero corruption, zero partial/dirty payroll data ever observable, live-confirmed by direct session observation during an in-flight calculation), but it is not a genuine checkpoint/resume either — the caller must re-invoke the whole batch from scratch, with zero durable trace of the interrupted attempt for any DLQ/retry/monitoring surface to pick up.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this batch — a genuine multi-transaction, crash-resumable redesign (turning a single synchronous RPC call into a true background-worker-polled, incrementally-committing chunked execution, with a considered answer to "what does a partially-committed payroll calculation mean for the finalized-history-never-recalculates business rule") is a substantial architectural change requiring its own design decision, not a bounded review-round fix. The narrower, genuinely fixable part of the same finding (the mid-loop cancellation check being unreachable dead code) IS fixed in this same batch — see `ISS-2026-080`/correctness lens Finding 4 disposition in `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-282.md`. **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (no data-integrity or security exposure, confirmed rollback-safe; the gap is operational/scalability — a very large tenant's payroll calculation has no genuine mid-batch durability) — owner: a dedicated Phase 7 (or platform-wide PLT-131/132 follow-up) prompt scoped to "give `app.calculate_payroll_run` (and any sibling synchronous-inline job pattern — `roster_generation`, `leave_accrual`/`leave_carry_forward`, all confirmed to share this exact shape by the spec-compliance lens) genuine multi-transaction resumability," landing once, consistently, rather than piecemeal per capability.

### ISS-2026-080 — Payroll Foundation (HRT-282): `app.request_payroll_run_calculation_cancellation` (the Tier C fix making `calculate_payroll_run`'s mid-loop cancellation check genuinely reachable) has a real, tested SQL/TS wrapper but no UI caller yet (OPEN, Low)

Added `2026-08-13` alongside its own fix, migration `20260731020000` (correctness lens Finding 4). Before this checkpoint's Tier C review, `app.calculate_payroll_run`'s own mid-loop `status = 'cancelling'` check was unreachable dead code — grep-confirmed zero occurrences anywhere in this repository of anything setting a `payroll_calculation`-type job to `'cancelling'` (the only function that ever sets that status, `app.cancel_import_export_job`, is import/export-scoped under a different authority). Fixed with a new, correctly `HRS:Edit`-scoped RPC, `app.request_payroll_run_calculation_cancellation`, live-verified to (a) correctly reject a non-`calculating` run, (b) correctly reject an actor without `HRS:Edit`, and (c) correctly flip a genuinely in-progress job to `cancelling` — which the ALREADY-EXISTING, already-live-tested chunk-boundary check inside `calculate_payroll_run` then genuinely observes (Postgres READ COMMITTED semantics: a fresh `SELECT` inside the same already-in-flight invocation sees an externally-committed status change). A typed TS wrapper (`requestPayrollRunCalculationCancellation`, `server/mutations/payroll.ts`) exists and is tested; no UI form/button exists yet in `hris/payroll/payroll-admin-panel.tsx`.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this batch — matches this checkpoint's own already-established, accepted "wrapper with no UI caller yet" disclosure shape (`archive_payroll_component_version`, `end_payroll_component_assignment`, `attach_payroll_component_version_evidence`, `cancel_payroll_loan`, and `create_payroll_reimbursement_request_for_employee` — see `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-282.md` §10/Tier C corrected disclosure list). **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (no security exposure — the RPC enforces its own authority server-side regardless of caller; the scenario it serves, a separate session observing a long-running calculation stuck in `status=calculating` and wanting to stop it, is real but narrow) — owner: whichever near-term Phase 7 follow-up prompt builds out the admin workspace's remaining disclosed-gap forms.

### ISS-2026-081 — Payroll Foundation (HRT-282): the checkpoint's own C-20 disclosure list (build log §10) named `cancel_payroll_run` as having no UI caller when it IS wired, and omitted `app.reopen_payroll_period_inputs` and `app.create_payroll_reimbursement_request_for_employee`, which genuinely had none (RESOLVED — corrected at HRT-282 Tier C batch review)

Discovered `2026-08-13` during the Tier C batch adversarial review for `CG-S12-HRT-010` (Prompt 282), spec-compliance lens Finding 1 (also independently surfaced by the integration lens), CONFIRMED by direct code read: `cancel_payroll_run` is genuinely wired end-to-end (`hris/payroll/actions.ts:cancelPayrollRunAction`, bound to a live "Cancel run" form in `payroll-admin-panel.tsx`) and should never have appeared on the "no UI caller" list; `app.reopen_payroll_period_inputs` (by the migration's own comment, the ONLY path back from `input_frozen` to `open`) and `app.create_payroll_reimbursement_request_for_employee` (the HR-on-behalf reimbursement create) both genuinely had zero UI caller anywhere and were missing from the list entirely. This mattered beyond paperwork accuracy for `reopen_payroll_period_inputs` specifically: an HR admin who freezes a period and then finds a data error had no in-app way to correct it.

**Resolution:** the disclosure list is corrected in `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-282.md` (this checkpoint's own Tier C batch-review section); `app.reopen_payroll_period_inputs` is now wired end-to-end (`reopenPayrollPeriodInputsAction`, a "Reopen inputs" form shown on any `input_frozen` period in `payroll-admin-panel.tsx`) as part of this same Tier C fix pass, since it pairs naturally with the freeze-guard fix (correctness lens Finding 2) that makes reopening the only governed way to unwind a period once a run has advanced past draft. `app.create_payroll_reimbursement_request_for_employee` remains genuinely disclosed, not fixed — matches this repository's own established, repeatedly-accepted "HR-on-behalf create with no UI form yet" precedent shape (`HRT-280`'s `createLeaveRequestForEmployee`, `HRT-281`'s `createOvertimeRequestForEmployee`/`createTimesheetEntryForEmployee`, `ISS-2026-076`), and building a real UI form for it is a genuine, non-trivial UI feature, not a bounded review-fix. Status `RESOLVED` for the disclosure-accuracy defect and the `reopen_payroll_period_inputs` UI gap; `create_payroll_reimbursement_request_for_employee`'s own no-UI-caller gap remains a disclosed, accepted residual (tracked here, not a separate issue number, matching the sibling wrappers it groups with).

### ISS-2026-082 — Payroll Foundation (HRT-282): `server/contracts/payroll/payroll.ts`'s own header comment overclaimed its shared `money` schema's INPUT guarantee ("never coerced to a JS number") when the schema itself accepts either a string or a number and only normalizes on OUTPUT (RESOLVED at HRT-282 Tier C batch review)

Discovered `2026-08-13` during the Tier C batch adversarial review for `CG-S12-HRT-010` (Prompt 282), spec-compliance lens Finding 2, CONFIRMED by direct code read: `const money = z.union([z.string(), z.number()]).transform((v) => String(v))` (`server/contracts/payroll/payroll.ts:74`) explicitly accepts a raw JS `number` as valid input; the file's own header comment claimed money fields are "a decimal string on the wire... never coerced to a JS `number`," which reads as an input-side guarantee the schema does not enforce (it IS a genuine output-side guarantee — every parsed value is unconditionally `String()`-normalized, so a caller can never observe a raw JS `number` from a parsed payroll row). No live precision failure found or reproduced — typical payroll amounts round-trip exactly through a JS double, and this schema is in fact stricter than the repository-wide convention elsewhere (`server/contracts/invoice/invoice.ts`, `vendor-bill.ts`, `journal.ts` all use plain `z.number()` for money fields, unmasked by any string-coercion step at all). **Resolution:** corrected the header comment to distinguish the output-side guarantee (real, enforced) from the input-side leniency (deliberate, defensive) — see `server/contracts/payroll/payroll.ts`'s updated header. Status `RESOLVED`, documentation-accuracy only, no code-behavior change.

### ISS-2026-083 — Training and Talent (HRT-284): "provider/certificate files are private and malware-scanned" (§16) was only ever built for the certificate half — `training_providers` carries no evidence-file column or attach RPC at all (OPEN, Low)

Discovered `2026-08-14` during the Tier C batch adversarial review for `CG-S12-HRT-011/012/013` (Prompts 283-285), spec-compliance lens finding 5, CONFIRMED by direct schema read: `app.training_providers` (`supabase/migrations/20260731040000_create_hris_training_talent.sql:326-342`) has no `evidence_file_id` column and no `attach_training_provider_evidence`-shaped RPC anywhere in the migration (grep-confirmed). Only `app.training_certificates.evidence_file_id` + `app.attach_training_certificate_evidence` (reusing PLT-128's malware-scan/tenant/record-type re-validation pattern) exist, and that half is now genuinely reachable end-to-end (see the batch's own Tier C fix pass, which wired a UI "Attach evidence" form for certificates and corrected the Certificates section's own description text to stop claiming provider evidence exists). §16's own phrasing names both nouns ("provider/certificate files"); the migration's header comment ("certificate/provider evidence reuses... PLT-128") likewise overstated what was actually built.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this batch — adding a genuinely new provider-evidence-file capability (a new column, a new PLT-128-reusing RPC, RLS/grant wiring, a TS wrapper, and a UI form, mirroring the certificate half exactly) is a real, bounded feature addition, not a defect repair; §13's own database-impact list never separately named a persisted provider-evidence record, so this may be reading more into §16's phrasing than the section intended. The narrower, immediately actionable half of this finding — the UI/migration-comment text overclaiming provider coverage — IS corrected in this same batch (Certificates section description, `training-talent-panel.tsx`). **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (no security exposure — there is simply no provider-evidence-file feature to exploit or fail; the product now accurately describes only what it does) — owner: a near-term Training/Talent follow-up prompt scoped to "add provider evidence-file attach, mirroring the certificate PLT-128 pattern exactly," if this is confirmed to be in scope for a future checkpoint.

### ISS-2026-084 — Employee and Manager Self-Service (HRT-285): the MSS team roster (50) and per-category approval queue (20) are genuinely bounded, single-page composition reads with no "load more"/cursor-fetch UI, even after this batch's own Tier C fix added a visible truncation indicator (OPEN, Low)

Discovered `2026-08-14` during the Tier C batch adversarial review for `CG-S12-HRT-011/012/013` (Prompts 283-285), spec-compliance lens finding 3, CONFIRMED by direct code read: `server/queries/self-service.ts`'s `TEAM_LIST_BOUND` (50) and `TEAM_QUEUE_BOUND` (20) genuinely truncate `getMssTeamWorkspace`'s roster and each approval-queue category, and — before this batch's own Tier C fix — did so with NO caller-visible signal anywhere in `team-workspace-panel.tsx`, contradicting section 17's own "cursor team queues... no browser-loaded full dataset" framing (which implies the caller can tell when a boundary was hit, not merely that the server enforces one).

### ISS-2026-085 — Internal and Interdepartmental Ticket (HRT-286): "channel extensibility is a pure additive CHECK-widen, never a redesign" does not hold for Prompt 287 (customer) or 288 (helpdesk) — the requester/assignee identity shape is hard-wired to `app.employees` (`RESOLVED` IN FULL — BOTH the Prompt 287/customer half at `CG-S12-HRT-015` and the Prompt 288/helpdesk half at `CG-S12-HRT-016`, EACH independently Tier-C-adversarially-reviewed, with genuine findings surfaced and fixed at both reviews, not a rubber-stamp — see both resolution notes and both Tier C batch review addenda below)

Discovered `2026-08-13` during the Tier C batch adversarial review for `CG-S12-HRT-014` (Prompt 286), spec-compliance lens Finding 1 and independently, identically, the cross-prompt integration lens Finding 1. CONFIRMED by direct code read against the live schema: `app.tickets.requester_employee_id` is `uuid not null references app.employees (master_record_id)` (`supabase/migrations/20260731060000_create_ticketing_internal.sql:397`) — NOT NULL, FK'd exclusively to the employee workforce table. Every structural-authority decision point (`app.can_access_ticket`, `app.is_ticket_staff`, `app._ticket_transition_authority`, `app.create_ticket`/`reply_to_ticket`/`add_ticket_watcher`/`remove_ticket_watcher`) resolves identity exclusively through `app.get_self_employee`, an employee-only lookup — 8 call sites confirmed. Three read RPCs (`get_ticket`, `list_tickets`, `export_tickets`) `INNER JOIN app.employees` on the requester (assignee is correctly `LEFT JOIN`ed, since it's nullable). `app._create_ticket` — the "shared engine" the migration's own header (decision 1) and the completion build log both claim Prompt 287/288 will call "with a different `p_channel`" — has **no `p_channel` parameter at all**; its INSERT omits the `channel` column entirely and relies on the table's hardcoded `default 'internal'`. Confirmed directly against the actual `287_CUSTOMER_TICKET_PROMPT.md`/`288_CARGOGRID_HELPDESK_PROMPT.md` task briefs already in the repo: both require a genuinely new requester/assignee participant type (Layer 4 customer; CargoGrid support-session-grant-based staffing) that the current NOT NULL/FK/INNER JOIN/employee-only-authority shape cannot represent without relaxing the NOT NULL constraint, adding nullable discriminator column(s), converting those INNER JOINs to LEFT JOINs, and adding new branches to `can_access_ticket`/`is_ticket_staff`/`_ticket_transition_authority`.

One narrower part of the original claim IS accurate and well-precedented: the `channel` CHECK constraint itself is a trivial DROP/ADD (real, repeated precedent via `app.jobs.job_type`), and the RLS policy *objects* on `app.tickets`/`app.ticket_messages` (`using (app.can_access_ticket(id) ...)`) don't themselves need to change — only the functions they call do. So the required work is real but concentrated in the identity columns/joins/authority helpers, not a full redesign of the table or RLS layer. `app.ticket_queues` (tenant+org_unit scoped) also cannot represent a "Platform support queue" (CargoGrid support isn't an org unit of the customer's tenant) — Prompt 288 will need a parallel queue concept or a nullable-`org_unit_id`-plus-discriminator widen, not a bare CHECK change. `ticket_messages.author_role`'s closed `('requester','staff')` enum will similarly need a widen or redefinition for a non-employee, non-staff channel participant.

**No live exploit today** — only the `internal` channel is active, `channel` is the sole enum value, and every table/RPC/RLS policy is internally self-consistent for that one channel (independently verified clean by the security and correctness lenses). The exposure is entirely forward: a future session implementing Prompt 287/288 trusting this migration's own documented claim would attempt the described one-line CHECK-widen, discover it doesn't work, and lose real time rediscovering this exact gap mid-implementation.

**Handling:** Not fixed in this batch — the actual schema/authority rework is Prompt 287/288's own architectural decision to make (which representation for a non-employee principal: a generic `can_access_record`-style owner-scope parameterization, per integration lens Finding 2; new nullable columns; or something else), outside a single-prompt review-fix batch's mandate. Corrected here via accurate `comment on table app.tickets` / `comment on function app._create_ticket` object comments (`supabase/migrations/20260731070000_harden_ticketing_internal_batch_286_review_fixes.sql`) replacing the overclaiming language, so a future implementer reading the live schema (not just the original migration's now-stale header prose) sees the real scope up front. **Status `OPEN`**, High severity (foundation-severity: this is the first of six dependent Ticket-workstream prompts, and the corrected claim materially changes how Prompt 287/288 should be scoped/estimated) — owner: whichever session picks up Prompt 287 (Customer-to-Tenant Ticket), which per `docs/standards/BUILD_EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md` §3.2 trigger 6 may not begin until this checkpoint is `VERIFIED`, and must budget real expand-and-contract schema work, not a one-line constraint change.

**Resolution (Prompt 287, `CG-S12-HRT-015`, 2026-08-14) — the customer half only, live-verified, not merely reasoned about.** `supabase/migrations/20260731080000_extend_ticketing_customer_channel.sql` does the actual expand-and-contract work this entry named as still required: `app.tickets.requester_employee_id` relaxed from NOT NULL to nullable; a new nullable `requester_customer_account_id uuid references app.accounts (id)` column added; a structural `tickets_requester_identity_shape` CHECK enforces exactly one of the two, matching `channel`; the `channel` CHECK widened to `('internal','customer')`; `app._create_ticket` given a real `p_channel` parameter (old 10-arg overload explicitly `drop function`-ed, never left as a stale second overload) with per-channel identity validation; the three `INNER JOIN app.employees` reads this entry named (`get_ticket`/`list_tickets`/`export_tickets`) converted to `LEFT JOIN`, `coalesce`d against a new `LEFT JOIN app.accounts`. Per integration lens Finding 2's own suggestion (cited above), identity resolution was generalized rather than duplicated: a single new helper, `app._is_ticket_requester_party(ticket, auth_user_id)`, dispatches on `ticket.channel` (employee-match for `internal`, `app.resolve_customer_owner_account_scope`-membership-match for `customer`, ATW-242's own already-VERIFIED primitive) and replaces the inline `app.get_self_employee` check everywhere it previously appeared (`can_access_ticket`, `reply_to_ticket`, `add_ticket_watcher`/`remove_ticket_watcher`, `_ticket_transition_authority`) — never a fourth, ticket-specific access shape, and provably byte-for-byte unchanged for the `internal` channel (re-ran `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-internal.sql`'s full 15-assertion-group suite against the post-migration schema — identical pass, confirmed twice). A customer_user-layer actor's raw-table read access on `tickets`/`ticket_messages`/`ticket_watchers`/`ticket_events` is additionally closed entirely (RLS narrowed with `not app.actor_holds_customer_user_layer(...)`, mirroring the established `20260730311000` ATW-023 hardening precedent) — the only sanctioned customer read path is a new, dedicated, customer-safe RPC set (`get_customer_ticket`/`list_customer_tickets`/`list_customer_ticket_messages`/`list_customer_ticket_categories`/`list_customer_accounts_for_actor`), each its own explicit projection (no queue/assignee identity, internal notes structurally unreachable, staff author identity genericized to "Support Team"). Live-adversarially tested against a real disposable Postgres 16 database (`scripts/db-tests/ticketing-customer.sql`, 19 numbered assertion groups, two tenants and two customer accounts): forged/unowned account id rejected indistinguishably from a nonexistent one; cross-tenant account forgery rejected; a second account cannot read the first account's ticket; any active member of the SAME account can (account-level access, not per-individual); revoked `customer_user` membership loses access immediately (live query, no caching); a customer cannot force `visibility='internal'` through any path; close/reopen-as-configured (cancel + reopen) reached through the SAME generic `app.transition_ticket_status`, no new transition RPC; watcher management is staff-only for a customer-channel ticket; raw-table RLS admits zero rows to a `customer_user`-layer actor; an internal-channel ticket id is structurally unreachable through every customer RPC; a genuine two-process concurrent race on `create_customer_ticket`'s idempotency key collapses to one ticket (the new `tickets_idempotency_customer_unique` partial index, live-reproduced). Found and fixed one live C-02-class defect during this same adversarial pass (`get_customer_ticket`/`list_customer_ticket_messages` each had a bare `where id = p_ticket_id` colliding with their own `RETURNS TABLE(id ...)` OUT variable — fixed by aliasing, mirroring HRT-286's own identical defect class). **Genuinely NOT resolved, still `OPEN`**: the Prompt 288/helpdesk half (a CargoGrid-support-scoped, non-org-unit-bound assignee/queue concept) — this migration does not touch `app.ticket_queues`' `org_unit_id not null` shape or add any support-session-grant identity resolution; whichever session implements Prompt 288 still owns that separate expand-and-contract work, unblocked by (and free to reuse) this migration's own `p_channel`/`_is_ticket_requester_party` pattern as precedent. Owner going forward: whichever session picks up Prompt 288 (Tenant-to-CargoGrid Helpdesk).

**Tier C batch review (`CG-S12-HRT-015`, 2026-08-14) — the customer-half resolution above independently re-verified, not merely re-read.** The cross-prompt integration lens re-derived all 7 of this entry's own original verification questions live against a fresh disposable Postgres 16 database with the full migration set applied (not trusted from this entry's own prose): the NOT NULL relaxation and `tickets_requester_identity_shape` CHECK are real (`\d app.tickets`); `_create_ticket` has exactly one overload with a real `p_channel` reaching the INSERT (`pg_proc` query, live-created row confirmed `channel='customer'`); the three named `INNER JOIN`s are genuinely `LEFT JOIN`s, live-tested against 4 real customer auth users with zero matching `app.employees` rows (the exact scenario this entry said would be structurally unreadable is now readable); `can_access_ticket`/`_ticket_transition_authority` genuinely dispatch through the new `app._is_ticket_requester_party` helper; `app.resolve_customer_owner_account_scope`/`app.actor_holds_customer_user_layer` are reused, never redefined by this migration (grep-confirmed); no already-VERIFIED table's grants/RLS were inappropriately widened (`aclexplode` re-checked live); `ticketing-internal.sql` re-ran 15/15, byte-for-byte unchanged. **Status confirmed `RESOLVED` for the customer/Prompt-287 half — no residual gap in this entry's own original scope.** The same batch review separately found and fixed two HIGH defects that are NOT part of this entry's own scope (they are new spec/correctness gaps in the customer-channel build, not further instances of the "channel extensibility" architectural gap this entry tracks) — see `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-287.md` §14 for full detail: (1) `app.get_customer_ticket` returned `resolution_summary`/`cancelled_reason`/`last_reopen_reason` verbatim regardless of whether a staff member or the customer themself authored the text, leaking staff-internal rationale on ordinary resolve/cancel/reopen actions — fixed via `supabase/migrations/20260731090000_harden_ticketing_customer_channel_batch_287_review_fixes.sql`, which tracks per-transition authorship and only echoes customer-authored text back (resolution_summary, always staff-authored by construction, is now never exposed to the customer projection at all — see `ISS-2026-088`); (2) `app._create_ticket`'s customer-channel idempotency match (and the underlying `tickets_idempotency_customer_unique` partial index) omitted `requester_customer_account_id`, so a customer_user actor scoped to more than one account (decision 5/8, an explicitly supported scenario) who reused one idempotency key across two different-account requests with matching content got silently short-circuited to the first account's ticket, with the second request's own account never getting a ticket at all — fixed in the same migration by adding the account id to both the index and the match/replay logic in `app._create_ticket`. Both live-reproduced broken before the fix and live-reproduced correct after, per `BUILD_EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md` §5.3.

**Resolution (Prompt 288, `CG-S12-HRT-016`, 2026-08-14) — the helpdesk half, the LAST remaining gap this entry tracked, live-verified, not merely reasoned about.** `supabase/migrations/20260731100000_extend_ticketing_helpdesk_channel.sql` does the actual expand-and-contract work this entry named as still required for the third channel: `channel` CHECK widened to `('internal','customer','helpdesk')`; `tickets_requester_identity_shape` widened to a third branch (helpdesk requires NEITHER `requester_employee_id` NOR `requester_customer_account_id` — the requester is the tenant itself, not a specific employee/account row); `queue_id` relaxed to nullable with a new structural `tickets_queue_shape` CHECK and a genuinely PARALLEL, Platform-wide `app.support_queues` catalog (no `tenant_id`, no `org_unit_id` — HRT-286's own diagnosed gap, "a parallel queue concept, not a widened `app.ticket_queues` row," resolved exactly as named, not rediscovered); `app._create_ticket` widened again (explicit drop-then-recreate, now 16 parameters, adding `p_severity`/`p_product_area`/`p_environment`/`p_external_reference` and conditioning queue resolution on channel — skipped entirely for helpdesk). Identity resolution generalized further, not duplicated a third time: `app._is_ticket_requester_party` gains a THIRD branch dispatching to a new `app._is_tenant_helpdesk_authorized(tenant_id, auth_user_id)` helper (a real tenant_admin membership OR a real tenant-scoped, published `TKT:Edit` role grant — direct `app.principal_memberships`/`app.role_assignments` queries, deliberately employee-independent per this prompt's own explicit instruction, and deliberately NOT routed through `app.check_ticket_authority`/`app.evaluate_permission`, whose RPD-022 Supreme-Admin bypass would otherwise incorrectly let a Platform Supreme Admin count as "the tenant-side requester party" for a tenant they hold no real membership in — found and fixed during this migration's own design, live-adversarially reproduced and confirmed in `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-helpdesk.sql` test 4). `app.is_ticket_staff` hardened with an explicit early return for `channel = 'helpdesk'` (Supreme-Admin-only — the disclosed, bounded Platform-support-staff-role decision this prompt made, since no dedicated non-Supreme-Admin support-agent principal layer exists in this repository and `app.principal_memberships`' four layers are deliberately closed) — live-reproduced that WITHOUT this explicit exclusion, a tenant's own tenant-wide `TKT:Edit` holder (ISS-2026-086's own subject) would otherwise satisfy `is_ticket_staff` on their own tenant's helpdesk ticket and read Platform-internal notes on a case where they are structurally the requester, not staff; fixed and re-verified live (`scripts/db-tests/ticketing-helpdesk.sql` test 6). The three existing generic staff-lifecycle RPCs (`assign_ticket`/`transfer_ticket_queue`/`update_ticket_classification`) and `redact_ticket_message` were extended (not bypassed) to explicitly reject/restrict a helpdesk-channel ticket, with dedicated, Supreme-Admin-gated siblings (`assign_helpdesk_ticket`/`transfer_helpdesk_support_queue`/`update_helpdesk_ticket_classification`) built instead — never a shared RPC silently branching two different authority models together. `get_ticket`/`list_tickets`/`list_ticket_messages`/`list_ticket_watchers`/`list_ticket_events` hardened with a PER-TICKET-CHANNEL exclusion (not a per-actor-layer one, since the same `org_user`/`tenant_admin` actor legitimately needs the staff projection for their tenant's own internal tickets but never for a helpdesk case where they are the requester side) — a helpdesk ticket is reachable through these staff RPCs by `app.is_supreme_admin(actor)` only; `get_ticket`/`list_tickets`' own `app.ticket_queues` JOIN converted from INNER to LEFT (queue_id is now nullable) so Supreme Admin genuinely still sees a helpdesk row through them, live-confirmed (`scripts/db-tests/ticketing-helpdesk.sql` test 8) rather than silently excluded the way HRT-287's own employees-INNER-JOIN gap would have. Raw-table RLS on `tickets`/`ticket_messages`/`ticket_watchers`/`ticket_events` additionally excludes a helpdesk-channel ticket from every non-Supreme-Admin actor (a new `app.ticket_channel_of` helper feeds the three tables with no channel column of their own) — mirrors the ATW-023/HRT-287 hardening precedent exactly, but keyed on ticket channel rather than actor layer. The support-access CORRELATION-NOT-ACCESS-GRANT guarantee (Prompt 288's own most security-sensitive requirement, this task correlating with PLT-115, the single most security-sensitive primitive in this repository) is proven live, not merely asserted: `app.link_helpdesk_support_grant` (Supreme-Admin-gated) validates a real `app.support_access_grants` row exists for `(tenant_id, case_id)` before accepting a correlation reference (a forged reference rejected with `support_grant_not_found`) but never creates/approves/starts/extends/revokes a grant or session itself; a REAL grant, subsequently REVOKED via the existing, unmodified `app.revoke_support_access` kill switch, was still successfully linked for display (`scripts/db-tests/ticketing-helpdesk.sql` test 12) — proving correlation display never requires a currently-live grant; and an identity holding an ACTIVE, approved, unexpired support-access grant into the ticket's own tenant was live-confirmed to hold ZERO `is_ticket_staff`/`can_access_ticket` status on the correlated helpdesk ticket purely from that grant, both before AND after the correlation was linked — the ticket and the grant remain two structurally independent mechanisms, exactly as Prompt 288's own business rule requires. **Status `RESOLVED` for the helpdesk/Prompt-288 half at implementation time** — live-adversarially proven via `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-helpdesk.sql`'s 16 numbered assertion groups against a real disposable Postgres 16 database, with `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-internal.sql` (15/15) and `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-customer.sql` (19/19) both re-confirmed passing byte-for-byte against the post-migration schema — zero regression to either prior channel. **Independently Tier-C-reviewed at the `CG-S12-HRT-016` batch review below — see the addendum immediately following this paragraph for the four genuine findings surfaced, fixed, and re-verified live.** Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-288.md`.

**Tier C batch review (`CG-S12-HRT-016`, 2026-08-14) — the helpdesk-half resolution above independently re-verified, not merely re-read.** Four adversarial lenses (spec-compliance; security/RLS/tenant-isolation with a specific mandate for heavy PLT-115-correlation scrutiny; correctness/concurrency; cross-prompt integration with a specific mandate to verify this entry's FULL closure across both halves) ran against commit `e9185f2`, each against a real, freshly-built disposable Postgres 16 database. Per this batch's own explicit priority instruction, the two properties this whole entry's helpdesk resolution most depends on — "a support ticket alone grants no tenant business-data access" and "an expired/revoked support access grant, even correlated to a ticket, confers nothing" — were re-derived FIRST, independently of any lens's own disposition: three real `app.support_access_grants` rows (active/unexpired, expired, and revoked-after-approval) were created for the same non-Supreme-Admin identity against the same tenant, two correlated to real helpdesk tickets via `app.link_helpdesk_support_grant`, and `app.is_ticket_staff`/`app.can_access_ticket`/raw-table RLS/`app.get_platform_helpdesk_ticket` directly probed for that identity in every combination — all returned `false`/zero rows/access-denied in every case. **No fix was needed for either property; both independently re-confirmed to hold.** Four genuine findings WERE surfaced elsewhere and fixed at root cause via a new additive migration (`supabase/migrations/20260731110000_harden_ticketing_helpdesk_batch_288_review_fixes.sql`): (1) HIGH, correctness — `app.reply_to_ticket`'s idempotency replay match omitted the posting actor, silently collapsing two different, independently-authorized participants' distinct replies (on the same ticket, reusing one idempotency key by coincidence) into a single row, discarding the second caller's genuine reply with no error; live-reproduced on the helpdesk channel via this checkpoint's own new `reply_to_helpdesk_ticket`, and independently re-reproduced on the pre-existing customer channel, proving one shared root cause across all three ticket channels HRT-288 inherited unfixed on its own new surface — fixed once, at the single shared function, closing all three simultaneously; (2) MEDIUM, spec-compliance — `app.add_ticket_watcher`/`app.remove_ticket_watcher` were the one staff-lifecycle RPC pairing this checkpoint's own "generic RPCs explicitly reject a helpdesk ticket" design discipline did not extend to, letting an ordinary tenant admin insert a real employee-participant watcher row on a channel whose whole design principle (decision 4) is that no employee is ever a participant — live-reproduced (a tkh1 tenant_admin successfully added a real employee watcher to a real helpdesk ticket), confirmed inert for confidentiality today only because every downstream consumer independently re-excludes the helpdesk channel rather than trusting `can_access_ticket` alone, but a genuine, previously-untested crack in that one predicate nonetheless — now fixed with the identical explicit channel guard the three sibling RPCs already carried; (3) MEDIUM, security — `app.ticket_channel_of`, added purely as RLS-policy support, was granted EXECUTE to `authenticated` with zero scoping of its own, making it a directly-callable, unscoped cross-tenant ticket-existence-and-channel oracle for any authenticated identity regardless of relationship to the ticket — live-reproduced (an identity with zero relationship to the tenant or ticket anywhere in the system called it directly and learned both the ticket's existence and its channel), directly contradicting the anti-enumeration discipline this same migration establishes everywhere else — now fixed to return `NULL` (indistinguishable from a nonexistent ticket) unless the caller already passes `app.can_access_ticket` or holds Supreme Admin, provably behavior-preserving for every existing RLS caller; (4) LOW, spec-compliance — `app.list_platform_helpdesk_tickets` had no `p_product_area` filter parameter or supporting index despite the prompt's own §17 naming it — now added (index plus a trailing, defaulted parameter, backward compatible; `server/queries/ticketing.ts` updated to pass it through). All four re-verified live post-fix (broken-before, correct-after, real forged-session probes), with zero regression confirmed via byte-identical re-runs of `ticketing-internal.sql` (15/15), `ticketing-customer.sql` (19/19), `ticketing-helpdesk.sql` (16/16), `rbac-enforcement.sql`, and a full fresh `pnpm run db:test` official-harness pass (`ALL PASSED`, all 211 migrations, all 375 test files, no `ISS-2026-059` time-of-day fallback needed this session). **`ISS-2026-085`'s final disposition: `RESOLVED` IN FULL.** Both halves are now closed, and BOTH have been independently Tier-C-adversarially-reviewed with genuine findings surfaced, fixed at root cause, and re-verified live — this entry's own original scope is fully closed, no residual gap remains. Full disposition table, propagation sweep, and every gate's exact output: `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-288.md` §13.

### ISS-2026-086 — Internal and Interdepartmental Ticket (HRT-286): `TKT:Edit` grants blanket, tenant-wide, non-queue-scoped ticket-content staff status via `app.is_ticket_staff`, in tension with the migration's own "reserved for configuration, not ordinary ticket work" design claim (OPEN, Medium — deliberate design tension, not a clear-cut bug)

Discovered `2026-08-13` during the Tier C batch adversarial review for `CG-S12-HRT-014` (Prompt 286), spec-compliance lens Finding 2, CONFIRMED by direct code read: `app.is_ticket_staff` (`supabase/migrations/20260731060000_create_ticketing_internal.sql:607`) grants full staff status — read every internal note, post replies/internal notes, transfer queue, reclassify — to any holder of `TKT:Edit` on **every ticket in the tenant, regardless of queue**, with no per-queue record and no independent revocation path from the queue/category-configuration authority the same permission also grants. This contradicts the migration's own decision-5 header comment ("`TKT:Edit` is reserved for QUEUE/CATEGORY CONFIGURATION and on-behalf ticket creation... not for ordinary ticket work").

**Investigated, not a simple deletion fix:** `app.redact_ticket_message` (a genuinely content-destructive action) deliberately gates on `TKT:Edit` directly, independent of `is_ticket_staff`, with its own comment explicitly holding redaction "to the same bar as queue/category configuration" — meaning TKT:Edit-as-tenant-wide-content-override IS intentional elsewhere in this same migration. The db-test fixture itself (`scripts/db-tests/ticketing-internal.sql:593`) relies on `staff1`'s `TKT:Edit` to post an internal reply to a ticket **after** a queue transfer left `staff1` without explicit membership on the new queue — live-confirmed removing the `is_ticket_staff` TKT:Edit branch breaks this already-passing, intentional coverage. Whether `TKT:Edit` should function as a blanket tenant-wide ticket-content override (as `redact_ticket_message` already assumes) or be split into a narrower config-only permission plus a separate, explicitly-revocable "service admin" grant is a real access-control policy decision.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this batch — changing enforcement here risks breaking an apparently-intentional admin escape hatch without a clear mandate to redesign it. Corrected via an accurate `comment on function app.is_ticket_staff` (`supabase/migrations/20260731070000_harden_ticketing_internal_batch_286_review_fixes.sql`) describing the actual tenant-wide behavior instead of the original, contradicted "config-only" framing. **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (no default role bundles `TKT:Edit` alone, so this is not automatically triggered by any seeded configuration — but any tenant that grants `TKT:Edit` purely for queue/category administration also silently grants that role-holder full read/reply/reclassify/transfer access to every ticket's content tenant-wide, with no independent way to revoke just the content-access half) — owner: a future Ticket-workstream prompt (naturally paired with Prompt 290, Ticket Assignment, or a dedicated RBAC-hardening pass) that decides and implements the intended shape.

### ISS-2026-087 — Internal and Interdepartmental Ticket (HRT-286): attachment-upload UI and browser/accessibility/performance E2E, both named in Prompt 286 §15/§28, are disclosed, not built (OPEN, Low — matches repository-wide precedent)

Discovered `2026-08-13` during the Tier C batch adversarial review for `CG-S12-HRT-014` (Prompt 286), spec-compliance lens Findings 3/4, CONFIRMED by direct code read: `app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/tickets/actions.ts:194` hardcodes `attachmentFileIds: null`, and no file-upload form/field exists anywhere in `ticket-detail-panel.tsx` (grep-confirmed zero "file"/"upload" references), even though `app.reply_to_ticket`'s attachment parameter and its full malware-scan-gating logic are real and live-tested at the DB layer (§15 names "attachments" in the same sentence as "reply versus internal note distinction," which the implementer did fully build). Separately, no Playwright/axe-core spec exists anywhere in the new files for the named TKT-LST/DET E2E requirement (§28/§33).

### ISS-2026-088 — Customer-to-Tenant Ticket (HRT-287): `app.get_customer_ticket` never exposes `resolution_summary` to the customer at all, following this batch's own containment fix for the staff-authored-rationale leak (RESOLVED as a fix, disclosed here as a residual scope narrowing — Low)

Discovered/created `2026-08-14` during the Tier C batch review for `CG-S12-HRT-015` (Prompt 287), as the direct consequence of fixing spec-compliance/security lens Finding 1 (see `ISS-2026-085`'s own Tier C batch review addendum above for the full finding). `app._ticket_transition_authority` never admits the ticket's own requester-side party into `resolved`/`closed` (`requester_allowed` is false for both transitions in `app.ticket_status_transitions`, live-confirmed) — `resolution_summary` is therefore unconditionally staff-authored by construction, with no way to distinguish "safe to echo back" text from "an internal rationale a staff member never meant a customer to read." Rather than build a second, explicit staff-authored "customer-visible closure note" field (real feature work, out of this review-fix batch's own bounded mandate), the fix (`supabase/migrations/20260731090000_harden_ticketing_customer_channel_batch_287_review_fixes.sql`) simply stops exposing `resolution_summary` to the customer projection at all — `app.get_customer_ticket` now always returns `null` for this column, regardless of content. `cancelled_reason`/`last_reopen_reason` are NOT affected the same way — those two are genuinely dual-authored (the same generic `app.transition_ticket_status` services both customer self-cancel/self-reopen and staff-driven cancel/reopen on the customer's behalf), so the fix tracks per-transition authorship (`cancelled_reason_authored_by_customer`/`last_reopen_reason_authored_by_customer`) and continues to echo the customer's own words back to them; only a staff-authored cancel/reopen rationale on a customer-channel ticket is now hidden.

**Handling:** Not fixed further by this batch — a customer-facing "why was my ticket resolved" affordance is a genuine, disclosed product gap now (previously it existed but leaked security-sensitive content unpredictably, which is strictly worse). A future prompt wanting to restore this UX should add an explicit, staff-authored, opt-in customer-visible closure note (a new nullable column and a new optional parameter on `app.transition_ticket_status`, populated only when staff deliberately writes customer-facing text, never inferred from the internal rationale field) rather than re-exposing `resolution_summary` unconditionally. **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (no security exposure — this is a pure feature-completeness gap, the opposite direction of the defect it replaces) — owner: whichever future Ticket-workstream prompt or dedicated UX pass wants to build a real customer-facing closure-note affordance.

**Handling:** Not fixed by this batch — both gaps already match this repository's own repeated, disclosed precedent (no live Supabase project or browser/E2E harness exists anywhere in this repository yet — identically disclosed at nearly every prior HRT checkpoint; e.g. `ISS-2026-064`, `ISS-2026-067`, `ISS-2026-070`), and both were already disclosed in `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-286.md` §9 before this review. Building a real file-upload widget and a browser/E2E harness are genuine, non-trivial feature/infrastructure additions, not bounded review-fixes. **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (no security exposure — the underlying RPC enforces its own malware-scan/tenant/authority checks regardless of caller; the gap is a missing UI affordance and missing test infrastructure) — owner: whichever near-term follow-up prompt builds out the Ticket workstream's remaining UI surface, paired with whichever platform-wide initiative eventually stands up a browser/E2E harness.

**Resolution (this batch, partial):** `getMssTeamWorkspace` now fetches one row past `TEAM_LIST_BOUND` to detect and report `teamTruncated`, and computes `approvalQueueTruncated` from each queue category's own team-scoped (pre-slice) length; `team-workspace-panel.tsx` renders a visible disclosure banner on both cards when either flag is true. The underlying `app.list_my_team_employees` RPC already supports real cursor pagination (`p_after_employee_number`) — HRT-274's own established mechanism — so a genuine "load more" UI is possible without any new RPC.

**Handling:** The silent-truncation half (the concrete harm the finding raises — a manager with 51+ direct reports or 21+ pending items in one category never learning anything is missing) is fixed. A full "load more"/cursor-fetch interaction (wiring `afterEmployeeNumber` through a client action, plus an equivalent mechanism for the four heterogeneous approval-queue sources) is NOT built in this batch — a genuine, non-trivial UI feature (client-side pagination state, a new bound server action per queue source), not a bounded review-round fix. **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (no security exposure — every underlying RPC enforces its own scope regardless of pagination; the caller can now see when more exists, just not fetch it in-app yet) — owner: a near-term Phase 7 follow-up prompt scoped to "wire genuine cursor-based load-more into the MSS team roster and approval queue," matching this repository's own established pattern of disclosing bounded UI-completeness gaps (`ISS-2026-075`/`076`/`080`).

### ISS-2026-089 — Ticket Assignment (HRT-290): Prompt 290's own "queue, team and user assignment" wording names a "team" dimension nowhere built and, until this batch review, nowhere disclosed as a narrowing (RESOLVED as a disclosure — Low, no security/correctness exposure)

Discovered `2026-08-14` during the combined Tier C batch review for `CG-S12-HRT-017`/`CG-S12-HRT-018` (Prompts 289/290), spec-compliance lens Finding 3, CONFIRMED by direct grep: no `team`/team-membership table or concept exists anywhere in this repository, at any phase (`grep -rn "create table app\.\(teams\|team_members\)"` across all 214 migrations returns nothing), yet `290_TICKET_ASSIGNMENT_PROMPT.md` names "team" repeatedly and load-bearingly (§3 "Capability: Queue, Team and User Assignment"; §13/§20/§24/§27's own "eligible queue/team/user criteria"/"effective employee/user/team status"/"canonical Platform user/HR employee/team identities"/"multiple teams... Tenant A/B fixtures"). `HRT-290.md` §9's own C-23 disclosure list ("named spec requirement silently dropped, not merely narrowed") named two deliberate narrowings by name (the claim/unclaim unification, bulk reassignment after revocation, round-robin-as-least-loaded) but never named this one — the entire "team" dimension was substituted for "queue" throughout the migration's implementation without ever being called out as a decision, unlike every other trim in that same section.

A second, same-shape gap in the same section was found in the same review: the cross-prompt integration lens confirmed (by direct code read) that `app._resolve_ticket_routing_rule_for_ticket` reuses only the ticket's own generic `priority` column and mirrors HRT-289's SLA-precedence-engine shape structurally — it never reads `app.ticket_sla_clocks` (no live SLA-clock/breach/reminder signal feeds routing at all), even though the source prompt's own wording (§76/§90/§96: "bind... SLA priority," "SLA due," "SLA-aware notification") reads as broader than what was built. This narrowing, too, was nowhere named in HRT-290's own §9 C-23 walk.

**Disposition:** Neither gap is a functional or security defect. The "team" substitution — independently re-verified by three of the four Tier C lenses — is the same queue-based grouping mechanism every other capability across HRIS and Ticketing already uses as its own team-analog (no sibling capability in Phase 7 built a distinct "team" entity either), and every eligibility/workload/routing dimension in HRT-290 is correctly, consistently keyed off it. The SLA-signal narrowing is a real, working, deterministic routing engine that simply does not yet consume the one additional live signal (an active breach/pause state) that a fuller reading of "SLA-aware" would imply; nothing it does is incorrect for what it claims to do. Both gaps are purely in disclosure discipline — the migration's own header (`supabase/migrations/20260731140000_create_ticket_assignment.sql:3`) copies the prompt's "queue/team/user assignment" phrase verbatim, and §9's own C-23 walk named three other deliberate narrowings by name without ever naming either of these two.

**Handling:** Corrected in the build log, not in code — `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-290.md`'s Tier C batch review section now names both substitutions explicitly as disclosed decisions (queue-based grouping stands in for "team" throughout, matching the rest of this phase; routing precedence is priority-column-based and structurally SLA-precedence-shaped, but does not yet consume a live SLA-clock breach/pause signal). No migration change for either: inventing a real, separate "team" entity distinct from `app.ticket_queues`/`app.ticket_queue_members` would be new, unreviewed data-model surgery outside this review-fix batch's own bounded mandate; wiring a live SLA-clock signal into routing precedence (e.g., an "escalate on breach" scope dimension) is genuine new feature work suited to a dedicated prompt (this phase's own upcoming Prompt 291, Ticket Escalation, is the natural home), not a bounded review-fix. **Status `RESOLVED`** (as a disclosure correction — the underlying behavior was already correct and needed no code fix), Low severity — owner: none for the "team" half; the SLA-signal half is a candidate for Prompt 291 (Ticket Escalation) to pick up explicitly once authorized.

### ISS-2026-090 — Ticket SLA and Knowledge Base (HRT-289): `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-sla.sql` never exercises a customer- or helpdesk-channel ticket, so the official `db:test` "ALL PASSED" evidence never actually drove the SLA engine's customer/helpdesk-channel policy-matching or RLS/RPC boundary paths (OPEN, Low — tracked per `BUILD_EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md` §5.6 as a HIGH-rated lens finding whose underlying live exposure is independently confirmed already fixed)

Discovered `2026-08-14` during the combined Tier C batch review for `CG-S12-HRT-017`/`CG-S12-HRT-018` (Prompts 289/290), spec-compliance lens Finding 2 (rated HIGH by that lens, "directly enables Finding 1 to go undetected"), CONFIRMED by direct grep: `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-sla.sql` calls `app.create_ticket` (internal channel) exclusively — zero occurrences of `app.create_customer_ticket`/`app.create_helpdesk_ticket` anywhere in the file, even though `app.sla_policy_versions.channel` supports all three channels and the precedence engine has dedicated `customer_account_id`/`support_queue_id` specificity dimensions for exactly this scenario. This gap is precisely what let `ISS-2026-089`'s sibling finding — the `ticket_sla_clocks` RLS leak, `ISS-2026-085`-adjacent in severity, see the batch review's own Finding 1 — ship with the official harness reporting "ALL PASSED."

**Disposition:** The concrete live exposure this test-coverage gap enabled (the `ticket_sla_clocks` RLS leak) is independently CONFIRMED FIXED and re-verified live by this same batch review, using the exact customer-channel scenario the shipped test never built (a real `app.create_customer_ticket` + customer-scoped published SLA policy + started clock + forged customer-account-member session) — see `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-289.md` §14.1 finding 1 and `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-290.md` §13.2 for the full live reproduction, both pre-fix (leaked) and post-fix (zero rows). The security lens's own independent live probing of the customer/helpdesk-channel SLA read surfaces (`app.search_customer_knowledge_articles`-adjacent audience checks, cross-tenant SLA policy resolution restricted to `service_role`) and the integration lens's own independent live channel-shape verification further reduce residual risk that an UNDISCOVERED customer/helpdesk-channel SLA defect remains. What remains genuinely open is process, not exposure: the CHECKED-IN `ticketing-sla.sql` still cannot catch a FUTURE regression in either non-internal channel's SLA policy-matching/RLS path, because it still never constructs one.

**Handling:** Not fixed in this batch — editing an already-committed db-test file is outside this batch's own established "additive migration only" fix-scope convention (matching HRT-286/287/288's own three prior fix commits, none of which touched a db-test file either, per direct `git show --name-only` verification against all three). A future prompt extending `ticketing-sla.sql` (or a dedicated regression-coverage follow-up) should add a customer/helpdesk-channel section mirroring the ad hoc scenario this review session built and live-verified for its own re-derivation. **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (the live exposure this gap enabled is fixed and re-verified; what remains is a coverage/regression-protection gap, not an active vulnerability) — owner: a near-term Ticket-workstream follow-up prompt (or a dedicated db-test-coverage pass) scoped to add real customer/helpdesk-channel SLA fixtures to `ticketing-sla.sql`.

### ISS-2026-091 — Sensitive Personal and Payroll Data Controls (HRT-293): RPD-025 retention/legal-hold classification is unbuilt for every Phase 7 HR/payroll structured-data table (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-14` during the Phase 7 audit (`CG-S12-HRT-021`, Prompt 293, Finding E) and re-confirmed live during this checkpoint's own fix pass: a repository-wide grep of every `20260730830000`–`20260731190000` migration (every HRT-274 through HRT-293 migration, inclusive) for `legal_hold`/`retention_class` returns zero hits on any HR/payroll data table — `app.employees`, `app.candidates`, `app.leave_requests`, `app.payroll_run_employee_results`, `app.performance_outcomes`, `app.training_certificates`, and every sibling table in between. Only the generic, reused Platform primitives carry this metadata: `app.audit_logs` (PLT-116, `legal_hold`/`legal_hold_reason` columns) and `app.files` (PLT-128, per-document-type retention/legal-hold-eligibility config) — neither of which any Phase 7 HR/payroll capability's own primary record tables inherit or reference. `scripts/data-classification/registry.ts`'s own `CATEGORY_RETENTION_CLASS` maps the `payroll` category to `finance_tax_10y` as "a disclosed inference, not an itemized RPD-025 row" (the comment's own words, unchanged by this checkpoint) — the retention CLASS is named in code, but no table anywhere in Phase 7 actually carries a `retention_class`/`legal_hold` column an operator could act on for a real legal hold or scheduled purge. Directly analogous to the Phase 5 gap `ISS-2026-027` (GPS telemetry retention), which was disclosed, then later fixed at `CG-S10-ATW-031` — this is the identical shape, one phase later, not yet on anyone's fix list before this audit surfaced it. Unlike that gap, this one had never been disclosed anywhere in `docs/build-log/phase-07/*.md` or this file before now.

**Disposition:** Narrow immediate blast radius — no live data leak, no access-control gap, and RPD-025's own governing business rule is itself explicit that CargoGrid "must never claim audit/retention records are tamper-proof" (RPD-022/025, `06_RLS_RBAC_WORKSTREAM.md` §8), so the honest posture is disclosure, not a false claim of coverage. **Not fixed by this checkpoint** — adding real `retention_class`/`legal_hold`/`legal_hold_reason` columns (plus whatever purge/hold-enforcement RPC would actually act on them) across the ~30 HR/payroll tables this gap spans is a genuine, multi-table schema addition requiring its own design decision (which retention class applies to which table — payroll figures plausibly warrant `finance_tax_10y` per the registry's own inference, but candidate/recruitment records, leave narrative, and performance data each need their own considered mapping, not a copy-paste default), not a bounded review-round fix within this checkpoint's own Critical/High-focused mandate (Findings A–D). **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (per this checkpoint's own explicit disposition rule: a confirmed Medium finding outside the batch's bounded file/migration scope is disclosed, not silently dropped) — owner: a dedicated future Phase 7 (or platform-wide RPD-025) follow-up prompt scoped to "add real retention_class/legal_hold columns and an enforcement mechanism across every HR/payroll data table," landing once, consistently, mirroring how `ISS-2026-027` was eventually closed for GPS telemetry rather than piecemeal per capability.

### ISS-2026-092 — Sensitive Personal and Payroll Data Controls (HRT-293): `app.employee_change_requests.reason`/`decided_reason` are readable by any active tenant member via a raw table SELECT — the identical shape as Finding A, self-found, not named by the audit (OPEN, Medium)

Self-found `2026-08-14` while fixing Finding A (the five `app.employees` HR-narrative reason columns) for this same checkpoint: `app.employee_change_requests.reason`/`decided_reason` (an employee's own stated reason for requesting a personal_email/phone/address correction, and HR's own decision rationale — HRT-274, `20260730830000_create_hris_employee_master.sql`) are included in that table's column-restricted `grant select (...) to authenticated` (unlike the five `app.employees` columns Finding A named, this grant was never built to exclude them), and the table's own RLS policy (`employee_change_requests_select_scoped`) admits any active, non-`customer_user` tenant member — not gated on `HRS:View`/`HRS:View personal data` at all. `app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/hris/employees/[masterRecordId]/page.tsx` reads this table directly via `.from("employee_change_requests").select("*")` (its own comment explicitly notes "no dedicated read RPC exists for change requests... RLS policy already governs this correctly" — a claim this finding shows is only true for row-scope, not field-scope, the identical Finding-A-shaped gap). Both `app.capture_audit_event` call sites for this table (`request_employee_change`/`decide_employee_change_request`) were independently found and fixed under Finding B in this same checkpoint (`20260731180000`) — only the raw-table-read vector remains open.

**Disposition:** Real, but narrower in content-sensitivity than Finding A's own five columns — a request to correct a phone number or mailing address is not inherently disciplinary/medical narrative the way a termination or suspension reason is, though it can occasionally disclose personal circumstances (the field_key allow-list is entirely `personal_email`/`personal_phone`/`personal_address_*`, so the surrounding narrative is always personal-contact-adjacent, never work-content). **Not fixed by this checkpoint** — closing it properly (mirroring Finding A's own fix shape) requires a genuinely new masking read path: a dedicated `get_employee_change_requests`-shaped RPC (mirroring `app.list_employee_emergency_contacts`'s own established self-or-`HRS:View-personal-data` masking convention), a new `server/queries/employee.ts` wrapper, and a `page.tsx` edit to stop reading the raw table — real, bounded, but genuinely NEW service/UI-layer work beyond this checkpoint's own migration-plus-registry-plus-docs mandate (Findings A–D), and this checkpoint's own scope discipline (§5.6-equivalent: a confirmed Medium finding outside the bounded scope is disclosed, not silently dropped, and no Critical/High finding is left open uncontained — this is assessed Medium, not High, given the narrower content class). **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity — owner: the same near-term HR/payroll follow-up that would also reasonably pick up `ISS-2026-091`, since both are Phase 7 HR field-level-control completeness gaps this same audit surfaced.

### ISS-2026-093 — Sensitive Personal and Payroll Data Controls (HRT-293): `app.cancel_approval_request`, the shared cross-domain PLT-123 approval-engine primitive, duplicates its own raw `p_reason` into `app.audit_logs.reason` -- a repository-wide C-24 gap, not scoped to HR, self-found and disclosed (OPEN, Medium)

Self-found `2026-08-14` while adding a live regression test for this checkpoint's own Finding B fix (`app.cancel_onboarding_case`, HRT-277): `app.cancel_onboarding_case` (and every other HR capability's own cancel-with-reason function, all fixed this checkpoint to stop passing their OWN raw reason to `capture_audit_event`) internally calls the SHARED, cross-domain `app.cancel_approval_request` (PLT-123) to cancel a still-in-flight approval request, passing the same caller-supplied reason through as that function's own `p_reason` parameter. `app.cancel_approval_request` has its own, separate `capture_audit_event` call (`action = 'cancel_approval_request'`) that passes this reason RAW, unaffected by any of this checkpoint's own fixes (which only touched the calling HR functions' own outer audit calls, never PLT-123's own inner one). Live-confirmed: cancelling an onboarding case with reason `'reorg cancelled the exit'` produces a genuine `app.audit_logs` row with `action='cancel_approval_request'` and `reason='reorg cancelled the exit'` in plaintext, readable by any plain `tenant_admin` via `app.query_audit_logs` — identical mechanism to Finding B, but on infrastructure this checkpoint's own charter (Phase 7 HR/payroll) does not own.

**Disposition:** Real, live-reproduced, same severity class as Finding B (a `tenant_admin` with zero domain-specific permission can read a cancellation reason through the generic audit log) — but `app.cancel_approval_request` is Platform Core's own PLT-123 primitive, called by every domain in this repository that has an approval workflow (Finance, Commercial, Procurement, HR, and more), not an HR-owned function. Fixing it here would mean altering shared Platform Core infrastructure and would need re-verification across every OTHER domain's own approval-cancellation call sites and their own regression suites — squarely outside this checkpoint's own Critical/High-focused, Phase-7-HR-scoped mandate (Findings A-D), and a genuinely different, larger-blast-radius change than this checkpoint's own additive-migration-only fix shape. **Not fixed by this checkpoint.** **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (same disclosure-not-fix reasoning as `ISS-2026-091`/`092`: a confirmed finding outside the batch's bounded scope, disclosed with full reasoning, never silently dropped) — owner: a dedicated Platform Core (PLT-1xx) follow-up prompt scoped to "close the C-24 shape in `app.cancel_approval_request` (and any other shared, unredacted-reason `capture_audit_event` call in Platform Core) once, for every domain that calls it," rather than a per-domain patch.

### ISS-2026-094 — Batch review (Prompts 291-293, `CG-S12-HRT-019/020/021`): `app.ticket_links`/`app.ticket_link_events` raw-table SELECT admitted the exact cross-domain, cross-tenant access the capability's own RPC layer exists to deny (RESOLVED — CRITICAL, fixed)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the Tier C batch review for Prompts 291-293 (security/RLS/tenant-isolation lens), independently re-derived live by the fix phase before fixing: `20260731170000`'s own RLS policies (`ticket_links_select_scoped`/`ticket_link_events_select_scoped`) admit any caller who can `app.can_access_ticket` the parent ticket (staff/watcher/requester) — or any Supreme Admin unconditionally — with no re-check of the linked record's own cross-domain FIN/PRC/OPS authorization or of `app._ticket_link_actor_may_view_tenant_data` (the capability's own decision-5 gate closing "any Supreme Admin can browse any tenant's data via a helpdesk ticket"). Live-reproduced twice pre-fix: (1) a same-tenant, domain-denied ticket-staff member's raw `select * from app.ticket_links` returned full `safe_snapshot` content (real invoice amounts, vendor names, shipment numbers) that `app.list_ticket_links` correctly withheld; (2) a fresh, zero-grant Supreme Admin's raw select against a helpdesk ticket in an unrelated tenant returned a real linked vendor's `safe_snapshot` in full, even though `app.search_ticket_link_candidates` correctly returned zero candidates for the identical actor/ticket — defeating the exact scenario the migration's own decision-5 commentary says this capability was built to close.

**Disposition:** **RESOLVED.** Fixed in `supabase/migrations/20260731210000_harden_ticketing_escalation_linked_records_hris_batch_291_293_review_fixes.sql` (Finding 1): raw table-level `SELECT` on both tables revoked from `authenticated` entirely (mirrors how writes on these tables were already RPC-only) — every legitimate read now goes exclusively through `app.list_ticket_links`/`app.list_ticket_link_events` (both `SECURITY DEFINER`, unaffected by the revoke since they execute under the function owner's privileges). RLS policies are left in place as defense in depth. Live-reproduced fixed: both probes above now return `permission denied for table ticket_links`/`ticket_link_events`; the RPC layer continues to return correct, masked data for the identical actors. `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-linked-records.sql` §13/§15 updated to assert the closed shape directly. **Status `RESOLVED`**, was Critical — owner: closed, no further action required. A repo-wide propagation sweep found no other new/changed table in this batch sharing the same shape (the six `app.ticket_escalation_*` tables carry only ticket-native fields, independently re-verified clean).

### ISS-2026-095 — Batch review (Prompts 291-293): five pre-existing HR/Recruitment RPCs (`app.list_employee_duplicate_candidates`, `app.get_employee_position_assignment_history`, `app.get_employee_current_assignment`, `app.get_application_detail`, `app.list_application_interviews`) silently bypassed `20260731200000`'s own raw-table column restriction by returning the full row/composite to any plain `HRS:View` holder (RESOLVED — HIGH, fixed)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the Tier C batch review (cross-prompt integration lens), independently re-derived live: each of the five functions above does `select *`/returns a full table composite (`app.employee_duplicate_candidates`, `app.employee_position_assignments`, `app.job_applications`, `app.interviews`) that includes a free-text reason column `20260731200000` restricted at the raw-table-grant level (`decided_reason`, `reason_note`, `rejection_reason`/`withdrawal_reason`, `cancel_reason`) — none of these five call sites were caught by that migration's own "repository-wide grep for each column name" verification, because none of them names the leaking column literally (they use `select *`/a whole-row variable). Live-reproduced: `viewer@hrmemp1.test` (plain `HRS:View`, zero `HRS:View personal data`) calling `app.list_employee_duplicate_candidates` receives the real `decided_reason` text in the clear, while a raw `select decided_reason from app.employee_duplicate_candidates` as the same actor is correctly denied (`permission denied for table employee_duplicate_candidates`) — proving the RPC, not the raw table, was the live bypass.

**Disposition:** **RESOLVED.** Fixed in `20260731210000` (Finding 2): all five functions now mask their respective reason column(s) to `null` unless the caller holds `HRS:View personal data`, mirroring `app.get_employee_profile`/`app.get_employee_lifecycle_history`'s established convention (`20260731180000`). No self-branch on any of the five (each function's own pre-existing call-authority gate already requires plain `HRS:View` for every caller, so no legitimate self-only caller could ever reach this point without it). Call-authority gates themselves are unchanged. Live-reproduced fixed for the `list_employee_duplicate_candidates` case above (now returns `null`); the other four independently confirmed by direct code read plus a live `has_view_personal_data`-gated probe. **Status `RESOLVED`**, was High — owner: closed. `docs/standards/HR_PAYROLL_FIELD_POLICY_MATRIX.md` §2's own claim ("Column-restricted grants... back every one of these at the raw-table level too, so a direct... read by `authenticated` cannot bypass the RPC's own masking") is corrected by this entry — that claim was false for these five RPCs before this fix.

### ISS-2026-096 — Batch review (Prompt 291, Ticket Escalation): `app.escalate_ticket`'s own documented double-submit protection was false whenever a manual escalation does not itself mutate `app.tickets` (RESOLVED — Medium, fixed)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the Tier C batch review (correctness/concurrency and spec-compliance lenses, independently), confirmed live with real concurrent `psql` processes: `app.escalate_ticket` guards against a duplicate call via `v_ticket.record_version <> p_expected_version`, but the function only mutates `app.tickets` when `p_reassign=true` and `target_type='employee'` (via `app._apply_ticket_assignment`'s own side effect). For every other supported combination — `target_type='queue'`, or an employee target with `p_reassign=false` (both explicitly named by decision 3, "notify and/or reassign, independently configured") — `app.tickets.record_version` is never touched, so a genuine network-retry/double-click with the identical `p_expected_version` is accepted a second time, advancing the ticket to a SECOND escalation level and sending a second real notification instead of being rejected as a duplicate. The natural-key unique index protecting auto-triggered escalations (`ticket_escalation_events_triggered_unique`) is explicitly scoped `where policy_version_id is not null` and gives manual escalations no equivalent protection.

**Disposition:** **RESOLVED.** Fixed in `20260731210000` (Finding 3): `app.escalate_ticket` now unconditionally touches `app.tickets` (bumping `record_version` via the existing `touch_ticket_row()` trigger) once its own escalation has been applied, for every successful manual escalation — placed AFTER `app._apply_ticket_escalation` returns, specifically so the reassignment path's own already-correct version check inside `app._apply_ticket_assignment` is never fought by a pre-emptive bump. `app._apply_ticket_escalation` itself (the shared engine the auto-evaluator batch also calls) is untouched — the auto-triggered path's real protection is the natural-key unique index, unaffected by this fix. `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-escalation.sql` §2 updated: the double-submit scenario now asserts `stale_version` is correctly raised, and the legitimate "second manual escalate advances to level 2" assertion now re-reads a fresh `expected_version` first. **Status `RESOLVED`**, was Medium — owner: closed.

### ISS-2026-097 — Batch review (Prompt 293 corrective functions): `app.adjust_leave_balance`/`app.load_opening_leave_balance` have a real idempotency-key unique index and pre-check, but no `exception when unique_violation` handler around the INSERT (RESOLVED — Medium, fixed)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the Tier C batch review (correctness/concurrency lens), confirmed by direct code read (both functions' bodies are redeployed in full by `20260731190000`, this batch's own migration): the pre-check `SELECT` comparing the complete target tuple cannot close the race between two concurrent identical calls, since neither sees the other's row before both `INSERT`. The real guarantee is the unique index (`leave_balance_ledger_idempotency_unique`) plus a real exception handler — only the index existed. A genuine concurrent retry with the same `p_idempotency_key` does not create a duplicate row (data integrity holds), but the losing session receives a raw, unhandled `duplicate key value violates unique constraint` error instead of the graceful idempotent-success return the pre-check was clearly designed to provide. Inherited from HRT-280 (not introduced by this batch), but in scope because this batch's own migration redeploys both bodies verbatim.

**Disposition:** **RESOLVED.** Fixed in `20260731210000` (Finding 4): both functions now wrap the `INSERT` in a real `exception when unique_violation` handler, mirroring `app.link_ticket_record`'s own established three-part idempotency shape (`20260731170000`) — on a unique violation, the winning row is re-selected and either returned (if it matches the caller's own requested target) or a clean `idempotency_key_conflict` is raised (never a mismatched row silently returned). **Status `RESOLVED`**, was Medium — owner: closed.

### ISS-2026-098 — `app.approval_requests.ended_reason` readable via a raw-table SELECT by any active tenant member of any domain — a self-found sibling of `ISS-2026-093`'s own raw-table vector, broader and lower-bar than the audit-log vector that entry named (RESOLVED — CRITICAL, fixed; `ISS-2026-093`'s own audit-log-duplication half remains OPEN, unchanged)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the Tier C batch review for Prompts 291-293 (spec-compliance lens's own re-derivation of `ISS-2026-093`), confirmed live: `app.approval_requests` (PLT-123, `20260719090000`, untouched by this batch) carries a full whole-table `grant select on app.approval_requests to authenticated` and an RLS policy admitting any active tenant member with zero permission of any kind. `ended_reason` carries the identical free-text cancellation/rejection narrative every domain's own approval-consuming capability (Finance, Commercial, Procurement, HR) passes through `app.cancel_approval_request`/`app.decide_approval_step` — a materially LOWER access bar than even `ISS-2026-093`'s own disclosed `app.audit_logs.reason` vector (which requires at least `tenant_admin`). Live-reproduced: a plain-role tenant member with zero HRS permission reads a real onboarding-case cancellation's `ended_reason` ("reorg cancelled the exit") straight off `app.approval_requests` via a raw select, no RPC involved.

**Disposition:** **RESOLVED for the raw-table vector.** Fixed in `20260731210000` (Finding 5): `ended_reason` excluded from the raw-table grant (`revoke select` + explicit re-`grant` of every other column), mirroring `20260731200000`'s own established shape. Verified before fixing, not assumed: no existing RPC anywhere in this repository returns this column to a broad caller (`app.get_approval_request_history` reads the separate, differently-gated `app.approval_decisions.reason` instead), and every raw TypeScript read of this table (`server/queries/{procurement-approval,credit,quotation-approval,leave}.ts`) already selects only `id, entity_type, entity_id` — so this is a pure closure of an unused bypass, not a narrowing of any real caller's functionality (re-confirmed via a full `pnpm run db:test` pass across every domain touching this table). `scripts/db-tests/approval.sql`'s own schema-privilege section updated to assert the restricted column directly.
