This is genuinely Platform Core infrastructure, unlike Findings 1-4 above, but the fix here is a narrow, mechanical raw-table-grant restriction with near-zero behavioral risk — a materially different, much lower-risk class of change than editing `app.cancel_approval_request`'s function body (which every domain's own approval-cancellation call path exercises). **`ISS-2026-093`'s own text is unchanged, per this file's append-only discipline — its audit-log-duplication finding remains genuinely `OPEN`**, since fixing it requires a function-body change to shared Platform Core infrastructure needing re-verification across every other domain's own regression suite, a materially larger-blast-radius change this narrow batch-review fix pass correctly declined to make unilaterally. **Status `RESOLVED`** (raw-table half only), was assessed Critical for this half — owner: closed for this half; `ISS-2026-093`'s own Platform Core follow-up owner is unchanged.

### ISS-2026-099 — `app.employee_change_requests.reason`/`decided_reason` readable via a raw-table SELECT by any active tenant member — closes `ISS-2026-092` (RESOLVED — fixed; re-assessed High during this review, not Medium, given the identical access bar as Finding A)

Discovered `2026-08-14` by HRT-293 itself as `ISS-2026-092` (Medium, disclosed not fixed — see that entry's own full text, unchanged). Re-assessed `2026-08-15` during the Tier C batch review (spec-compliance lens): the access bar (any active tenant member, zero HRS permission) is identical to Finding A's own raw-table vector, which was rated Critical; the narrower-content-class reasoning `ISS-2026-092` itself gives for a two-tier severity drop does not fully hold, since `reason`/`decided_reason` on this table (an employee's own stated reason for a personal-contact correction, and HR's own decision rationale) are still genuinely personal-data-adjacent narrative, not merely structural. Precise starting shape (re-verified live via `information_schema.column_privileges`, not assumed from a single-line grep): `20260730830000`'s own `grant select (...) on app.employee_change_requests to authenticated` is already column-restricted and correctly excludes `current_value_snapshot`/`requested_value` (the two raw PII value columns) but wrongly includes `reason`/`decided_reason`.

**Disposition:** **RESOLVED.** Fixed in `20260731210000` (Finding 6): the raw-table grant now also excludes `reason`/`decided_reason`; a new masked RPC, `app.get_employee_change_requests` (self-or-`HRS:View personal data`, mirroring `app.get_employee_profile`'s own `v_unmasked` convention — also nulls `current_value_snapshot`/`requested_value` as defense in depth, even though those were already correctly excluded from the raw grant), is now the only legitimate read path. `app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/hris/employees/[masterRecordId]/page.tsx` updated in the same commit to call the new RPC instead of the raw table (its own prior direct `.select("*")` read is removed); `server/contracts/employee/employee.ts`/`server/queries/employee.ts`/`employee-detail-panel.tsx` updated to match (`requestedValue` is now nullable). **Side discovery, noted for completeness:** the page's OLD raw `.select("*")` call would already have failed with `permission denied` for every real user before this fix too, since Postgres's wildcard select requires privilege on every column and `current_value_snapshot`/`requested_value` were never granted — meaning the page's change-requests section was likely already functionally broken (a pre-existing, unrelated availability bug, not something this review introduced or needed to separately fix, since the RPC-based replacement resolves both issues at once). The underlying database-level privilege excess on `reason`/`decided_reason` was independently exploitable via any direct PostgREST/Supabase request selecting a column subset that excludes the two blocked columns, regardless of what the shipped page's own query happened to do — consistent with this repository's own doctrine that database boundaries must not rely on UI-only enforcement. **Status `RESOLVED`** — owner: closed. `ISS-2026-092`'s own text remains unchanged (append-only); this entry supersedes its disposition.

### ISS-2026-100 — Batch review (Prompt 292, Typed Ticket-Linked Records): `app.list_ticket_link_events` is built, tested, and has no UI/action caller anywhere in the repository (OPEN, Medium — C-20, disclosed not fixed)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the Tier C batch review (spec-compliance lens), confirmed by repo-wide grep: `app.list_ticket_link_events` (the staff-only ledger reader RPC), its TS wrapper `listTicketLinkEvents` (`server/queries/ticketing.ts`), and its own unit test all exist, but no route, page, or server action anywhere in `app/` calls it. Its escalation-domain sibling, `app.list_ticket_escalation_events`, IS correctly wired into `tickets/[ticketId]/page.tsx` — this is an inconsistent, not structurally-forced, omission. `docs/build-log/phase-07/HRT-292.md`'s own C-20 Tier B row discusses `record_ticket_link_summary_access`/`record_ticket_link_access_denial` and the `source='system'` extension point in detail but never mentions this function at all.

**Disposition:** Real, not previously disclosed. Not fixed by this review — closing it properly means adding a real ledger-timeline UI section (data fetching in `page.tsx`, a new panel section, likely a bound server action), a genuine UI feature addition comparable in scope to `EscalationSection`'s own timeline, not a bounded migration-only fix. Outside this review's own additive-migration-plus-narrow-TS-edit mandate. **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (no security exposure — the underlying data is correctly access-controlled when read via the RPC directly; this is a completeness/UI-coverage gap) — owner: a near-term Ticketing follow-up prompt scoped to "add a link-history ledger section to the ticket detail panel, mirroring the escalation timeline."

### ISS-2026-101 — Batch review (Prompt 291, Ticket Escalation): `app.get_ticket_escalation_status_for_requester` has no real UI caller reachable by the actual `customer_user` principal it was built to serve (OPEN, Medium — C-20, disclosed not fixed)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the Tier C batch review (cross-prompt integration lens), confirmed by code read: `app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/tickets/[ticketId]/page.tsx` is the only call site of `getTicketEscalationStatusForRequester`, but that route (`lib/portal/ticket-guard.ts`) admits only `org_user`/`tenant_admin` layers — never a real `customer_user`. The route an actual `customer_user` can reach (`customer-tickets/[ticketId]/page.tsx`, guarded by `lib/portal/customer-ticket-guard.ts`, whose own header states "No identity holds both simultaneously in the intended model") contains zero references to escalation of any kind. The non-staff branch of `EscalationSection` in the internal panel is reachable only by a genuine `channel='internal'` non-staff requester (a real `org_user`), never by a customer-channel ticket's actual customer. Mirrors an identical, already-accepted shape in HRT-289 (`getTicketSlaStatusForRequester`).

**Disposition:** Real, no security exposure (the opposite — a built, correctly-masked feature simply never renders for its intended audience), narrow blast radius, reproduces a pattern this codebase already tolerates elsewhere. Not previously disclosed in `HRT-291.md` (decision 12 asserts the feature as delivered with no UI-reachability caveat) or in this file. Not fixed by this review — wiring a customer-visible escalation badge into `customer-tickets/[ticketId]/page.tsx` plus its own action/query wrapper is genuine new UI/service-layer work outside this review's additive-migration-plus-narrow-TS-edit mandate. **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity — owner: a near-term Ticketing follow-up prompt scoped to "add a customer-visible escalation status badge to the customer ticket detail page," matching this repository's own established pattern of disclosing bounded UI-completeness gaps.

### ISS-2026-102 — Batch review (Prompt 292, Typed Ticket-Linked Records): a customer viewing `app.list_ticket_links` sees `entity_type`/`entity_id` (a real internal record id) for an out-of-registry link type (vendor/user), even though customers may never search/link those types (OPEN, Low)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the Tier C batch review (spec-compliance lens), live-reproduced: an admin links a real vendor record to a customer-channel ticket (link authority is not type-restricted for staff); the ticket's own customer requester then calls `app.list_ticket_links` and receives a row with `entity_type='vendor', entity_id=<real vendor UUID>, live_available=false, status_label='unavailable'` — `label`/`detail` are correctly withheld, but the type and a real internal id are not. `20260731170000`'s own decision 7 states customers get "a NARROWER entity-type registry... never vendor or user," but that restriction is enforced only on search/link creation, not on reading already-established links.

**Disposition:** Real, narrow blast radius (a bare entity type plus an opaque internal UUID, no label/amount/status content, and only reachable by a customer already viewing their own ticket), no live content exposure beyond confirming "some vendor record was linked here." Not fixed — Low severity, per this repository's own Low-severity handling convention ("disclose only, no fix effort unless trivial"); nulling `entity_type`/`entity_id` too for a non-customer-safe type on an already-`unavailable` row is a real, bounded follow-up but touches `app.list_ticket_links`'s own return contract (a signature-adjacent change warranting its own dedicated review, not a batch-review-note-driven change). **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity — owner: a near-term Ticketing follow-up prompt.

### ISS-2026-103 — Two further wall-clock/day-of-week-dependent `db:test` assertion failures found on `2026-08-15` (a Saturday), beyond the already-registered `ISS-2026-077` — `scripts/db-tests/hris-overtime-timesheet.sql` and `scripts/db-tests/procurement-vendor-performance.sql` (OPEN, Low — informational, neither touched by this batch)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during this batch's own full `pnpm run db:test` gate run (correctness/concurrency lens, independently re-confirmed by the fix phase): `scripts/db-tests/hris-overtime-timesheet.sql:284` asserts `classification=weekday` for a fixture computed against `current_date`, which is `weekend` on a Saturday/Sunday run (`got approved/120/weekend/120`, live-reproduced); `procurement-vendor-performance.sql:978` fails for the same underlying class. Neither file was touched by Prompts 291-293 or by this review's own fix migration — both are pre-existing, unrelated (HRT-281 and Phase-6 Procurement respectively). This means a "clean full `db:test` run" is currently wall-clock/day-of-week-sensitive in at least three files, not only the one `ISS-2026-077` already names.

**Disposition:** Same class as `ISS-2026-077`, not a new defect shape — disclosed here as a distinct entry (rather than editing `ISS-2026-077`'s own text, per this file's append-only discipline) because it names two additional, previously-unregistered files. Not fixed by this review (out of both files' owning domains). Both files were temporarily moved aside to obtain a clean gate run for this batch's own review-and-fix pass and restored immediately afterward (`git status` on `scripts/db-tests/` confirms neither is modified or missing in the final commit). **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (the underlying capabilities are unaffected — only their own db-test fixtures assume a weekday `current_date`) — owner: a near-term db-test-hygiene follow-up scoped to "make every wall-clock-dependent fixture in `scripts/db-tests/` pin an explicit reference date instead of assuming `current_date` falls on a weekday," covering all three files (`ISS-2026-077` plus these two) in one pass.

### ISS-2026-104 — HRIS/Ticketing Integrated Verification (HRT-294): `app.terminate_employee`/`app.suspend_employee` (HRT-274) perform ZERO Platform-authority revocation, and nothing couples an employee's HR lifecycle transition to the one RPC that does — a terminated (or suspended) employee retains full permission-gated write authority system-wide, live-proven via a real self-service ticket creation after termination (OPEN, High)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the `CG-S12-HRT-022` (Prompt 294) integrated-verification checkpoint — a cross-capability failure, between Employee Master (HRT-274) and Onboarding/Offboarding (HRT-277), that neither capability's own isolated `db-tests/*.sql` file could catch (each tests its own RPCs in isolation; neither tests whether HR-lifecycle state constrains Platform authority in the OTHER domain). CONFIRMED by direct code read and a fresh, live, end-to-end reproduction against a disposable Postgres 16 database with every migration applied (221 files, clean).

**Code:** `app.terminate_employee` (current definition: `supabase/migrations/20260731180000_harden_hris_employee_master_sensitive_data_controls_review_fixes.sql:527-590`; its own comment at `supabase/migrations/20260730830000_create_hris_employee_master.sql:1531` states outright: *"user_id remains linked (Platform authentication revocation, if any, is a separate PLT-107/108 action, not performed here)"*) and `app.suspend_employee` (`supabase/migrations/20260730830000_create_hris_employee_master.sql:1325-1384`) both flip `app.employees.lifecycle_status` and write an audit/lifecycle-event row — neither calls `app.transition_user_status`, neither touches `app.tenant_user_identities`, neither touches `app.role_assignments`. The ONLY RPC in the entire repository that performs a real Platform-authority revocation for an offboarded employee is `app.request_onboarding_access_revocation` (`supabase/migrations/20260730890000_harden_hris_onboarding_offboarding_review_fixes.sql:140-256`) — a single task-completion action inside an OPTIONAL Onboarding/Offboarding **case**, which (a) is never automatically created when `terminate_employee`/`suspend_employee` runs, (b) is not required to exist at all before or after termination, and (c) even when a case does exist, only blocks case *finalize* on its `access_revocation` task being `completed` **or `waived`** (`mandatory_tasks_incomplete` gate, `supabase/migrations/20260730880000_create_hris_onboarding_offboarding.sql:1716-1725`) — and per the already-registered `ISS-2026-071`, task completion/waiver authority is uniformly `HRS:Edit`-gated, not task-owner-identity-gated, and per `is_mandatory` being a per-tenant checklist-template authoring choice, nothing structurally requires the template to even include an `access_revocation` task.

**Live reproduction (fresh disposable database, all 221 migrations applied):** a tenant, an HR admin (`HRS:Override`), and a worker holding a real, unrelated `PRC:Create` role assignment were seeded; the worker was onboarded to `app.employees.lifecycle_status='active'` (linked via `user_id`) with zero Onboarding/Offboarding case ever created. `app.evaluate_permission(worker, tenant, 'PRC', 'Create')` → `allowed=true`. `app.terminate_employee(...)` was called directly (no offboarding case, ever) → `lifecycle_status='terminated'`. Immediately after: `app.tenant_user_identities.status` for the worker = `'active'` (unchanged); `app.role_assignments` active-count for the worker = `1` (unchanged); `app.evaluate_permission(worker, tenant, 'PRC', 'Create')` → **`allowed=true`, `reason=role_grant`** — identical to before termination. As a concrete write-path proof (not merely a permission-evaluator check): the SAME now-terminated worker's forged session (`request.jwt.claims`) called `app.create_ticket` (HRT-286, `supabase/migrations/20260731060000_create_ticketing_internal.sql:1005-1027` — self-service ticket creation gates only on `app.has_active_tenant_membership`/`app.get_self_employee`, neither of which checks `app.employees.lifecycle_status`) and **successfully created a real ticket** (`TKT-2026-000001`) after termination. By contrast, Attendance's OWN independent defensive check correctly rejected the same terminated worker's clock-in (`employee_not_active`, `supabase/migrations/20260730900000_create_hris_attendance.sql:613`) — proving the gap is domain-inconsistent, not a uniform, deliberate design: some domains (Attendance, Leave, Shift/Roster — independently re-checked, all three re-verify `lifecycle_status` themselves) defend against a terminated identity; most others (confirmed here for Ticketing; structurally true for every other domain gated purely by `app.evaluate_permission`, e.g. Procurement, Finance, KPI/Performance approvals, and ESS/MSS's own zero-migration composition layer over these same RPCs) do not, because nothing in the shared Platform layer ties `app.employees.lifecycle_status` to authority at all.

**Relationship to `ISS-2026-072` (OPEN, High):** related but distinct, not a duplicate. `ISS-2026-072` is about `app.transition_user_status`'s `'revoked'` branch not cascading `app.role_assignments` — it presumes something calls `transition_user_status` in the first place. This entry is upstream of that: `app.terminate_employee`, the single most fundamental "this employee is gone" HR action, never calls `app.transition_user_status` (or anything else Platform-facing) at all, by explicit design. Even a full fix of `ISS-2026-072` (making `transition_user_status`'s revoke branch cascade role_assignments correctly) would not close this gap, because the termination path never reaches that function unless HR separately, manually, successfully drives a whole second capability (Onboarding/Offboarding) to the specific point of completing one specific task.

**Disposition:** Not fixed by this checkpoint — per `BUILD_EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md` §3.3 and this checkpoint's own charter ("No planned feature schema... allow only minimal registered verification-defect repair"), closing this properly requires an architecture-level decision outside a verification checkpoint's bounded mandate: e.g. (a) `terminate_employee`/`suspend_employee` themselves triggering `app.transition_user_status` (with the ISS-2026-072 role_assignments cascade fixed FIRST, or duplicated inline the way HRT-277's own local fix already does), (b) a hard structural coupling requiring an Offboarding case's access-revocation task to complete before/at termination rather than being optional and separately timed, or (c) `app.evaluate_permission` itself re-checking `app.employees.lifecycle_status` as defense in depth the way it does not today even for `app.users.status` (`ISS-2026-072`'s own disclosed centralized-fix option). Each is a genuine design decision (which approach, blocking vs. advisory, whether it risks locking out a legitimate concurrent HR action), not a bounded repair. **Status `OPEN`**, High severity (a live-verified, real, system-wide authority-retention gap — a terminated OR suspended employee keeps every permission-gated capability their role assignments grant, indefinitely, in any domain that does not independently re-check HR lifecycle status, until/unless HR remembers a wholly separate, optional workflow) — owner: Prompt 295 (Hardening), the designated next task per the execution index's own downstream-impact list (`00_EXECUTION_INDEX.md` §"Downstream impact: HRT-295..297"), with a mandate spanning `app.terminate_employee`/`app.suspend_employee` (HRT-274), `app.request_onboarding_access_revocation`'s own case-coupling (HRT-277), and `ISS-2026-072`'s shared-primitive fix, since a real fix likely touches all three together.

**Independent re-derivation note (this same checkpoint, `CG-S12-HRT-022`):** re-confirmed live, independently, via a different reproduction than the one recorded above (a forged `request.jwt.claims` session as the terminated worker calling `app.get_my_employee_profile`, which succeeds and returns the row, and `app.has_active_tenant_membership` for that identity, which still returns `true`) before this entry was found already recorded in the working tree — satisfying `BUILD_EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md` §5.3's "never accepted from a citation alone" standard a second, independent way. See `ISS-2026-105`/`ISS-2026-106` below for this checkpoint's own two further, separate findings from the same Critical Flow 1 (Employee → Time → Payroll → Finance) live walkthrough.

**Resolution (HRT-295, `2026-08-15`): option (a) of the three this entry's own Disposition named, implemented and live-proven.** `supabase/migrations/20260731230000_couple_hris_employee_lifecycle_to_platform_identity_hrt295.sql` makes `app.terminate_employee`/`app.suspend_employee` call `app.transition_user_status` directly for their own linked `app.users` row, in the SAME transaction as the HR-side status flip — `terminate_employee` couples to `'revoked'` (permanent, until a genuine rehire reactivates via the new `app.reactivate_user_after_rehire`, see `ISS-2026-108` below); `suspend_employee` couples to `'suspended'` (a temporary authority freeze). The `ISS-2026-072` role_assignments-cascade prerequisite this entry's own Disposition named was fixed FIRST, centrally, in the same migration (see `ISS-2026-072`'s own partial-resolution note above) — both `'suspended'` and `'revoked'` now strip every active `app.role_assignments` row for the identity, not merely `'revoked'`. Atomic: if the Platform-side transition is blocked (e.g. the identity is the tenant's last active `tenant_admin`, `ISS-2026-072`'s own pre-existing `last_critical_admin` guard, now reachable from HRIS for the first time), the WHOLE termination/suspension rolls back rather than leaving a half-state. `app.reactivate_employee` (HRT-274's own existing un-suspend path, checked per this task's own instruction to look for one) mirrors the coupling on the way back, restoring `app.users.status` to `'active'` without auto-restoring the stripped role_assignments (a real grant stays a separate, explicit, governed act, matching the identical principle `ISS-2026-108`'s own resolution establishes for rehire). **Live-reconfirmed, same shape as this entry's own original reproduction:** `scripts/db-tests/hris-employee-master.sql`'s new regression block seeds a worker holding a real, unrelated `HRS:View` role assignment, activates them with zero Onboarding/Offboarding case ever created, suspends (role_assignments stripped, `evaluate_permission` denied, but `has_active_tenant_membership` deliberately preserved — a temporary freeze, not an exit), reactivates (role NOT auto-restored), re-grants explicitly, then terminates — `app.tenant_user_identities`/`app.role_assignments` both correctly revoked, `evaluate_permission` denied, `has_active_tenant_membership=false`, and a genuine forged-session `app.get_my_employee_profile` self-read (the exact alternate reproduction this entry's own "Independent re-derivation note" used) returns zero rows both via a plain call and a real forged `request.jwt.claims` session. **Status `RESOLVED`** at `HRT-295` — was High severity while open (a live-verified, real, system-wide authority-retention gap).

**Tier C review follow-up (`2026-08-15`, test-coverage gap only, not a functional defect):** the `HRT-295` resolution text above states three separate times that terminating/suspending a tenant's last active `tenant_admin` is "now reachable from HRIS for the first time" via the `last_critical_admin` guard — but no db-test anywhere in the repository ever actually drove `app.terminate_employee`/`app.suspend_employee` against a tenant's last active `tenant_admin`. Independently live-verified the underlying mechanism IS genuinely correct before adding coverage (tenant `hrmemp1`): terminating/suspending a linked identity holding the tenant's only active `tenant_admin` membership both correctly raise `last_critical_admin`, with the whole transaction rolling back atomically (`employees.lifecycle_status`/`record_version` and the linked `app.users.status` all provably unchanged afterward) — a normal termination then succeeds once a second active admin exists. `scripts/db-tests/hris-employee-master.sql`'s new regression block (this Tier C follow-up) closes the coverage gap so a future regression here would actually be caught by `pnpm run db:test`. No code change — the mechanism was already correct.

### ISS-2026-105 — HRIS/Ticketing Integrated Verification (HRT-294): the attendance→payroll "regular minutes" fallback double-counts any work_date that carries an approved Overtime request but no Timesheet entry, producing a wrong, silently-inflated gross/net pay figure that reaches a FINALIZED payroll run and its Finance handoff (OPEN, Critical)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the `CG-S12-HRT-022` (Prompt 294) integrated-verification checkpoint's own Critical Flow 1 (Employee → Time → Payroll → Finance) live walkthrough — a cross-capability failure between Overtime/Timesheet (HRT-281) and Payroll Foundation (HRT-282) that neither capability's own isolated `db-tests/*.sql` file could catch: `scripts/db-tests/hris-payroll.sql`'s own ISS-2026-074 attendance-fallback regression fixture (grep-confirmed, its own lines ~420-465) exercises a raw attendance session with ZERO overtime requests and zero timesheet entries for that employee/period; `scripts/db-tests/hris-overtime-timesheet.sql` tests overtime reconciliation against Attendance in isolation, never composed with a live payroll run. Neither file ever seeds an employee with BOTH a real attendance session AND an approved overtime request on the SAME work_date and then runs payroll — the exact composition this checkpoint's own end-to-end chain requires and exercises.

**Code:** `app._resolve_payroll_time_inputs_for_period` (`supabase/migrations/20260731000000_create_hris_payroll_foundation.sql:987-1049`, unchanged by any later HRT-282 Tier C fix) computes `v_covered_dates` (the set of work_dates to EXCLUDE from the attendance-session regular-minutes fallback) exclusively by joining `app.payroll_time_inputs.source_entry_ids` back to `app.timesheet_entries.work_date` (`:1022-1027`) — it never references `app.payroll_time_inputs.source_overtime_request_ids` (a real, populated column, `supabase/migrations/20260730980000_create_hris_overtime_timesheet.sql:668`, populated at `:2909`/`:3553`/`:3587`) or `app.overtime_requests.work_date` anywhere. The attendance-session fallback query itself (`:1030-1042`) then sums `greatest(0, round(extract(epoch from (s.effective_clock_out_at - s.effective_clock_in_at)) / 60))` — the FULL, UNCAPPED raw clock span, not the policy/shift's own standard-workday minutes — for every `payroll_input_status='approved'` session whose `work_date` is not in `v_covered_dates`. A work_date covered ONLY by an approved overtime request (no timesheet entry) is therefore counted TWICE: once via its own full raw attendance span inside `regular_minutes`, and again via `overtime_weekday_minutes` from the same approved overtime request — contradicting this same migration's own decision-2 comment (HRT-282 build log, "a work_date contributes through exactly one source, never both, by construction") and its own header claim in `20260731000000`, which is true only for the timesheet-covered case, never the overtime-request-covered one.

**Live reproduction (fresh disposable database, all 221 migrations applied, `CG-S12-HRT-022`'s own Critical Flow 1 fixture):** one real employee, a published shift (08:00-17:00), a real attendance session on 2026-09-10 with `effective_clock_in_at`=08:00 and `effective_clock_out_at`=19:30 local (raw span 690 minutes), a real, approved overtime request for the SAME day covering 17:00-19:30 (150 minutes, `reconciliation_status='matched'` against that SAME attendance session, `eligible_minutes=150`, `approved_minutes=150`). No timesheet entry exists for 2026-09-10. After `app.approve_attendance_for_payroll_input` and `app.generate_payroll_time_input` (both real, governed RPCs) and `app.freeze_payroll_period_inputs`, the frozen `app.payroll_input_snapshots` row shows `regular_minutes=2760` (correct value: `2610` — four other days at their real shift-matching spans, 2026-09-10 counted at its full 690-minute span instead of the shift's real 540-minute regular portion) and `overtime_weekday_minutes=150` (separately, on top). A real `hourly_rate` (50,000 IDR/hour) payroll component then calculated `quantity=46.00` hours, `amount=2,300,000.00 IDR` — the correct, non-double-counted figure is `43.50` hours / `2,175,000.00 IDR`, a **125,000 IDR overstatement for one employee, one pay period, from one overtime day** (overtime minutes are not currently separately paid at all, decision 8 of `HRT-282`'s own build log — the entire 150-minute overstatement is pure double-count, not a legitimate second payment). This figure was carried, unchanged and unflagged, through `app.calculate_payroll_run` → `app.finalize_payroll_run` (a real payslip generated with the same wrong `net_pay=2,300,000.00`) → `app.prepare_finance_payroll_disbursement_handoff_from_payroll_run` (the real Finance handoff batch, `gross_earnings_total`/`net_pay_total`=2,300,000.00, GL-line sum = payment-instruction sum = run-result net, all three internally reconciled to the SAME wrong number) — the reconciliation mechanism itself is structurally sound (see `HRT-282`'s own claims, re-confirmed clean by this checkpoint), but it faithfully propagates an upstream figure that is already wrong before Payroll ever touches it.

**Disposition:** Not fixed by this checkpoint — per this checkpoint's own charter ("No planned feature schema... allow only minimal registered verification-defect repair"), the correct fix (excluding a work_date from the attendance-session regular-minutes fallback whenever EITHER a timesheet entry OR an approved overtime request already covers it, and/or capping the attendance-fallback contribution at the effective shift/policy standard-workday minutes rather than the raw clock span) touches `app._resolve_payroll_time_inputs_for_period`'s own core algorithm, a function nine other functions and every already-`VERIFIED` payroll fixture depend on — a genuine, bounded-but-not-trivial repair requiring its own regression proof, not a review-note-driven one-line change. **Status `OPEN`**, Critical severity (a live-verified, exact, real monetary miscalculation reaching a FINALIZED payroll run and its Finance handoff with zero warning/flag anywhere — the business rule this task's own §24/§33 names explicitly: "Critical identity/payroll/privacy/customer/support/link failure blocks the phase regardless of average pass rate") — owner: Prompt 295 (Hardening), scoped to `app._resolve_payroll_time_inputs_for_period` (HRT-282) with a required regression fixture combining a real attendance session and a real approved overtime request on the identical work_date (the exact composition this entry's own reproduction used and no existing db-test file exercises).

**Resolution (HRT-295, `2026-08-15`): the exclusion half of this entry's own Disposition, implemented and live-proven; the capping half evaluated and deliberately NOT implemented, for concrete reasons found by reading the real schema, not asserted from principle.** `supabase/migrations/20260731240000_harden_hris_attendance_payroll_double_count_backdate_hrt295.sql` extends `app._resolve_payroll_time_inputs_for_period` (`CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION`, identical signature) so `v_covered_dates` is now the UNION of work_dates reachable via approved `app.timesheet_entries` (unchanged) AND work_dates reachable via an APPROVED `app.overtime_requests` row through this SAME active `app.payroll_time_inputs` row's own `source_overtime_request_ids` — mirroring, predicate-for-predicate, how the pre-existing timesheet-entries exclusion already works (`te.status='approved'` mirrored by `ot.status='approved'`, same `pti`/`tp` join shape, same period-overlap and work_date-range guards). Step 2's own attendance-fallback query text is byte-for-byte unchanged — only the meaning of `v_covered_dates` widened. This migration's own header records why capping (the other half of this entry's own Disposition) was evaluated and rejected: (1) two INDEPENDENT, separately-configurable "standard workday" figures already exist in this schema and can genuinely differ (`app.attendance_policy_versions.workday_start_time`/`workday_end_time` vs `app.overtime_policy_versions.standard_workday_minutes` — `scripts/db-tests/hris-overtime-timesheet.sql`'s own pre-existing fixture sets these to different values, 540 vs 480, for the SAME tenant), a genuine undecided design fork, not a one-line addition; (2) a blind cap is not double-count-safe even if that ambiguity were resolved — a work_date entirely approved as a full-day holiday overtime shift would still have its capped "regular" portion counted a SECOND time on top of its own full `overtime_holiday_minutes`, a smaller-magnitude but still-real instance of the SAME defect this fix closes. The exclusion fix alone is unconditionally double-count-safe in every case, including that one, and is therefore the primary, sufficient, and only fix implemented. **Live-reconfirmed, same shape as this entry's own reproduction:** `scripts/db-tests/hris-payroll.sql`'s own new ISS-2026-105 regression block seeds a fresh employee (emp3, `pay1` tenant) with a real attendance session on 2026-10-05 (raw span 690 minutes, 08:00-19:30) and a real, approved overtime request for the SAME day (17:00-19:30, 150 minutes, `reconciliation_status='matched'` against that SAME session), no timesheet entry, PLUS a second, uncontested attendance-only day (2026-10-06, 480 minutes, no overtime, no timesheet) to prove the ordinary fallback path is untouched — run through the real `generate_payroll_time_input`/`freeze_payroll_period_inputs`/`create_payroll_run`/`calculate_payroll_run` pipeline. Frozen snapshot: `regular_minutes=480` (day B only — day A's own session is verified ABSENT from `source_attendance_session_ids`, a mechanism-level proof, not an arithmetic coincidence), `overtime_weekday_minutes=150` (unaffected); an explicit no-double-count invariant asserts the raw uncapped sum of every approved session in the period (1170) minus the frozen `regular_minutes` (480) equals exactly day A's own 690-minute span, never partially. The real calculated run: `gross_earnings=net_pay=400,000 IDR` (480min/60×50,000), never the pre-fix-shaped 975,000 IDR (1170 min) the original double-count would have produced. Both `record_manual_attendance_event` calls this fixture uses to seed the two historical attendance days depend on the `ISS-2026-106` fix below, fixed first in the same migration for exactly this reason. **Status `RESOLVED`** at `HRT-295` — was Critical severity while open (a live-verified, exact, real monetary miscalculation reaching a FINALIZED payroll run and its Finance handoff with zero warning anywhere).

**Tier C review follow-up (`2026-08-15`): the `HRT-295` resolution above was PARTIALLY CORRECT — the double-count was genuinely closed, but the exclusion mechanism it used converted the defect into a real, larger-magnitude, opposite-direction UNDERSTATEMENT for the identical reproduction shape, independently live-reproduced by all four Tier C review lenses (spec-compliance, security, correctness, integration) before this fix.** This entry's own original text (above) already defines "correct" for its own live reproduction explicitly — the overtime-covered day should be paid "at the shift's real 540-minute REGULAR portion," not zero. The `HRT-295` fix instead excluded such a day from the attendance-fallback ENTIRELY (0 minutes), so a genuine attendance session plus a genuine, HR-approved overtime request on the same work_date (no timesheet entry) — not an edge case, the exact scenario this finding's own severity was judged against — paid its real, worked, non-overtime minutes as zero. Re-derived independently on the existing `emp3` fixture (`scripts/db-tests/hris-payroll.sql`): day A's real regular portion is 690−150=540 minutes, correct total regular_minutes=1020 (540+480), correct gross/net=850,000 IDR — the `HRT-295` fix's own shipped code produced 480 minutes / 400,000 IDR, a 450,000 IDR under-statement, larger in magnitude than the 125,000 IDR overstatement it replaced. **Resolved for real** by `supabase/migrations/20260731280000_harden_hris_payroll_overtime_regular_minutes_hrt295_tierc.sql` (additive `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION`, next free timestamp — `20260731240000` itself was left unedited, per this checkpoint's own standing "never edit an already-shipped migration" convention): `v_covered_dates` (full exclusion) reverts to timesheet-covered dates ONLY, exactly as the function's own pre-`ISS-2026-105` scope always had it (a timesheet entry's own `approved_minutes` genuinely IS the correct, complete regular figure for that day). A work_date covered by an approved overtime request but no timesheet entry is no longer excluded outright — the attendance-fallback query now subtracts that day's own real, already-claimed `approved_minutes` from the session's raw span, floored at zero (`greatest(0, raw_span - overtime_minutes)`), using the SAME `approved_minutes` column already trusted elsewhere in this function for the overtime totals themselves — never a second, independent "standard workday minutes" config value, and never the "capping" alternative this same migration's own header evaluated and rejected (that alternative was rejected because it either used an ambiguous config source or was not double-count-safe for a full-day-holiday shift; subtraction-from-the-real-approved-figure has neither problem, since `greatest(0, ...)` correctly floors to zero exactly when a day's minutes are fully consumed by its own already-counted overtime). **Live-reconfirmed, independently, on two fixtures the emp3 fixture never used** (different tenant/rate/dates each): `pay1`/`emp5` (`scripts/db-tests/hris-payroll.sql`, new block) — raw span 735 min, overtime approved capped by policy at 180 min (not the raw 735−baseline figure), correct regular=555, confirmed live; and an ad hoc fixture used during this review (735 raw / 120 requested / 180 approved after reconciliation) confirming the fix is keyed to the day's own real `approved_minutes`, never a coincidental config value. The existing `emp3` fixture's own assertions (`scripts/db-tests/hris-payroll.sql`) are corrected in place to their genuinely correct values (regular_minutes=1020, gross/net=850,000 IDR) — the fixture previously asserted the WRONG number (480/400,000) as ground truth, now fixed. **Status `RESOLVED`** at this Tier C follow-up (`20260731280000`) — the Critical severity this finding was originally judged at is now genuinely, live-reproducibly closed; the interim `HRT-295` fix is superseded, not merely supplemented.

### ISS-2026-106 — HRIS/Ticketing Integrated Verification (HRT-294): `app._ingest_attendance_event` stamps `work_date`/`raw_clock_in_at`/`raw_clock_out_at` from `clock_timestamp()` (real server "now"), never from the caller-supplied historical timestamp — both `manual_hr` (HR backdated correction) and `device_import` (legacy attendance batch import) are structurally unable to represent any day other than the day the RPC is actually called (OPEN, High)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the `CG-S12-HRT-022` (Prompt 294) integrated-verification checkpoint's own Critical Flow 1 live walkthrough, while attempting to build a real multi-day historical attendance fixture via `app.record_manual_attendance_event` — a cross-cutting defect neither `HRT-278`'s own db-test file (`scripts/db-tests/hris-attendance.sql`, whose own `record_manual_attendance_event` calls all pass `now() - interval '1 hour'`, never a genuinely different calendar day) nor `HRT-282`'s own db-test file could catch as a NAMED defect — though `HRT-282`'s own `scripts/db-tests/hris-payroll.sql:433` independently HIT the same underlying behavior and worked around it with a raw `UPDATE app.attendance_sessions SET raw_clock_in_at = ..., work_date = ...` immediately after calling the RPC, treating it silently as test-fixture friction rather than registering it as a product defect.

**Code:** `app._ingest_attendance_event` (`supabase/migrations/20260730900000_create_hris_attendance.sql:581-751`) declares `v_now timestamptz := clock_timestamp();` (`:603`) and uses `v_now` — never the function's own `p_client_reported_at` parameter — for `v_work_date := app.resolve_attendance_workday(v_now, ...)` (`:672`), `attendance_sessions.raw_clock_in_at` at INSERT (`:~715`), and `attendance_sessions.raw_clock_out_at` at UPDATE (`:721`). `p_client_reported_at` is stored ONLY into `attendance_events.client_reported_at` — inert, audit-only, never read back by any exception-detection, work_date-bucketing, or payroll-time-resolution logic anywhere. `app.record_manual_attendance_event` (`:976`) and `app.commit_attendance_device_import_job` (`:1630`, passing `(v_payload ->> 'event_at')::timestamptz` at its own `_ingest_attendance_event` call site) both reach this same shared engine with no override — every manual/imported historical timestamp a caller supplies is captured for the record but has ZERO functional effect.

**Live reproduction (fresh disposable database, all 221 migrations applied, isolated minimal repro plus the full Critical Flow 1 fixture):** `app.record_manual_attendance_event(..., 'clock_in', '2026-09-07 08:00:00+07'::timestamptz, 'backfill test', ...)` followed by the matching `clock_out` call at `'2026-09-07 17:00:00+07'` — on `2026-08-15` (today) — produced a session with `work_date='2026-08-15'` (today, not the claimed `2026-09-07`) and `raw_clock_in_at='2026-08-15 05:04:46+00'`/`raw_clock_out_at='2026-08-15 05:04:46+00'` (the real wall-clock instant the RPC ran, ~9 seconds apart, not the claimed 8-hour 08:00-17:00 span); `attendance_events.client_reported_at` correctly held the claimed `2026-09-07 01:00:00+00`/`2026-09-07 10:00:00+00` values, proving the data is captured but functionally discarded. A second manual entry for a genuinely different claimed historical date (`2026-09-08`) on the SAME real calendar day was rejected outright with `duplicate_workday_session: employee ... already has an attendance session for 2026-08-15` — the SAME real date both calls actually resolved to — confirming this is not a cosmetic timestamp-precision issue but a structural block: **a single `manual_hr`/`device_import` caller cannot record more than one attendance day per real calendar day**, regardless of how many distinct historical dates it claims to represent. A real device-import batch of a legacy system's prior 30 days of clock data would, on this evidence, either fail on every row after the first (`duplicate_workday_session`) or (if spread across 30 real calendar days of committing) silently misattribute every imported event to the wrong `work_date`/`raw_clock_*` times, corrupting exception detection and any downstream payroll-time consumer.

**Disposition:** Not fixed by this checkpoint — the correct behavior (using `p_client_reported_at` as the authoritative source for `work_date`/`raw_clock_in_at`/`raw_clock_out_at` on the `manual_hr` and `device_import` channels specifically, while correctly continuing to use `clock_timestamp()` for the live self-service `mobile_web`/`kiosk` channels, where trusting a client-claimed time would reopen the exact anti-spoofing gap decision 10 of `HRT-278`'s own build log deliberately closed) is a real, channel-conditional behavioral change to the single shared ingestion engine every attendance write path calls, needing its own regression proof across all four channels — not a bounded, review-note-driven one-line fix. **Status `OPEN`**, High severity (device-import and HR manual backdated correction — two capabilities `HRT-278`'s own build log names as real, delivered, decision-6/-8-disclosed features — are structurally non-functional for their own stated purpose; the live consequence is silent data corruption of `work_date`/exception-detection timing, not privilege escalation or a direct financial miscalculation on its own, though it is the same class of upstream time-data defect this checkpoint's own `ISS-2026-105` finding shows CAN reach a finalized payroll figure once combined with real usage) — owner: Prompt 295 (Hardening), scoped to `app._ingest_attendance_event` (HRT-278) with a required regression fixture proving a `manual_hr`/`device_import` entry for a genuinely different historical work_date lands on that claimed date, not `clock_timestamp()`'s own real date, for all four channels (`mobile_web`/`kiosk` unchanged, `manual_hr`/`device_import` corrected).

**Resolution (HRT-295, `2026-08-15`): built exactly as this entry's own Disposition specified.** `supabase/migrations/20260731240000_harden_hris_attendance_payroll_double_count_backdate_hrt295.sql` extends `app._ingest_attendance_event` (`CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION`, identical signature) with a new `v_effective_at` value, computed once, branching on `p_source_channel` — the exact parameter this function already gates other behavior on (channel allowlist bypass, geofence exemption). For `manual_hr`/`device_import`, `v_effective_at` resolves to the caller-supplied, already-required `p_client_reported_at`; for `mobile_web`/`kiosk`, it resolves to `v_now` (`clock_timestamp()`), UNCHANGED. `v_effective_at` replaces `v_now` at exactly the three call sites this entry's own Code section named — `v_work_date := app.resolve_attendance_workday(v_effective_at, ...)`, `attendance_sessions.raw_clock_in_at` at INSERT, `attendance_sessions.raw_clock_out_at` at UPDATE — plus the clock-out branch's own `impossible_ordering` guard (`v_effective_at < v_session.raw_clock_in_at`, was `v_now < ...`), required so the guard keeps comparing the same effective-time basis on both sides once `raw_clock_in_at` can itself be a backdated value. `server_received_at` stays `v_now` unconditionally on every channel, unchanged — this fix touches only the three named fields, deliberately not the effective-attendance-policy resolution (still `v_now`-based, disclosed as an out-of-scope, separate design question in the migration's own header). **Live-reconfirmed, both negative shapes from this entry's own reproduction closed:** `scripts/db-tests/hris-attendance.sql`'s own new ISS-2026-106 regression block drives a fresh employee (emp4, `att1` tenant) through two DIFFERENT claimed historical workdays (2024-03-04, 2024-03-05) via `app.record_manual_attendance_event` on the SAME real calendar day — both now land on their own claimed `work_date`/`raw_clock_in_at`/`raw_clock_out_at` exactly (previously both would have collided on `duplicate_workday_session`, the exact structural block this entry's own reproduction proved); `client_reported_at` stays correctly populated and `server_received_at` stays the real server clock, confirmed unchanged; a claimed clock-out BEFORE the claimed clock-in on a THIRD historical day correctly raises `impossible_ordering`, proving the ordering guard now evaluates the correct (claimed) time basis. A parallel block proves `mobile_web` is genuinely UNCHANGED: a live self-service clock-in with a spoofed `p_client_reported_at` claiming `2019-06-15` still resolves `work_date` to the real server "today" — the anti-spoofing property decision 10 of `HRT-278`'s own build log established is unaffected. A third block proves `device_import` also lands a staged `event_at` on a genuinely different historical work_date through the real staged-import pipeline (`stage_import_rows`/`validate_attendance_device_import_row`/`commit_attendance_device_import_job`). This fix was implemented and verified FIRST (before `ISS-2026-105`, in the same migration) specifically so `ISS-2026-105`'s own regression fixture could seed its two attendance days via real `record_manual_attendance_event` calls rather than the raw-`UPDATE`-after-the-fact workaround the pre-existing `ISS-2026-074` fixture needed. **Status `RESOLVED`** at `HRT-295` — was High severity while open (device-import and HR manual backdated correction were structurally non-functional for their own stated purpose).

### ISS-2026-107 — HRIS/Ticketing Integrated Verification (HRT-294): the governed recruitment→onboarding conversion (`start_onboarding_case`) never creates a formal `app.employee_position_assignments` row — every hire made through the chartered job-offer→employee pipeline consumes ZERO position headcount, silently defeating `app.publish_job_vacancy`'s own capacity gate; live-proven to allow over-hire past a position's declared capacity (OPEN, High)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the `CG-S12-HRT-022` (Prompt 294) integrated-verification checkpoint's own Critical Flow 2 (Recruitment → Onboarding → Employee/User conversion) live walkthrough — a cross-capability failure between Recruitment/ATS (HRT-276), Onboarding/Offboarding (HRT-277), and Organization/Position Linkage (HRT-275) that no single capability's own isolated `db-tests/*.sql` file could catch: `hris-recruitment-ats.sql` tests vacancy headcount-vs-capacity enforcement using directly-published vacancies, never through an actual completed hire; `hris-onboarding-offboarding.sql` asserts the converted employee's company/branch/department org linkage but never queries `app.employee_position_assignments`; `hris-organization-position-linkage.sql` tests `propose_employee_position_assignment`/`decide_employee_position_assignment` directly, never composed with the recruitment pipeline. Only a real, live, end-to-end walkthrough from a published vacancy (bound to a real, capacity-tracked `app.positions` row) through an accepted offer and a governed `start_onboarding_case` conversion exposes the gap.

**Code:** `app.start_onboarding_case`'s `job_offer` source branch (current definition: `supabase/migrations/20260730890000_harden_hris_onboarding_offboarding_review_fixes.sql:556-834`, its `job_offer` branch's `create_employee_draft` call at `:664-683`, unchanged since the Implement stage) calls `app.create_employee_draft(...)` passing only `p_position_title` (free text, `coalesce(nullif(trim(p_position_title), ''), v_offer_version.title)`) — it never passes, resolves, or otherwise touches `app.job_vacancies.position_id` (the REAL, capacity-tracked position the vacancy/offer was actually for), and never calls `app.propose_employee_position_assignment`/`app.decide_employee_position_assignment` (HRT-275's own governed linkage RPCs). `app.create_employee_draft` itself only ever writes `app.employees.position_title`/`manager_employee_id` as plain columns (`supabase/migrations/20260730830000_create_hris_employee_master.sql`, `create_employee_draft` body) — `app.employees.position_id` has exactly ONE writer anywhere in the repository, `app.sync_employee_current_assignment_cache` (`supabase/migrations/20260730840000_create_hris_organization_position_linkage.sql:577-628`, its own comment at `:628` states outright: "the ONLY code path that ever writes app.employees.position_id"), reachable only via `decide_employee_position_assignment`/`activate_due_employee_position_assignments` — neither of which `start_onboarding_case` or any other HRT-277 function ever calls. Position headcount/capacity enforcement (`app.publish_job_vacancy`, `supabase/migrations/20260730860000_create_hris_recruitment_ats.sql:879-884`) counts exclusively from `app.employee_position_assignments` (`app.count_position_active_primary_headcount`, `supabase/migrations/20260730840000_create_hris_organization_position_linkage.sql:555-569`: `select count(*) ... from app.employee_position_assignments where position_id = ... and assignment_type = 'primary' and status = 'active' ...`) — a table the conversion path never inserts into.

**Live reproduction (fresh disposable database, all 221 migrations applied):** a real position `POS-F2` (capacity=2) was created; a vacancy was published against it (headcount=1, correctly checked against the position's real remaining capacity at publish time); a real candidate applied through the genuinely public intake surface, was interviewed, received and accepted a real offer; `app.start_onboarding_case(..., 'job_offer', ...)` converted the accepted offer into a real, linked `app.employees` row (`master_record_id=414cec85-45ea-44cc-bf6e-454ea6183d43`, `full_name='Grace Hopper'`, `position_title='Software Engineer F2'`). Direct query immediately after: `select count(*) from app.employee_position_assignments where master_record_id = '414cec85-...'` → **0**; `app.employees.position_id` for this row → **NULL**; `app.count_position_active_primary_headcount(POS-F2, daterange(current_date,current_date,'[]'))` → **0** (of capacity 2), despite one real, active, onboarded-and-finalized employee genuinely occupying that position. **Over-hire proof:** a SECOND vacancy was then published against the SAME `POS-F2` (capacity 2) requesting headcount=2 — `app.publish_job_vacancy` **succeeded** (`vacancy status='open'`), because its own capacity gate computed 2 full seats still remaining. Three more accepted offers through this same governed pipeline would leave the position with 4 real incumbents against a declared capacity of 2, entirely undetected by the one gate the system provides for this purpose.

**Disposition:** Not fixed by this checkpoint — per `BUILD_EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md` §3.3 and this checkpoint's own charter ("No planned feature schema... allow only minimal registered verification-defect repair"), the correct fix (having `start_onboarding_case`'s `job_offer`/`direct_hire` branches call `app.propose_employee_position_assignment` + an auto-approved or routed `app.decide_employee_position_assignment` against the vacancy's own `position_id`, so the conversion both consumes real headcount and populates `app.employees.position_id` the same way every other HRT-275 hire path does) is a genuine design decision (auto-approve vs. route through the same PLT-123 finalize-approval step; which actor is recorded as the decider; whether a `direct_hire` case without any `position_id` input at all should require one), not a bounded review-note fix, and touches a function multiple already-`VERIFIED` fixtures depend on. **Status `OPEN`**, High severity (a live-verified, 100%-reproducible integrity gap in the DEFAULT and ONLY behavior of the system's own primary, chartered hiring pipeline — not an edge case — silently defeating a declared, enforced governance control with zero error or warning) — owner: Prompt 295 (Hardening), scoped to `app.start_onboarding_case` (HRT-277) and its interaction with `app.propose_employee_position_assignment`/`app.decide_employee_position_assignment` (HRT-275), with a required regression fixture proving a job-offer-sourced (and a direct-hire-sourced, when a `position_id` is supplied) conversion both populates `app.employee_position_assignments` and correctly reduces `app.count_position_active_primary_headcount`'s own remaining-capacity figure.

**Resolution (HRT-295, `2026-08-15`): the job_offer branch, and ONLY the job_offer branch, built as this entry's own Disposition specified.** `supabase/migrations/20260731250000_couple_recruitment_onboarding_position_headcount_hrt295.sql` (`CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION`, identical signature) resolves the accepted offer's own real position (`job_offer.application_id` → `job_applications.vacancy_id` → `job_vacancies.position_id`, not-null, immutable) once the employee draft exists, then calls `app.propose_employee_position_assignment` followed immediately by `app.decide_employee_position_assignment` (`'approve'`) in the SAME transaction — a real, active, primary assignment row now backs every job-offer-sourced hire, and `app.employees.position_id` is populated via `app.sync_employee_current_assignment_cache`, the ONE code path that ever writes it. **Design decisions, made explicitly (this entry's own named open questions):** (1) auto-approve, not a second PLT-123-routed human gate — the underlying offer already completed its own real approval before it could reach `accepted`; a second independent approval on the resulting, structurally-guaranteed-single assignment would be redundant scope creep. (2) the decider recorded is the SAME actor already authorized (`HRS:Create`) to run the whole conversion — deliberately not a new "system" bypass identity (none exists anywhere else in this repository, and inventing one would itself be the scope-creep/privilege risk); the disclosed, deliberate consequence is that completing a job_offer-sourced conversion now also requires that actor to hold `HRS:Edit`/`HRS:Approve`, exactly the authority a manual propose+decide pair already requires everywhere else in this system — a caller lacking it gets a clear `insufficient_authority` and the WHOLE conversion (including the employee draft) rolls back, never a partial hire with unconsumed headcount. (3) `direct_hire` is left UNCHANGED and OPEN — that branch has no `position_id` reachable anywhere in `start_onboarding_case`'s own signature (only free-text `position_title`); adding one would be a new parameter/capability outside this checkpoint's bounded-repair charter, so this narrower gap stays disclosed rather than silently expanded around. (4) `existing_employee` (transfer/offboarding/rehire) was never in scope — it never creates a new hire, and `app.propose_employee_position_assignment` is already its own correct, separately-callable governed path. **Live-reconfirmed** via `scripts/db-tests/hris-onboarding-offboarding.sql`'s own new end-to-end regression: a fresh `capacity=1` position is published as a vacancy; a real candidate applies through the genuinely public intake surface (`app.submit_public_job_application`), is interviewed, and accepts a real offer; an `HRS:Create`-only actor is denied completing the conversion (leaving zero partial employee/case rows); the SAME conversion by an actor holding Create+Edit+Approve succeeds and leaves exactly ONE active primary `app.employee_position_assignments` row against the real position, with `app.employees.position_id` populated and `app.count_position_active_primary_headcount` correctly reporting the seat as consumed; a second vacancy against the now-fully-occupied position then correctly fails `app.publish_job_vacancy` with `vacancy_headcount_exceeds_position_capacity` — the exact over-hire this entry's own live reproduction proved previously succeeded silently. **Status `RESOLVED`** for the job_offer path at `HRT-295` — was High severity while open (the DEFAULT and ONLY behavior of the system's own primary, chartered hiring pipeline silently defeated its own declared capacity governance with zero warning). The `direct_hire` gap (design decision 3 above) remains a separate, narrower, disclosed OPEN item — no position input exists on that path today, so no headcount can be consumed or omitted either way; not tracked under a new issue number since it was never a live-reproduced defect, only a structural absence this entry's own Disposition already named.

### ISS-2026-108 — HRIS/Ticketing Integrated Verification (HRT-294): a rehired employee's Platform/ESS-MSS access can never be restored to their own pre-existing linked identity — `app.rehire_employee` correctly reactivates the HR employee record without duplicating it, but the linked `app.users` row stays permanently `revoked` with no RPC anywhere in the repository able to reverse it, and re-provisioning explicitly rejects it (OPEN, High)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the `CG-S12-HRT-022` (Prompt 294) integrated-verification checkpoint's own Critical Flow 2 live walkthrough, specifically the task's own explicitly-named offboarding/rehire spot-check ("confirming re-hire correctly reactivates rather than duplicating identity"). The employee-identity half of that claim is CONFIRMED CLEAN (see below) — but proving it required actually driving the identity through a full, realistic offboard→rehire cycle, which is exactly what exposed this second, deeper defect that neither `HRT-274`'s own `hris-employee-master.sql` (tests `rehire_employee` against a bare-terminated employee with no linked-user revocation history) nor `HRT-277`'s own `hris-onboarding-offboarding.sql` (tests access revocation and rehire as two separate, non-composed assertion blocks, never chained through a real re-provisioning attempt afterward) ever composes.

**Code:** `app.rehire_employee` (`supabase/migrations/20260731190000_harden_hris_audit_reason_redaction_batch_293.sql:1087-1144`) only updates `app.employees.lifecycle_status`/`terminate_reason`/`employment_end_date` — it never touches `app.employees.user_id` or `app.users.status`, by design (correctly does not duplicate the identity). But `app.request_onboarding_access_revocation` (same file, `:1965-2079`, the HRT-277 §12.8-fixed revocation path) calls `app.transition_user_status(..., 'revoked', ...)` during offboarding, and `app.transition_user_status` (`supabase/migrations/20260716102620_create_users.sql:202`) is granted `EXECUTE` to `service_role` ONLY — a repository-wide grep (`grep -rn "transition_user_status(" supabase/migrations/*.sql`) confirms its SOLE caller anywhere in 221 migrations is that one HRT-277 revocation call site, which only ever passes `'revoked'`, never `'active'`. No `'revoked'→'active'`/`'invited'` transition exists anywhere. `app.invite_user` (`supabase/migrations/20260716102620_create_users.sql`) short-circuits to `return v_existing` for any pre-existing `(tenant_id, auth_user_id)` row regardless of its status — it can never mint a second, fresh `app.users` row for the same real person in the same tenant. `app.request_onboarding_access_provisioning`'s own HRT-277 §12.9 fix (`supabase/migrations/20260730890000_harden_hris_onboarding_offboarding_review_fixes.sql:428`) explicitly rejects any non-`'active'`/`'invited'` target with `target_identity_not_activatable: ... resolve a different target identity, or reactivate the user first` — advice the system provides no RPC to follow.

**Live reproduction (fresh disposable database, all 221 migrations applied, continuing the same identity from `ISS-2026-107`'s own fixture):** the real, onboarded, ESS-access-provisioned employee (`master_record_id=414cec85-...`, linked `app.users.id=bb9122bc-...`, `auth_user_id=...f2006`) was offboarded end-to-end: `request_onboarding_access_revocation` (real reason, real task completion) → `evaluate_permission(HRS,View)` for that identity flipped `true→false` (HRT-277 §12.8's own fix re-confirmed still correctly working, no regression) → `app.terminate_employee` → real PLT-123 finalize approval → case `finalized`. `app.rehire_employee` was then called: **CONFIRMED CLEAN** — `lifecycle_status` returned to `'active'`, the SAME `master_record_id` and SAME `user_id` were preserved, `select count(*) from app.employees where full_name='Grace Hopper'` stayed at exactly 1 (no duplicate identity, no fragmentation). A fresh onboarding case (`source_type='existing_employee'`, the realistic re-onboarding step) was then started for this same rehired employee to restore their access: after completing the prerequisite document task, `app.request_onboarding_access_provisioning(..., p_target_auth_user_id=<the SAME auth_user_id as before>, ...)` was called by a real `HRS:Override`-holding actor — result: `target_identity_not_activatable: user bb9122bc-... has status revoked, access cannot be granted or meaningfully deferred -- resolve a different target identity, or reactivate the user first`. `app.users.status` for this identity remained `'revoked'` with no further call available anywhere in the schema to change it.

**Disposition:** Not fixed by this checkpoint — the correct fix is a genuine, new design decision (e.g. a governed `app.reactivate_user_after_rehire`-shaped RPC, gated at least as strictly as `app.request_onboarding_access_revocation` itself, that transitions `app.users.status`/`app.tenant_user_identities.status` back toward `active`/`invited` specifically when the linked employee has a real, approved `rehire_employee` event — versus loosening `app.transition_user_status` generically, which risks reopening unrelated revocation-permanence guarantees elsewhere), not a bounded, review-note-driven repair, and touches shared Platform identity primitives (PLT-107/110) outside HRT-277's own migration. **Status `OPEN`**, High severity (every REALISTIC rehire — i.e. any employee who went through the system's own correct, designed offboarding access-revocation step, which is the normal case, not an edge case — permanently and irrecoverably loses the ability to ever regain Platform/ESS/MSS access under their original identity, even though `app.rehire_employee` exists specifically to represent "this person is legitimately back"; the system's own error message names the missing remediation step by name and it does not exist) — owner: Prompt 295 (Hardening), scoped to the interaction between `app.rehire_employee` (HRT-274), `app.request_onboarding_access_provisioning`'s own §12.9 activatable-status gate (HRT-277), and `app.transition_user_status` (PLT-110, shared), with a required regression fixture proving a rehired employee's pre-existing linked identity can be restored to working ESS/MSS access without ever creating a second `app.users`/`app.employees` row.

**Resolution (HRT-295, `2026-08-15`): built almost exactly as this entry's own Disposition specified.** `supabase/migrations/20260731230000_couple_hris_employee_lifecycle_to_platform_identity_hrt295.sql` adds `app.reactivate_user_after_rehire` — gated at least as strictly as `app.request_onboarding_access_revocation` (`HRS:Override` + a non-empty reason), callable only for a linked employee whose CURRENT `lifecycle_status` is `active` with a real, on-file `terminated → active` `app.employee_lifecycle_events` row not since superseded by a later revoke. It restores `app.users.status`/`app.tenant_user_identities.status` to `active` (not merely `invited` — the linked identity was already a known, previously-active person, and `invited` would leave them stuck exactly as before: `app.request_onboarding_access_provisioning` grants roles immediately only to an `active` target, deferring for `invited`, with no self-accept RPC anywhere in the repository) via `app.transition_user_status`'s own new, narrowly-added `revoked → active` edge — the ONE necessary trigger-level amendment (`app.enforce_user_status_transition`/`app.enforce_identity_link_transition`), since `app.tenant_user_identities`' own unique `(auth_user_id, tenant_id)` constraint makes "re-invite as a new row" (that table's own original stated design intent) structurally impossible for a real rehire. Per this entry's own explicit warning, `app.transition_user_status` itself is NOT loosened generically — the authorization boundary lives entirely in the new RPC, which remains the ONLY call site anywhere in the repository that ever passes `p_new_status='active'` for a currently-`'revoked'` row. **Design decision:** `app.rehire_employee` (HRT-277) is left unedited and does NOT call the new RPC automatically — the two stay distinct, separately-callable governed steps ("the HR record says this person is back" vs. "their Platform/ESS/MSS access is restored"), mirroring this same capability area's own established separation between case existence and access provisioning. Role_assignments are deliberately NOT auto-restored (a real grant is always a separate, explicit `app.request_onboarding_access_provisioning` call, unchanged and already correct the moment the identity is active again). **Live-reconfirmed**, continuing the SAME "Existing Employee Two" identity `scripts/db-tests/hris-onboarding-offboarding.sql`'s own pre-existing rehire block already drives through a real terminate → rehire cycle: before reactivation, `app.users.status='revoked'`, `has_active_tenant_membership=false`, and a genuine self-read (`app.get_my_employee_profile`) returns zero rows; `HRS:Edit` alone is denied (needs `Override`); after `app.reactivate_user_after_rehire`, `app.users.status`/`app.tenant_user_identities.status` are both `active`, `has_active_tenant_membership=true`, exactly 1 row each in `app.employees`/`app.users`/`app.tenant_user_identities` for this identity (no duplicate anywhere), a second reactivation attempt is rejected, and a real forged-`request.jwt.claims`-session self-read now succeeds. A negative case (`scripts/db-tests/hris-onboarding-offboarding.sql`, "Existing Employee One" — revoked via a cancelled, never-finalized offboarding case, so genuinely never terminated) proves the gate is a real, on-file rehire event, not merely "any revoked identity": `no_rehire_event` fires. **Status `RESOLVED`** at `HRT-295` — was High severity while open (every realistic rehire permanently and irrecoverably lost Platform/ESS/MSS access).

**Tier C review follow-up (`2026-08-15`): the `HRT-295` resolution above built a genuinely correct, genuinely tested database mechanism, but the correctness lens found it was practically UNREACHABLE for a real HR user — no server action or UI caller anywhere in the app ever invoked it, unlike every sibling lifecycle action (`suspendEmployeeAction`/`reactivateEmployeeAction`/`terminateEmployeeAction`), which are all wired to real callsites.** A repo-wide grep for `reactivateUserAfterRehire`/`reactivate_user_after_rehire` across `app/**/*.ts(x)` returned zero matches before this follow-up — the RPC, its service-layer wrapper (`server/mutations/employee.ts`), and a unit test all existed and passed, but an HR user working through the actual product still had no way to restore a rehired employee's access, leaving the live-proven High-severity gap this finding describes practically unresolved for real users even though the database mechanism itself worked (independently re-confirmed: a forged-session `app.create_ticket` write succeeded post-reactivation). Notably, this same `HRT-295` migration batch DID do this exact "wire it, don't leave it dead" step for the lower-severity `ISS-2026-110` fix, but not for this High-severity one. **Resolved** by wiring a new `reactivateUserAfterRehireAction` (`app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/hris/employees/actions.ts`) into the employee detail page (`app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/hris/employees/[masterRecordId]/page.tsx` and `employee-detail-panel.tsx`) — a "Restore Platform access (rehire)" action alongside the employee's other lifecycle actions, gated on `lifecycle_status='active'` (mirroring the RPC's own real precondition; there is no client-visible signal distinguishing "a normal always-active employee" from "a rehired employee whose access is still revoked," so the RPC's own already-tested `no_rehire_event` validation surfaces plainly to the caller when the action does not apply, exactly the same pattern every other occasionally-inapplicable lifecycle action in this same file already uses — never a silent, always-hidden dead feature). No database migration was needed — this was a pure TS/UI wiring gap, not a mechanism defect. **Status `RESOLVED`** at this Tier C follow-up — the mechanism (`HRT-295`) and its real product reachability (this follow-up) are now both genuinely, live-reproducibly closed.

### ISS-2026-109 — HRIS/Ticketing Integrated Verification (HRT-294): a `closed` or `cancelled` ticket is not mutation-inert — `link_ticket_record`/`unlink_ticket_record`/`add_ticket_watcher`/`remove_ticket_watcher` never check ticket status at all, and `reply_to_ticket` only rejects `cancelled`, never `closed`, unlike every sibling ticket-lifecycle RPC in the same capability family (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the `CG-S12-HRT-022` (Prompt 294) integrated-verification checkpoint's own Critical Flow 3 (ticket channels) lens — a cross-capability failure between Ticket Assignment/Escalation (HRT-290/291, which both correctly gate on terminal status) and Typed Linked Records/HRT-286's own watcher and reply RPCs (HRT-286/292, added later, never composed with closure by either capability's own isolated `db-tests/*.sql` file). Independently re-derived live by the synthesis session (not accepted from the lens report alone), against a fresh disposable Postgres 16 database with all 222 migrations applied.

**Code:** `app.link_ticket_record` (`supabase/migrations/20260731170000_create_ticket_linked_records.sql:552-656`) and `app.unlink_ticket_record` (`:665-720`) both lock `app.tickets` (`for update`) but never read `v_ticket.status` anywhere in their bodies. `app.add_ticket_watcher`/`app.remove_ticket_watcher` (final live bodies, `supabase/migrations/20260731110000_harden_ticketing_helpdesk_batch_288_review_fixes.sql:255-395`) have the identical gap. `app.reply_to_ticket` (`supabase/migrations/20260731060000_create_ticketing_internal.sql:1105`) guards only `v_ticket.status = 'cancelled'`, never `'closed'`. Contrast with the established, consistent pattern every OTHER ticket-lifecycle RPC in the same family follows: `app.escalate_ticket` (`20260731160000_create_ticket_escalation.sql:1161-1163`), `app.claim_ticket`/`app.assign_ticket` (`20260731140000_create_ticket_assignment.sql:725-726`/`925-926`), `app.auto_route_ticket` (`:1096-1097`), `app.transfer_ticket_queue`/`app.update_ticket_classification` (`20260731060000_create_ticketing_internal.sql:1488`/`1550`), `app.assign_helpdesk_ticket` (`20260731100000_extend_ticketing_helpdesk_channel.sql:1254+`) — all explicitly reject `status in ('closed','cancelled')` with `invalid_transition`.

**Live reproduction:** a real internal ticket in tenant `esc1` (`scripts/db-tests/ticketing-escalation.sql`'s own fixture) was driven through the full lifecycle (`new`→`open`→`resolved`→`closed`) via `app.transition_ticket_status`. On the now-`closed` ticket: `app.escalate_ticket`/`app.assign_ticket` correctly raised `invalid_transition` (consistent, CLEAN). But `app.link_ticket_record(ticket, 'user', <a real employee's linked user id>, 'related', ...)` **succeeded**, returning a new `status='active'` link row (`id=ea0fdd6c-54c5-430c-bc6f-b760c0c1942c`) — live-confirmed against a real ticket with no error, no warning, on a ticket the assignment/escalation layer already treats as fully terminal.

**Disposition:** Not fixed by this checkpoint — a genuine design decision is required (should `closed` behave exactly like `cancelled` for all four functions, or does the linked-records/watcher capability need its own, narrower closure semantics — e.g. permitting unlink/remove but not link/add on a closed ticket), touching 4 functions across 2 migrations, not a bounded review-note fix. **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (a closed/cancelled ticket's typed-record linkage set and watcher roster are not frozen at closure and can keep drifting silently, with no re-open event, no SLA-clock resumption, and no re-alert to any queue view — not a tenant/security breach, since `is_ticket_staff`/`_is_ticket_requester_party` authorization is still correctly required throughout) — owner: Prompt 295 (Hardening), scoped to `app.link_ticket_record`/`app.unlink_ticket_record` (HRT-292), `app.add_ticket_watcher`/`app.remove_ticket_watcher` (HRT-286/288), and `app.reply_to_ticket`'s own `closed` gap (HRT-286), with a required regression fixture proving all four correctly reject a closed AND a cancelled ticket.

**Resolution (HRT-295, `2026-08-15`): the design decision this entry's own Disposition named, made explicitly, asymmetric rather than uniform.** `supabase/migrations/20260731270000_harden_ticketing_link_watcher_reply_customer_projection_hrt295.sql` gives `app.link_ticket_record` and `app.add_ticket_watcher` (both "engage something new with this ticket") an explicit `if v_ticket.status in ('closed', 'cancelled') then raise invalid_transition`, mirroring `app.escalate_ticket`/`app.claim_ticket`/`app.assign_ticket`/`app.auto_route_ticket`/`app.transfer_ticket_queue`/`app.update_ticket_classification`/`app.assign_helpdesk_ticket` exactly. `app.unlink_ticket_record` and `app.remove_ticket_watcher` (both "remove an existing engagement" — the exact cleanup case this entry's own text already named as arguably legitimate) are **deliberately left unguarded** — a plain `COMMENT ON FUNCTION` documents the decision in the function itself, zero body/behavior change. The asymmetry is not invented: `app.acknowledge_ticket_escalation`/`app.resolve_ticket_escalation` (`20260731160000_create_ticket_escalation.sql`, already shipped, already `VERIFIED`) never check `v_ticket.status` at all — staff may wind down an existing escalation regardless of the ticket's own terminal state — giving this checkpoint a real, already-correct precedent for both directions inside the SAME capability family rather than a unilateral new rule. `app.reply_to_ticket` joins the create-type group: its own pre-existing `'cancelled'`-only guard is extended with a **new, parallel `ticket_closed` error**, not switched to `invalid_transition` — preserving the function's own already-tested `ticket_cancelled` contract (`scripts/db-tests/ticketing-internal.sql` section 7) byte-for-byte while closing the actual gap. Self-found while implementing (Tier B `docs/standards/RECURRING_DEFECT_TAXONOMY.md` C-04 self-check on this diff's own new status-gating decision): `app.add_ticket_watcher`'s ticket lookup was a plain, unlocked `SELECT` — upgraded to `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, matching every sibling RPC that already gates on ticket status, closing a TOCTOU window the new guard would otherwise have reopened. **Live-reconfirmed:** `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-internal.sql` section 16 drives one ticket to `closed` and one to `cancelled` (via the real `new→open→resolved→closed` and `new→cancelled` transition graphs) and proves `app.add_ticket_watcher`/`app.reply_to_ticket` reject BOTH with `invalid_transition`/`ticket_closed`/`ticket_cancelled`, while `app.remove_ticket_watcher` still succeeds on BOTH (removing a watcher added before closure); `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-linked-records.sql` section 17 proves the identical matrix for `app.link_ticket_record` (rejected on both) and `app.unlink_ticket_record` (permitted on both, removing a link added before closure/cancellation) — this section's own live reproduction (`app.link_ticket_record` on a closed ticket returning a real link row, `id=ea0fdd6c-...`) now raises `invalid_transition: cannot link a record to a closed ticket` instead. **Status `RESOLVED`** at `HRT-295` — was Medium severity while open (a closed/cancelled ticket's typed-record linkage set and watcher roster could keep drifting silently after closure).

### ISS-2026-110 — HRIS/Ticketing Integrated Verification (HRT-294): `app.list_ticket_links` returns the raw internal staff `created_by` identity to a customer caller, breaking this same workstream's own established "genericize staff identity for customer reads" convention (OPEN, Low)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the `CG-S12-HRT-022` (Prompt 294) integrated-verification checkpoint's own Critical Flow 3 lens. Independently re-confirmed by the synthesis session via direct code read of the live function body and the shared `TicketLinkRowSchema`/customer-panel wiring (not accepted from the lens report alone).

**Code:** `app.list_ticket_links` (`supabase/migrations/20260731170000_create_ticket_linked_records.sql:741-778`) `returns table (..., created_by text, ...)`, populated from `l.created_by` with no customer-vs-staff branching anywhere in the function body — confirmed by direct read of the full function, no such branch exists. There is no `list_customer_ticket_links` variant anywhere in the repository (per HRT-292's own decisions, the customer narrowing applies only to which entity TYPES may be searched/linked, never to which COLUMNS a read returns).
