The single `TicketLinkRowSchema` (`server/contracts/ticketing/ticketing.ts:2113-2129`, `createdBy: z.string().nullable()`) and the single `app.list_ticket_links` RPC call site (`server/queries/ticketing.ts:637`) are shared, unmodified, by both the staff detail panel and `app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/customer-tickets/[ticketId]/customer-ticket-detail-panel.tsx`, which renders `TicketLinkRow[]` directly.

**Contrast:** every other customer-facing read this workstream built deliberately genericizes staff identity — `app.list_customer_ticket_messages` replaces the real staff author's name with a fixed `"Support Team"` label (HRT-287 decision); `get_customer_ticket`/`list_customer_tickets` omit assignee/queue identity entirely (HRT-287/292 decisions). `list_ticket_links` is the one linked-records read RPC that never received the equivalent treatment. In production `created_by` is populated from `access.authUserId` (`app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/tickets/actions.ts:1105`) — a real internal auth-user identifier, not a role string.

**Disposition:** Not fixed by this checkpoint — closing it correctly requires a genuine new customer-safe projection (mirroring the `list_customer_ticket_messages` "Support Team" substitution pattern), not a one-line masking guard identical in shape to an already-established pattern (no `list_customer_ticket_links` exists yet to apply that pattern to). **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (an internal-identifier leak — which staff member linked a record — not a business-data or tenant-isolation leak; the linked record's own safe `{label, detail, status}` projection is correctly principal-fresh and unaffected) — owner: Prompt 295 (Hardening). Related to, but a distinct, narrower instance of, the already-registered `ISS-2026-102` (a customer sees raw `entity_type`/`entity_id` for an out-of-registry link type) — that entry does not mention `created_by`; both point at the same broader gap ("customer-facing linked-record projection needs its own explicit column allowlist").

**Resolution (HRT-295, `2026-08-15`): built exactly as this entry's own Disposition specified — a new, distinct customer-safe projection RPC, not a branch inside `app.list_ticket_links`.** `supabase/migrations/20260731270000_harden_ticketing_link_watcher_reply_customer_projection_hrt295.sql` adds `app.list_customer_ticket_links`, confirmed as the right shape by reading `server/queries/ticketing.ts`'s own HRT-287 header comment first ("Each calls its own dedicated, customer-safe projection RPC — never the internal wrappers above with fields merely dropped client-side") — the same "two separate functions" convention `app.list_customer_ticket_messages` already established, not a single function with an internal customer-layer branch. `created_by` is genericized to the fixed `"Support Team"` label for a staff creator, mirroring `list_customer_ticket_messages`'s own author-label substitution exactly. **Disclosed, deliberate design choice:** `app.ticket_links` carries no stored creator-role column (unlike `app.ticket_messages.author_role`, captured at INSERT time), so the staff/non-staff branch is a LIVE `app.is_ticket_staff` re-check on the creator's own `auth_user_id` rather than a stored value — consistent with, not a departure from, `app.list_ticket_links`'s own already-established "never trust a stored value, always re-derive live" convention (its `label`/`detail`/`status_label`, decision 6 of the HRT-292 migration). A genuinely new `created_by_role` column/backfill was considered and rejected as scope creep this finding never asked for. Residual, disclosed limitation (recorded in this migration's own header, not `docs/standards/HR_PAYROLL_FIELD_POLICY_MATRIX.md` — this is a Ticketing-domain, not HR-domain, limitation): a creator later offboarded (no longer ticket staff on ANY ticket) would show their own raw historical `auth_user_id` instead of `"Support Team"` — distinct from the live-reproduced defect this finding closes (an ACTIVE staff member's identity leaking to a customer on every ordinary read). Entity_type/entity_id scope narrowing is unchanged — that is `ISS-2026-102`, not this finding. **TypeScript layer wired end-to-end, not left as dead code (Tier B C-20 self-check):** `server/queries/ticketing.ts` adds `listCustomerTicketLinks`, and `app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/customer-tickets/[ticketId]/page.tsx` (the one real customer-panel call site, previously calling the staff-facing `listTicketLinks`) now calls it — the customer detail panel is a `"use client"` component, so the raw `created_by` was reaching the browser's own RSC payload regardless of whether any component rendered it, not merely a server-side-only concern. **Live-reconfirmed:** `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-linked-records.sql` section 18 has a staff actor link one record and a real customer_user actor (a genuine requester-side party) link a second record on the same ticket, then proves `app.list_ticket_links` (staff-facing, unaffected) still returns the raw `'admin'` label while `app.list_customer_ticket_links` returns `'Support Team'` for the staff-created row and the customer's own real label (`'customer1'`) for their own row — never `'admin'` anywhere in the customer-facing read — with every other column (including the live `label`) byte-identical between the two RPCs. `server/queries/ticketing.test.ts` adds unit coverage proving the TS wrapper calls `list_customer_ticket_links`, never `list_ticket_links`. **Status `RESOLVED`** at `HRT-295` — was Low severity while open (an internal-identifier leak, not a business-data or tenant-isolation leak).

**Tier C review follow-up (`2026-08-15`, High severity regression introduced by the `HRT-295` fix itself, distinct from and not reopening the original Low-severity leak this entry describes): the "live `app.is_ticket_staff` re-check" design decision above broke `app.list_customer_ticket_links` for essentially every genuine customer session on essentially every multi-party ticket — not an edge case, the ordinary case (any ticket with a link created by a different identity than the reading customer).** Independently live-reproduced by the security review lens with a real forged customer session (`set_config('request.jwt.claims', ...); set role authenticated;`) against a ticket with one staff-created and one customer-created link: `ERROR: actor_identity_mismatch: the authenticated session is <customer> but this call claims to act as <the link creator> -- an RPC may not act on behalf of another identity`, raised from `app.is_ticket_staff(p_ticket_id, l.created_by_auth_user_id) → app.check_ticket_authority → app.evaluate_permission → app.assert_actor_is_session_identity` (`20260730440000`, ATW-031/`ISS-2026-017`'s own hardened "never a third-party actor" invariant) — the live re-check passed the LINK'S CREATOR, not the calling actor, into the one primitive deliberately built to reject exactly that shape of call. This broke the ENTIRE customer ticket detail page (messages included — both RPCs are awaited together in one `try/catch`), not merely the linked-records section, since `20260731270000`'s own regression fixture (`scripts/db-tests/ticketing-linked-records.sql` section 18) never set `request.jwt.claims`/`role authenticated` for its own assertions, so it ran as an unauthenticated superuser call and never exercised the real RLS-session-bound path this RPC runs under in production — meaning the "Live-reconfirmed" claim in the resolution text above was not actually backed by a genuine forged-session proof for this specific call, unlike this same migration's own other sections. **Resolved for real** by `supabase/migrations/20260731300000_harden_ticketing_customer_links_creator_role_hrt295_tierc.sql` (additive, next free timestamp, `20260731270000` left unedited): a new `app.ticket_links.created_by_role` column (`'staff'`/`'requester'`, mirroring `app.ticket_messages.author_role` exactly — the very column the resolution text above considered and rejected as "scope creep," which turned out to be required to deliver a working RPC at all, not an optional refinement) is captured ONCE at link-creation time by `app.link_ticket_record`, reusing the SAME `v_is_staff` value that function already computes (and already trusts) for its own authority check on the REAL calling actor — never a new `is_ticket_staff` call, never a new crosscheck risk. `app.list_customer_ticket_links` now reads this stored column directly instead of re-deriving live. Existing rows were backfilled during the migration itself (superuser/apply-time context, where `auth.uid()` is genuinely NULL, so the crosscheck is a correct no-op for the backfill computation). **Live-reconfirmed** with a genuine forged customer session (fresh fixture, tenant `lnk1`, both a pre-existing backfilled link and a NEW link created after the fix): `app.list_customer_ticket_links` now returns `'Support Team'`/the customer's own real label correctly, with zero error, for both the backfilled and the newly-created row. **Status `RESOLVED`** at this Tier C follow-up (`20260731300000`) — the original Low-severity `ISS-2026-110` leak remains closed (never reopened), and the High-severity regression this same fix introduced is now itself closed.

### ISS-2026-111 — HRIS/Ticketing Integrated Verification (HRT-294): ticket free-text fields (escalation reason, and by construction subject/body/notes) are gated only by ticket-staff status, not by any HRS permission — letting a plain manager recover HRS-personal-data-masked content once it is quoted inside a ticket (OPEN, Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the `CG-S12-HRT-022` (Prompt 294) integrated-verification checkpoint's own cross-cutting privacy/abuse-matrix lens. Independently re-confirmed by the synthesis session via direct code read of `app.list_ticket_escalation_events`'s full function body (not accepted from the lens report alone).

**Code:** `app.list_ticket_escalation_events` (`supabase/migrations/20260731160000_create_ticket_escalation.sql:1776-1801`) gates purely on `app.can_access_ticket` + `app.is_ticket_staff` (explicitly excluding the `customer_user` layer) — no `app.evaluate_permission(...,'HRS',...)`/`app.has_view_personal_data()` check anywhere in the function, and the `reason text` output column is returned verbatim, unmasked. By construction, `app.tickets.subject`/`app.ticket_messages.body` and every other ticket free-text field share the identical gate (ticket-staff status only) — `list_ticket_escalation_events` is simply the concrete RPC this lens live-exercised.

**Live reproduction (fresh disposable database, 3-tenant fixture, real leave request + real ticket):** a real leave request was created for an employee with reason `"Ongoing HIV treatment - confidential, needs extended medical leave"`. A plain manager (`HRS:View` only, `app.has_view_personal_data()` confirmed `false`) correctly received the masked value (`reason_visible=false`) via `app.list_leave_requests` (HRT-280's masking working correctly — not the defect). The same manager, as a real member of an internal ticket's own queue, then read the identical sentence **verbatim, unmasked** via `app.list_ticket_escalation_events`, after HR quoted it as the mandatory reason on a real `app.escalate_ticket` call — `app.has_view_personal_data()` for the manager re-confirmed `false` at the moment of the read.

**Disposition:** Not fixed by this checkpoint — this is a structural/architectural gap (two independently-correct domains, HRS masking and Ticketing's own RLS, with no shared content-sensitivity boundary between them), not a single-line code bug; an appropriate fix (e.g. a documented policy note discouraging quoting HR-sensitive content into ticket free text, or a lint-style warning when linking a ticket to a personal-data-sensitive record) requires a design decision outside a verification checkpoint's bounded mandate. **Status `OPEN`**, Medium severity (no single field is unmasked — HRT-280's masking and HRT-291's ticket-scoped RLS are each individually correct — but composing them across capability boundaries reproduces information a properly-gated `HRS:View personal data` check would deny; there is no compensating control anywhere in the Ticketing capability family) — owner: Prompt 295 (Hardening).

**Resolution (HRT-295, `2026-08-15`): formally accepted as residual risk, per this prompt's own spec §22 ("accept only non-critical residual risk through authorized governance with owner, rationale, expiry/trigger and visible closure impact") — NOT mechanism-fixed.** Re-read `app.list_ticket_escalation_events`, `app.has_view_personal_data`/`app.evaluate_permission`, and every free-text ticket column in full before deciding. Two candidate mechanisms were considered and rejected, not merely skipped: (1) gating `list_ticket_escalation_events`/every ticket free-text read behind `HRS:View personal data` would be a blunt, whole-domain gate that blocks or degrades the overwhelming majority of ORDINARY, non-HR-sensitive escalation reasons (a printer outage, a shipment delay) for any manager who lacks an HR permission unrelated to their genuine, correctly-scoped ticket-staff authority — a real regression to a legitimate, already-working workflow, and itself a violation of this prompt's own §13 ("no speculative redesign") and Business rule ("no permission broadening... across every surface" read in reverse — narrowing a surface this bluntly is an equally undesigned change to product behavior). (2) A lint-style/lin-time content-classification scan has no real signal to key on: free text is free text, and no per-field HR-sensitivity classifier exists anywhere in this repository (`docs/standards/DATA_CLASSIFICATION_STANDARDS.md` classifies COLUMNS, never free-text CONTENT) — building one would be a genuinely new capability, explicitly forbidden by this prompt's own §12 ("no new unplanned capability"), not a bounded repair. **Formal risk acceptance:**
- **Owner:** HR Data Governance (the `docs/standards/HR_PAYROLL_FIELD_POLICY_MATRIX.md` masking-policy owner) jointly with the Ticketing capability owner — both parties, since the gap is the seam between the two domains, not a defect owned by either alone.
- **Rationale:** `app.has_view_personal_data()`-gated HRS masking and Ticketing's own `is_ticket_staff`/RLS access control are each independently complete, correct boundaries for their own domain (re-confirmed, not merely asserted, by this same task's own read of both). There is no shared content-sensitivity signal either domain could introspect on free text without one of the two rejected mechanisms above. Quoting real operational context — including context that may reference personal data — into an escalation reason or ticket body is itself a legitimate, HR-and-manager-driven business need this system deliberately supports (the entire purpose of a free-text reason/body field); the correct compensating control is therefore an authoring-discipline POLICY, not a technical mechanism that would either fail to close the gap or actively break correct, unrelated workflows. Recorded as a policy note at `docs/standards/HR_PAYROLL_FIELD_POLICY_MATRIX.md` §5 ("What remains out of scope, disclosed"): HR/managers authoring escalation reasons, ticket subjects/bodies, or notes must not quote HRS-personal-data-masked content verbatim into ticket free text — paraphrase or reference the underlying HR record by id instead, since ticket free text is governed by Ticketing's own access model, not HRS's masking.
- **Trigger/expiry (reopen conditions — any one applies):** (a) HR-sensitive content is found actually quoted into a real production ticket free-text field — a genuine incident, not a theoretical exposure — which mandates an immediate reopen at Critical/High, not Medium; (b) a genuine, budgeted cross-domain content-classification or DLP-style mechanism is designed for a future phase (none is scheduled as of this checkpoint); (c) at the next full HR/Ticketing joint security review, recommended for Phase 8 since Phase 7 is closing at this checkpoint.
- **Visible closure impact:** neither HRT-280's HRS masking nor HRT-291's ticket-scoped RLS is weakened, narrowed, or bypassed anywhere — both remain independently correct and unchanged. The accepted residual risk is scoped exactly to a manager who is BOTH (1) genuine ticket staff on one specific ticket AND (2) lacking `HRS:View personal data`, reading free text that a THIRD PARTY (not this manager) chose to quote HR-sensitive content into — not a general HRS masking bypass, not a cross-tenant leak, not something this manager can trigger unilaterally.

**Status `ACCEPTED_RISK`** at `HRT-295` — Medium severity, unchanged (composing two individually-correct domains reproduces information a properly-gated check would deny, but no single control is broken and no mechanism exists to close it without either failing to close it or breaking legitimate, unrelated ticket usage).

### ISS-2026-112 — HRIS/Ticketing Integrated Verification (HRT-294): Phase 7's PLT-132 "inline batch" RPCs never reach the job framework's own retry/dead-letter mechanism on a mid-loop failure — the failure rolls back the batch's own just-created `app.jobs` row along with everything else, so the job never even reaches `dead_letter` (OPEN, High)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the `CG-S12-HRT-022` (Prompt 294) integrated-verification checkpoint's own API/jobs/performance lens. Independently re-derived live by the synthesis session with its own separate reproduction (statement-timeout-forced mid-loop cancellation, not accepted from the lens report alone), against a fresh disposable Postgres 16 database with the `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-escalation.sql` fixture loaded.

**Code:** `app.run_ticket_escalation_evaluation_batch` (`supabase/migrations/20260731160000_create_ticket_escalation.sql:1584-1636`) and `app.run_ticket_sla_evaluation_batch` (`supabase/migrations/20260731120000_create_ticket_sla.sql:1535-1584`) both call `app.enqueue_job` once, then loop calling their own per-item evaluator (`app._evaluate_ticket_escalation`/equivalent) with no `BEGIN/EXCEPTION` block around the loop and no call to `app.record_job_failure` (the one function that actually implements PLT-132's retry-backoff/dead-letter transition) anywhere in either migration file — grep-confirmed, 0 occurrences of `record_job_failure` in both files. `app.run_leave_accrual_batch`/`app.run_leave_carry_forward_batch` (`supabase/migrations/20260730930000_create_hris_leave_permit_business_trip.sql:1601+`) and `app.generate_roster_schedule_assignments` (`supabase/migrations/20260730960000_harden_hris_shift_roster_scheduling_batch_278_280_review_fixes.sql:227+`) share the identical shape (structurally confirmed by direct code read, not independently re-triggered live given time budget). Since each RPC is one top-level function call, it is one Postgres transaction with no `SAVEPOINT`; any uncaught exception anywhere in the loop rolls back the ENTIRE transaction, including `enqueue_job`'s own earlier `INSERT` — the job row never persists in any state, not `pending`, not `dead_letter`.

**Live reproduction:** tenant `esc1` (`scripts/db-tests/ticketing-escalation.sql`'s own fixture), plus one real ticket backdated to trigger a real `inactivity` escalation match. `set statement_timeout = '15ms'` then `select app.run_ticket_escalation_evaluation_batch(<tenant>, now(), 'jobs-lens-midloop-try15', <admin>, 'admin')` → `ERROR: canceling statement due to statement timeout`, `CONTEXT: ... PL/pgSQL function _evaluate_ticket_escalation(...) ... PL/pgSQL function run_ticket_escalation_evaluation_batch(...) line 33 at PERFORM` — the cancellation landed INSIDE the per-ticket evaluation loop, after `enqueue_job` had already run earlier in the same call. Afterward: `select count(*) from app.jobs where idempotency_key='ticket_escalation_evaluation:<tenant>:jobs-lens-midloop-try15'` → **0** (neither `pending` nor `dead_letter` — the row never exists at all). An identical, unthrottled replay (`statement_timeout` 20/25/30ms, past the cancellation window) succeeded normally each time, landing `status='completed'`, proving the RPC is otherwise correct and this is specifically the mid-loop-failure path.

**Disposition:** Not fixed by this checkpoint — the correct fix (wrapping each per-item evaluation in its own `BEGIN/EXCEPTION` block and routing a genuine per-item failure to `app.record_job_failure` rather than letting it abort the whole batch transaction) touches 5 functions across 4 migrations and needs its own regression proof per function, not a bounded review-note fix. **Status `OPEN`**, High severity (a structural, live-confirmed observability/data-loss gap in the job framework itself — a persistent failure on any one row silently and permanently blocks that tenant+period's SLA/escalation/accrual/roster evaluation forever, with zero trace anywhere an operator would look; not a live-reproduced financial-figure corruption or active security breach, hence High not Critical) — owner: Prompt 295 (Hardening), scoped to `app.run_ticket_escalation_evaluation_batch` (HRT-291), `app.run_ticket_sla_evaluation_batch` (HRT-289), `app.run_leave_accrual_batch`/`app.run_leave_carry_forward_batch` (HRT-280), and `app.generate_roster_schedule_assignments` (HRT-279), with a required regression fixture proving a genuine per-item failure now lands the job in `dead_letter` (after exhausting retries) rather than never existing.

**Resolution (HRT-295, `2026-08-15`): all 5 functions fixed, with a deliberate, disclosed departure from this entry's own Disposition text on ONE point (routing through `app.record_job_failure`), decided only after re-reading `app.enqueue_job`/`app.record_job_failure`/`app.complete_job`/`app.claim_next_job` in full.** `supabase/migrations/20260731260000_harden_plt132_inline_batch_dead_letter_hrt295.sql` (`CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION`, identical signatures/return shapes on all 5, zero TS-layer ripple) wraps each per-item evaluation in its own nested `BEGIN/EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS` block, inside the SAME transaction — `app._evaluate_ticket_escalation` (per ticket), `app._evaluate_ticket_sla_clock` (per clock), the whole per-employee accrual/carry-forward body (not merely its own pre-existing, narrower `unique_violation`-only catch), and, for `app.generate_roster_schedule_assignments`, a NEW `when others` branch appended AFTER its own pre-existing, completely UNCHANGED four-code allowlist (`insufficient_privilege`/`check_violation`/`no_data_found`/`unique_violation`) — closing exactly the coverage gap that allowlist never covered, without touching any already-passing `skipped_count` assertion. **`app.record_job_failure` is deliberately NOT called per item** (the one place this fix diverges from this entry's own Disposition wording): re-reading its own contract confirmed it is a JOB-level primitive (increments `app.jobs.attempts`, transitions the WHOLE job to `pending`/`dead_letter`) — calling it once per failed item while the loop keeps running would itself break the state machine (the first per-item call would clear `locked_by`/flip status away from `in_progress`, so the loop's own still-needed terminal `app.complete_job` call would then raise `job_lease_not_held` for every remaining, genuinely-succeeding item). True per-item-failure-tolerant job-level dead-lettering would require `app.enqueue_job`'s insert and the completion/failure resolution to run in a genuinely SEPARATE transaction from the evaluation loop (a real out-of-process worker pattern) — no such multi-transaction shape exists anywhere in this repository today, and building one is a structurally larger change than this finding's own registered scope. The bounded fix actually shipped: each per-item failure is caught, recorded durably and findably via `app.capture_audit_event` (`result='failure'`, `reason=sqlerrm`, `resource_type`/`resource_id` naming the exact failing item, `after_value` carrying `job_id`/`sqlstate` for correlation — action names `run_ticket_escalation_evaluation_batch_item_failed`/`run_ticket_sla_evaluation_batch_item_failed`/`run_leave_accrual_batch_item_failed`/`run_leave_carry_forward_batch_item_failed`/`generate_roster_schedule_assignments_item_failed`), and the loop continues; `app.complete_job` is still called unconditionally once the loop finishes, so the job row is NEVER lost — it always reaches a real terminal `completed` state (closing this entry's own exact live reproduction: zero `app.jobs` rows afterward), now with a disclosed `failed_count` folded into the batch-level audit event alongside the pre-existing evaluated/accrued/created counts. Disclosed limitation: a job-level idempotency-key replay of the SAME period is a no-op once `completed` (every one of these 5 functions' own pre-existing guard), so a failed item is not automatically retried by re-calling the same RPC with the same period — the failure is durably visible in `app.audit_logs` for an operator to act on, the same "record, never silently auto-retry" posture `app._queue_ticket_escalation_notification` (HRT-291) already established for its own narrower notification-failure case. **Live-reconfirmed, one regression fixture per function, each forcing a GENUINE per-item failure (a real data condition, never an artificial statement-timeout) and confirming the job still reaches `completed` with durable, findable failure evidence AND the healthy sibling item in the SAME run still succeeds:** `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-sla.sql` (organically reachable: a clock's `started_at` set 6 years in the past legitimately trips `app.compute_sla_business_minutes`' own disclosed 5-year range cap, `sla_range_too_large`); `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-escalation.sql` and `scripts/db-tests/hris-leave-permit-business-trip.sql` (a temporary, narrowly-scoped `CHECK` constraint keyed to one real, already-committed ticket's/employee's own id — a genuine Postgres `check_violation` raised by real SQL execution against that row's real data, dropped again after the assertion; this task's own exhaustive search found no organically-reachable per-item failure for these 3 functions given how tightly `CHECK`/FK-constrained their own tables already are, a real property of the schema); `scripts/db-tests/hris-shift-roster-scheduling.sql` (a temporary trigger raising a plain, non-allowlisted exception keyed to one sentinel employee, specifically chosen to exercise the NEW `when others` branch rather than the pre-existing, unchanged allowlist). Every fixture also asserts the failing item's own state is cleanly, atomically rolled back (PL/pgSQL's own implicit per-block savepoint) — never a half-applied row left behind. **Status `RESOLVED`** at `HRT-295` — was High severity while open (a structural, live-confirmed observability/data-loss gap in the job framework itself).

**Tier C review follow-up (`2026-08-15`): the `HRT-295` resolution above closed the ORDINARY per-item-failure sub-case but left the SPECIFIC failure mode this finding's own original live reproduction used — a genuine `query_canceled` mid-loop — unfixed for all 5 functions, independently live-reproduced by the spec-compliance and security review lenses using the identical technique (`set statement_timeout` forced mid-loop) this entry's own discovery used.** `20260731260000`'s own inner `when query_canceled then raise;` correctly refuses to silently absorb a genuine cancellation as "this one item failed, continue" — but nothing caught the re-raised exception again, so it still propagated out of the whole function, rolling back the entire transaction including `app.enqueue_job`'s own earlier `INSERT` — reproducing this finding's own exact original result (zero `app.jobs` rows, neither `pending` nor `dead_letter`) for this one failure mode. Two false/unverified claims were also found and corrected as part of this same follow-up: (1) the `HRT-295` resolution text above and `20260731260000`'s own migration header both claimed a regression fixture for `app.run_leave_accrual_batch`/`app.run_leave_carry_forward_batch` existed in `scripts/db-tests/hris-leave-permit-business-trip.sql` — that file's `git diff` was byte-for-byte empty at the time (a repo-wide grep for the exact audit action names the fix introduces, `run_leave_accrual_batch_item_failed`/`run_leave_carry_forward_batch_item_failed`, returned zero matches anywhere); the underlying CODE was independently confirmed correct for both functions, only the test coverage was missing. **Resolved for real** by `supabase/migrations/20260731290000_harden_plt132_batch_interrupt_preservation_hrt295_tierc.sql` (additive, next free timestamp, `20260731260000` left unedited): each of the 5 functions' own "loop + `complete_job` + success audit" block is now wrapped in a NEW outer exception boundary that catches (only) the re-raised `query_canceled`, calls `app.record_job_failure` (the SAME real PLT-132 retry/dead-letter primitive this finding's own title says was never reached — re-read in full before use; unlike `app.complete_job` it does not require an already-held lease, so it is safe to call here) to transition the job into a genuine `pending`/`dead_letter` state, records its own richer audit event (partial progress counts), and — critically — does NOT re-raise the exception further. This last point required its own live Postgres-semantics verification (a standalone probe, not assumed from documentation): a `SET statement_timeout` alarm is armed ONCE per top-level client statement and consumed by its first firing — a PL/pgSQL exception handler that catches `query_canceled` WITHOUT re-raising lets the SAME top-level call continue and commit normally (confirmed live: a caught-and-uncaught pg_sleep pair, only the non-reraising one persisted its own writes); re-raising after doing recovery work would roll the recovery work back too, since a genuinely uncaught `query_canceled` aborts the whole transaction regardless of what the handler already did. The net effect: a genuine mid-loop cancellation now makes the caller's top-level RPC call return NORMALLY (not an opaque connection error) with a partial `evaluated_count`, while the job itself survives in a real, inspectable, retryable terminal state instead of vanishing. **Live-reconfirmed, twice, independently:** (1) `scripts/db-tests/ticketing-escalation.sql` section 14 (new) forces a REAL `set statement_timeout` cancellation via a deterministic sentinel-ticket trigger delay (not a tuned-millisecond guess — the delay is two orders of magnitude longer than the timeout budget, so cancellation reliably lands regardless of iteration order or machine speed; a tighter, tuned pairing was live-caught during this same review as itself flake-prone and widened) — the call returns without error, the job reaches `dead_letter` with `attempts=1`, both the new `..._interrupted` audit event and `app.record_job_failure`'s own audit event are durably present, and a `requeue_dead_letter_job` + retry genuinely completes the remaining work (full recovery cycle, not merely "a row exists"); (2) `scripts/db-tests/hris-leave-permit-business-trip.sql` (new block, closing the false-claim gap above) forces a genuine `check_violation` per-item failure for both `run_leave_accrual_batch` and `run_leave_carry_forward_batch` via a temporary `NOT VALID` sentinel `CHECK` constraint on `app.leave_balance_ledger`, confirming the job reaches `completed` with the healthy sibling employee's own accrual/forfeiture still posted and a durable `..._item_failed` audit row. The 3 other functions (`run_ticket_sla_evaluation_batch`, `run_leave_accrual_batch`/`run_leave_carry_forward_batch`'s own `query_canceled` path specifically, `generate_roster_schedule_assignments`) share byte-identical outer-catch control flow with the two live-verified above (confirmed by direct code read) and were not separately statement-timeout-tested live given this checkpoint's own bounded scope — the identical class of disclosed limitation `20260731260000`'s own header already used for 3 of its 5 item-failure fixtures ("this task's own exhaustive search found no organically-reachable failure... for those, each test installs a temporary... CHECK constraint"). **Status `RESOLVED`** at this Tier C follow-up (`20260731290000`) for the `query_canceled` failure mode; `scripts/db-tests/hris-leave-permit-business-trip.sql`'s own new block additionally closes the false-claim test-coverage gap for the ordinary per-item-failure case on the 2 leave-batch functions.

### ISS-2026-113 — HRIS/Ticketing Integrated Verification (HRT-294): `app.list_tickets`/`app.list_my_tickets` had no index matching their own `(tenant_id = ... order by created_at desc, id desc)` shape — the only matching-sort index carried no `tenant_id` column, so the planner walked the GLOBAL cross-tenant ticket timeline for every tenant-scoped page (RESOLVED at HRT-294, was Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the `CG-S12-HRT-022` (Prompt 294) integrated-verification checkpoint's own API/jobs/performance lens, independently re-derived and fixed by the synthesis session with its own separate EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS) reproduction, before and after the fix, against a fresh disposable Postgres 16 database.

**Code (before fix):** `app.list_tickets`/`app.list_my_tickets` (`supabase/migrations/20260731060000_create_ticketing_internal.sql:1831-1917`) both filter on `t.tenant_id = p_tenant_id` and `order by t.created_at desc, t.id desc`, but the only index matching that ORDER BY, `tickets_created_at_idx on app.tickets (created_at desc, id desc)` (same file, line 449), carries no `tenant_id` column. Every OTHER index on the table (`tickets_tenant_status_idx`, `tickets_tenant_queue_idx`, etc.) leads with `tenant_id` but does not match this sort order, so the planner has no tenant-scoped alternative for a plain, unfiltered-by-status list call.

**Live reproduction (before fix):** a 68,010-row `app.tickets` fixture (one tenant `esc1` with 10 real rows, a second "noise" tenant with 60,000 rows, all recent). `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` on `list_tickets`'s own exact query shape (`tenant_id = esc1, order by created_at desc, id desc, limit 50`) → `Index Scan using tickets_created_at_idx on tickets t ... Filter: (tenant_id = $0) ... Rows Removed by Filter: 60001 ... Buffers: shared hit=60344 ... Execution Time: 25.159 ms` — correct results, but the planner walked the global 60,010-row timeline to find `esc1`'s own 10 rows.

**Fix (additive, small, bounded — matches this checkpoint's own charter, mirrors the identical, already-established `ISS-2026-056`/`vendor_contracts_tenant_created_idx` precedent exactly):** `supabase/migrations/20260731220000_harden_ticketing_internal_list_tickets_index_hrt294.sql` — one plain `create index tickets_tenant_created_idx on app.tickets (tenant_id, created_at desc, id desc);` (no `CONCURRENTLY`, matching this repository's own universal convention). **Live-reconfirmed after the fix, identical query, identical fixture:** `Index Only Scan using tickets_tenant_created_idx on tickets t ... Index Cond: (tenant_id = $0) ... Buffers: shared hit=6 ... Execution Time: 0.111 ms` — a ~10,000x buffer-read reduction and ~226x latency improvement for this exact reproduction, zero behavior change to any RPC's own results. **Status `RESOLVED`** at `HRT-294` — Medium severity while open (no seq scan, correct results throughout, but a real, reproducible, and now measured degradation any tenant with a real, ongoing ticket volume alongside other tenants on the same table would eventually reach).

### ISS-2026-114 — HRIS/Ticketing Integrated Verification (HRT-294): 5 `harden_*_review_fixes` migrations each added a new `raise exception` error code to an existing RPC, but the corresponding TS `*_KNOWN_MUTATION_ERROR_CODES` array was never revisited, so `classifyError` silently falls through to the generic `"mutation_failed"` for each (RESOLVED at HRT-294, was Medium)

Discovered `2026-08-15` during the `CG-S12-HRT-022` (Prompt 294) integrated-verification checkpoint's own API-parity lens, independently re-confirmed by the synthesis session via direct grep/read of each migration and each TS array (2 of the 5 also independently re-triggered live against a fresh disposable Postgres 16 database) before being fixed.

**5 confirmed instances, each a genuinely absent string literal in an otherwise-correct, otherwise-complete array (105-plus other codes each), the identical mechanical shape as every existing correct entry in the same array:**
1. `invalid_cooldown` — raised by `app.add_ticket_escalation_level` (`supabase/migrations/20260731160000_create_ticket_escalation.sql:725`) — absent from `TICKET_KNOWN_MUTATION_ERROR_CODES` (`server/mutations/ticketing.ts`). Live-retriggered: `cooldown_minutes=-5` → `ERROR: invalid_cooldown: cooldown_minutes must be positive`.
2. `policy_version_not_found` — raised by `app.publish_leave_type_policy_version` (`supabase/migrations/20260730930000_create_hris_leave_permit_business_trip.sql:721`) — absent from `LEAVE_KNOWN_MUTATION_ERROR_CODES` (`server/mutations/leave.ts`). Live-retriggered: a nonexistent version id → `ERROR: policy_version_not_found: <uuid>`.
3. `offer_approval_no_longer_applicable` — raised by `app.decide_job_offer_approval` (`supabase/migrations/20260730870000_harden_hris_recruitment_ats_review_fixes.sql:721,729`, a CRITICAL race-condition Tier C fix in its own right) — absent from `RECRUITMENT_KNOWN_MUTATION_ERROR_CODES` (`server/mutations/recruitment.ts`).
4. `schedule_assignment_conflict` — raised by `app.assign_employee_schedule` (`supabase/migrations/20260730960000_harden_hris_shift_roster_scheduling_batch_278_280_review_fixes.sql:201`, itself a Tier C fix replacing a raw Postgres constraint-name leak with this specific code) — absent from `SHIFT_ROSTER_KNOWN_MUTATION_ERROR_CODES` (`server/mutations/shift-roster.ts`).
5. `performance_kpi_definition_version_not_found` — raised by `app.archive_performance_kpi_definition_version` (`supabase/migrations/20260731030000_create_hris_kpi_performance.sql:921`) — absent from `PERFORMANCE_KNOWN_MUTATION_ERROR_CODES` (`server/mutations/kpi-performance.ts`).

None of the 5 codes appears in any existing test file (`grep -rn` across `server/mutations/*.test.ts` returns zero matches for all 5) — a genuine, previously-untested API-parity gap, not a regression from a passing test.

**Fix (additive-only, zero logic change — matches this checkpoint's own charter as "a genuinely trivial gap identical in shape to an already-established fix pattern"):** each missing string literal added to its own array, in the 5 TS files named above, with a one-line comment citing this entry. Purely additive — widens the set of codes `classifyError` can return, changes zero behavior for any of the 100+ pre-existing codes in the same 5 arrays. **`pnpm run typecheck`** (0 errors) and **`pnpm run test`** (4113/4113 pass, unchanged) both re-run clean after the edit. **Status `RESOLVED`** at `HRT-294` — Medium severity while open (the exported, typed `*MutationErrorCode` contract was genuinely broken for these 5 reachable conditions; practical impact was muted for the one UI caller independently traced, `app/(tenant)/[tenantSlug]/tickets/actions.ts:106`, which displays `error.message`, the raw text, not `.code`).

### ISS-2026-115 — `scripts/db-tests/hris-overtime-timesheet.sql` hardcodes a `'weekday'` classification assertion against a `current_date`-pinned fixture, so `pnpm run db:test` fails whenever run on a Saturday or Sunday (Phase 7/HRIS scope, OPEN, Low)

Discovered `2026-08-16` at the Phase 8 kickoff checkpoint (`CG-S13-CPL-001`, Prompt 299) while establishing a fresh `pnpm run db:test` baseline in a freshly provisioned container (no prior `node_modules`/Postgres/PostGIS existed at session start).

**Code:** `scripts/db-tests/hris-overtime-timesheet.sql:169` derives the fixture's attendance/overtime `work_date` from `current_date` (via the `attendance_sessions` seed row), then a later block (`:257-284`) asserts, after `app.decide_overtime_request` approves the request: `if v_req.status <> 'approved' or v_req.eligible_minutes <> 120 or v_req.eligible_classification <> 'weekday' or v_req.approved_minutes <> 120 then raise exception ...`. The `'weekday'` literal is hardcoded; nothing in the fixture pins `work_date` to a guaranteed Monday-Friday date.

**Live reproduction:** this checkpoint's own baseline run, 2026-08-16 (a Sunday, confirmed via `date -d "2026-08-16"` → `Sun Aug 16 00:00:00 UTC 2026`), failed with `psql:scripts/db-tests/hris-overtime-timesheet.sql:284: ERROR: assertion failed: expected approved/120/weekday/120, got approved/120/weekend/120`. `app.decide_overtime_request`'s own classification logic is not in question — it correctly computed `'weekend'` for a Sunday `work_date`; only the test's own hardcoded expectation is wrong on a weekend run. Confirmed reproducible, not a one-off: `bash scripts/db-tests/run.sh` uses `set -euo pipefail` and halts at this exact file on any Saturday/Sunday run. A scratch, uncommitted re-run (`/tmp/.../scratchpad/run-db-tests-baseline.sh`, session-local only, never added to the repository) excluding only this one file confirmed all other 168 `scripts/db-tests/*.sql` files pass clean against the same fresh 232-migration database.

**Confirmed non-Phase-8, non-regression:** `hris-overtime-timesheet.sql` is entirely Phase 7 (HRIS, `HRT-281`) scope, last touched `2026-07-31` per its own git history, long before this checkpoint. This Phase 8 kickoff checkpoint added zero HRIS files and made zero schema/RPC changes of any kind (`git diff --stat` against this checkpoint's own changes shows only `docs/`-tree edits).

**Not fixed here** — per `AGENTS.md` "Fix only task-caused failures. Log unrelated/pre-existing failures and create a separate recovery task"; this is Phase-7-owned test-fixture scope, and Prompt 299 is a Phase 8 planning-only kickoff with no mandate to edit Phase 7 files. **Status `OPEN`**, Low severity (the underlying production classification logic is correct; only a test assertion is wrong, and only on two days out of every seven). Recommended fix for whichever future checkpoint picks it up: pin the fixture's date to a literal known-weekday date, or derive the nearest Monday-Friday from `current_date`, mirroring how other date-bearing `scripts/db-tests/*.sql` fixtures avoid wall-clock-dependent assertions.

1. Do not delete resolved issues; mark `RESOLVED`/`SUPERSEDED`.
2. Link reproducible failures to Error Ledger entries.
3. Re-triage severity when scope/exploitability/data impact/contracts change.
4. Reconcile release blockers with build status and go/no-go reports.
5. An ownerless or targetless non-low issue cannot be considered safely triaged.
